# 语料表格修复证据表 —— **带合并格的表**（A5 同族，上一批跳过的）

写于 2026-10-08。共 **362** 张 / **339** 篇。

**每张都逐格核对过**：md 的**每一行格子数与上游 HTML 逐行一一对应**，解析出来的格子必须与上游一致，对不上整张不动。

**展开是「重复」**：上游一格跨 N 行/列 → 写成 N 个相同格子。实测平均 25% 的格子是重复出来的；**占比 >45% 的整张挂账**。

**只改 `agent-markdown/`，上游 `raw/*.htm` 一个字节不动。**

For example, SEARCH is the minimum mode of the two modes SEARCH and AUTO. \|⏎\| command \| MAX \| Sets the 'maximum' mode of either the mode specified or the previous mode. Maximum refers to maximum time consumption.<br><br>The following order applies:<br><br>AUTO, SEARCH, FREEZE. For example, SEARCH is the maximum mode of the two modes SEARCH and FREEZE. \|⏎\| mode \| The mode can take one of the following values: \| The mode can take one of the following values: \|⏎\| mode \| AUTO \| Automatic level swing adjustment is done for all waveform sets. Any change of waveforms or levels triggers an automatic swing adjustment. \|⏎\| mode \| FREEZE \| No swing adjustment is performed. The actual swing adjustment settings are used. As soon as primary sets change, the mode is switched back to AUTO. \|⏎\| mode \|  \|  \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98487.htm`。` |
| 37 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.09_BDID__BDID__-__BoarD_ID.md` | `98489.htm` | #0 | 5×3 | 10 | 5 | `\| address \| Specifies how the value of the string parameter is to be processed. Possible values are: \| Specifies how the value of the string parameter is to be processed. Possible values are: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| address \| 0,2,3 \| Writes to or reads from the EEPROM with the specified logical address. These addresses represent the two address lines of the X24C04 EEPROM. The corresponding physical addresses are:<br><br>0 = 160 (= 2^7+ 2^5)<br><br>2 = 168 (= 2^7+ 2^5 + 2^3)<br><br>3 = 172 (= 2^7+ 2^5 + 2^3 + 2^2)<br><br>The logical address 0 is the address of the DUT board EEPROM. The logical address 1 = 164 (= 2^7+ 2^5 + 2^2) is reserved for the DUT interface EEPROM. \|⏎\| address \| -1 \| Interprets the string as a sequence of command characters and executes the commands. This can be used for debugging purposes.<br><br>The following characters are valid:<br><br>c: Set SCL to low.<br><br>C: Set SCL to high.<br><br>d: Set SDA to low.<br><br>D: Set SDA to high<br><br>?: Print state of SDA to report window.<br><br>In the query, BDID? returns the state of the SDA when called with -1. \|⏎\| address \| ALL \| Searches for an existing EEPROM in descending address order and returns the content of the first EEPROM that it finds (only for the query). \|⏎\| string \| The string that is written to or read from the EEPROM. (This reply is always returned in offline mode.) \|⏎\| valid_code \| OVFL \| Signifies error counter overflow. The hardware error counter has a range of 2^31. \|⏎\| no_of_errors \| This parameter returns the total number of results containing errors for one of the specified pins. | 54 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.15_CDCP__-__Cycle_Device_Cycle_Parameter.md` | `98499.htm` | #0 | 23×3 | 46 | 23 | `\| start_cycle \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle to be analyzed. The range of values for this parameter depends on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle to be analyzed. The range of values for this parameter depends on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| cycle \| In the query, the consecutive number of the displayed cycle. \| In the query, the consecutive number of the displayed cycle. \|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles to be analyzed. The range for this parameter is 0 to 1000. \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles to be analyzed. The range for this parameter is 0 to 1000. \|⏎\| strt_disp \| This parameter defines the offset of your range of interest from start_cycle. The actual display starts with the last device cycle that does not begin before start_cycle and does not end before start_cycle + strt_disp.<br><br>The purpose of this construction is to enable a correct device cycle reconstruction even if the first cycle you are interested in does not lie at a device cycle boundary (a device cycle can generate up to 16 cycles). \| This parameter defines the offset of your range of interest from start_cycle. The actual display starts with the last device cycle that does not begin before start_cycle and does not end before start_cycle + strt_disp.<br><br>The purpose of this construction is to enable a correct device cycle reconstruction even if the first cycle you are interested in does not lie at a device cycle boundary (a device cycle can generate up to 16 cycles). \|⏎\| disp_length \| Specifies the display length in cycles. This parameter determines the end of your range of interest. The actual display stops with the last device cycle that starts before start_cycle + strt_disp + disp_length.<br><br>If this parameter is larger than no_of_cycles, it is ignored. \| Specifies the display length in cycles. This parameter determines the end of your range of interest. The actual display stops with the last device cycle that starts before start_cycle + strt_disp + disp_length.<br><br>If this parameter is larger than no_of_cycles, it is ignored. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The pin name or names for which a device cycle data transfer is to be made. If more than one pin is required, the names must be separated with a comma. The @ character can be used to download data for all configured pins. \| The pin name or names for which a device cycle data transfer is to be made. If more than one pin is required, the names must be separated with a comma. The @ character can be used to download data for all configured pins. \|⏎\| dev_cyc_name \| A reply parameter, giving the name of the reconstructed device cycle. \| A reply parameter, giving the name of the reconstructed device cycle. \|⏎\| dev_cyc_len \| A reply parameter, giving the length of the reconstructed device cycle. For SmarTest 7.10 this is always 1. \| A reply parameter, giving the length of the reconstructed device cycle. For SmarTest 7.10 this is always 1. \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| Drive Data parameters. They contain values for both drive data and tri-state. The values the system returns are: \| Drive Data parameters. They contain values for both drive data and tri-state. The values the system returns are: \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F00 \| drive 0, tri-state off \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F10 \| drive 1, tri-state off \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F0Z \| drive 0, tri-state on \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F1Z \| drive 1, tri-state on \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| N \| Not used. Returned if no drive data parameter is set to a drive and tri-state value. \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| UC \| Unclear. The system may return this value to indicate that the interpretation of the device cycle for that parameter is unclear, that is, the physical waveforms are inconsistent for the device cycle. \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| Expected Data Parameters. The values the system returns are: \| Expected Data Parameters. The values the system returns are: \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| F0 \| compare to 0 \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| F1 \| compare to 1 \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FI \| compare to intermediate \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FX \| compare to mask \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FU \| compare to unstable \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| N \| Not used. Returned if no expected data parameter is set to a compare value. \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| UC \| Unclear. The system may return this value to indicate that the interpretation of the device cycle for that parameter is unclear, that is, the physical waveforms are inconsistent for the device cycle. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98499.htm`。` |
| 55 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.164_MSET__MSET__-__definition_of_Measurement_SET.md` | `98657.htm` | #0 | 53×3 | 101 | 58 | `\| id_dc \| The DC set identification number. The number is an integer in the range 1 to 256. A DC set consists of up to 10 measurement sets. For the query, you can specify all the defined DC sets with the ALL specifier. \| The DC set identification number. The number is an integer in the range 1 to 256. A DC set consists of up to 10 measurement sets. For the query, you can specify all the defined DC sets with the ALL specifier. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| id_mset \| The measurement set identification number. The number is an integer in the range 1 to 10. For the query, you can specify all the defined measurement sets with the ALL specifier. \| The measurement set identification number. The number is an integer in the range 1 to 10. For the query, you can specify all the defined measurement sets with the ALL specifier. \|⏎\| glob_act \| Global action in the measurement set. The allowed values are: \| Global action in the measurement set. The allowed values are: \|⏎\| glob_act \| LEN \| This action creates a default measurement set of the length specified with par1. par2 must be omitted. LEN must be executed before you can define any individual action.<br><br>This action can only be executed for all configured pins (@). \|⏎\| glob_act \| WAIT \| This action defines the waiting time for the sequencers after execution of the measurement set. The actual time is specified with the par2 parameter (in ms). par1 must be omitted.<br><br>Note that it takes 0.32/0.58/0.40 ms respectively for the PS1600 (DC-relays only)/PS9G/pin scale[1)](<#fntarg_1>) cards to open or close relays. Further, 4.0/2.8/1.0 ms respectively is needed for the PS1600/PS9G/pin scale cards as settling time of PMUs after closing relays. Therefore, for PS1600 for example, you must set at least 4.32 ms (for connecting relays) or 0.32 ms (for disconnecting relays) for the action for opening and closing relays.<br><br>This action can only be executed for all configured pins (@). \|⏎\| glob_act \| SWAIT \| This action defines the waiting time for the sequencers before execution of the measurement set. The actual time is specified with the par2 parameter (in ms). par1 must be omitted.<br><br>The default is 0, in which case the sequencers start the measurement immediately.<br><br>A possible reason for defining such a wait time is to ensure that the vectors needed for preparing the measurement have actually been shifted out of the data queue. This is ensured if<br><br>wait_time = 28 * user_period.<br><br>This action can only be executed for all configured pins (@).<br><br>Note This action is only supported for DC measurements that are started from within the sequencer program with the MEAS instruction. It cannot be used with the MEAS firmware command. \|⏎\| glob_act \| RSET \| This action undefines the specified measurement set. par1 and par2 must be omitted.<br><br>RSET can only be executed for all configured pins (@). \|⏎\| glob_act \| ACT \| This action activates the specified measurement set for execution. This is the default. \|⏎\| glob_act \| X \| This action inactivates the specified measurement set for execution. \|⏎\| act \| Individual action in the measurement set. The allowed values for the measurement setup are: \| Individual action in the measurement set. The allowed values for the measurement setup are: \|⏎\| act \| SEL \| Selects the working mode of the PMU. Set par2 to CURR for current measurements, or to VOLT for voltage measurements. \|⏎\| act \| IRNG \| Specifies the current range with par2 that is. You can specify a value in µA or one of the following codes:<br><br>IRA = +/- 10 µA range<br><br>IRB = +/- 100 µA range<br><br>IRC = +/- 1 mA range<br><br>IRD = +/- 40 mA range<br><br>If a value is specified, the smallest range that does not exceed the value will be set. \|⏎\| act \| CRNG \| Specifies the current range for the clamp with par2. The possible options are the same as for IRNG. The range for CRNG and IRNG must be identical. \|⏎\| act \| Note that SEL and IRNG must have been defined before any of the following setup actions can be executed. Also the follwing actions must be redefined after a change of SEL or IRNG. \| Note that SEL and IRNG must have been defined before any of the following setup actions can be executed. Also the follwing actions must be redefined after a change of SEL or IRNG. \|⏎\| act \| IFOR \| Specifies a force current with par2 in µA when the working mode (SEL action) is set to voltage measurement. \|⏎\| act \| UFOR \| Specifies a force voltage with par2 in mV when the working mode (SEL action) is set to current measurement. \|⏎\| act \| PMUL \| Specifies the lower pass limit of the DC test with par2 in µA for current measurements, or in mV for voltage measurements. This value is used for pass/fail judgment and is set to the PPMU hardware. \|⏎\| act \| PMUH \| Specifies the higher pass limit of the DC test with par2 in µA for current measurement, or in mV for voltage measurement. This value is used for pass/fail judgment and is set to the PPMU hardware. \|⏎\| act \| You can control the AC relay, the PPMU relay and the DC relay if the used hardware supports these relays. The allowed values for relay control are: \| You can control the AC relay, the PPMU relay and the DC relay if the used hardware supports these relays. The allowed values for relay control are: \|⏎\| act \| AC \| The AC relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The other two relays are always open. \|⏎\| act \| PPMU \| The PPMU relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The other two relays are always open. \|⏎\| act \| DC \| The DC relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The other two relays are always open. \|⏎\| act \| ACDC \| The AC and DC relays can be closed or opened simultaneously with ON/OFF for par2. The PPMU relay is always open. \|⏎\| act \| ACPM \| The AC and PPMU relays can be closed or opened simultaneously with ON/OFF for par2. The DC relay is always open. \|⏎\| act \| DCPM \| The PPMU and DC relays can be closed or opened simultaneously with ON/OFF for par2. The AC relay is always open. \|⏎\| act \| ALL \| All three relays can be closed or opened simultaneously with ON/OFF for par2. \|⏎\| act \| OAC \| The AC relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The state of the other two relays remains unchanged. \|⏎\| act \| OPM \| The PPMU relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The state of the other two relays remains unchanged. \|⏎\| act \| ODC \| The DC relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The state of the other two relays remains unchanged. \|⏎\| act \| The following actions set up the voltage clamp for Pin Scale systems: \| The following actions set up the voltage clamp for Pin Scale systems: \|⏎\| act \| CLPL \| Specifies the clamp low voltage with par2 in mV. \|⏎\| act \| CLPH \| Specifies the clamp high voltage with par2 in mV.<br><br>This clamp high voltage must be greater than the clamp low voltage. The setting range is same as for CLPL. \|⏎\| act \| CLMP \| Enables or disables the PPMU clamp with par2. ON enables the clamps, OFF disables them.<br><br>The clamp operates for both current force (IVOR action) and voltage force (UFOR action).<br><br>Before setting this parameter to ON, both the clamp low and high voltage (current) should be defined by CLPL (ICPL) and CLPH (ICPH) to prevent that unknown clamp voltages will be set momentarily. \|⏎\| act \| ICPL \| Specifies the clamp low current with par2 in µA. \|⏎\| act \| ICPH \| Specifies the clamp high current with par2 in µA. \|⏎\| act \| The following actions define the measurement mode: \| The following actions define the measurement mode: \|⏎\| act \| PMUM \| This action provides a PASS/FAIL result. This depends on how the measured value is compared with the limits. The compare mode can be set as par2 with the following specifiers:<br><br>I (for Intermediate): PASS is returned if the measured value lies between the lower and the higher limit. This is the default.<br><br>H (for High): PASS is returned if the measured value is not smaller than the higher limit.<br><br>L (for Low): PASS is returned if the measured value is not greater than the lower limit.<br><br>X: Mask (Always returns PASS).Note For EXA Scale digital cards, to push the register setting into the hardware, the SYNC action must be executed immediately after this action. \|⏎\| act \| VMUM \| This action provides a measurement value, but also a PASS/FAIL result. For the latter a compare mode must be specified with par2. The possible options are same as for PMUM.<br><br>Note This action is only supported for DC measurements that are started within the sequencer program with the [MEAS](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.16.02.06.10.02_Sequencer_instructions.md#_99880__row_MEAS>) instruction. It cannot be used with the MEAS firmware command. Note For EXA Scale digital cards, to push the register setting into the hardware, the SYNC action must be executed immediately after this action. \|⏎\| act \| DVM1 \| This action provides the first value for a delta measurement. It also returns a PASS/FAIL result, for which a compare mode must be specified with par2. The possible options are same as for PMUM.<br><br>Note This action is only supported for DC measurements that are started within the sequencer program with the [MEAS](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.16.02.06.10.02_Sequencer_instructions.md#_99880__row_MEAS>) instruction. It cannot be used with the MEAS firmware command. Note For EXA Scale digital cards, to push the register setting into the hardware, the SYNC action must be executed immediately after this action. \|⏎\| act \| DVM2 \| This action<br><br>- provides the second value for a delta measurement,<br>- calculates the difference between this value and the one provided by DVM1,<br>- creates a PASS/FAIL result from this difference by comparing it with the limits (PMUL and PMUH).<br><br>For the last step, a compare mode must be specified with par2. The possible options are same as for PMUM.<br><br>Note This action is only supported for DC measurements that are started within the sequencer program with the [MEAS](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.16.02.06.10.02_Sequencer_instructions.md#_99880__row_MEAS>) instruction. It cannot be used with the MEAS firmware command. Note For EXA Scale digital cards, to push the register setting into the hardware, the SYNC action must be executed immediately after this action. \|⏎\| act \| MUX_PPMU_BADC<br><br>(Available on PS1600) \| Supports using the board ADC to measure voltage/current on pins during a pattern running in a functional test.<br><br>This action controls the multilplexer before a board ADC to select the signal coming from the pin in {pinlist} via its PPMU and inputs it to the board ADC for value measurement.<br><br>To support this action:<br><br>- The values for {part2} are "ON" and "OFF".<br>- {pinlist} can contain only one pin or pins of different logical boards.<br><br>Putting a number of pins of the same logical board in the {pinlist} will result in resource confliction errors, because they share the same one board ADC and this board ADC can measure only one of them each time. \|⏎\| act \| MUX_VM_HIZ<br><br>(Available on PS1600) \| Supports using the board ADC to measure high impedance or high voltage on pins during a pattern running in a functional test.<br><br>This action controls the multiplexer before a board ADC to select the voltage signal coming from the pin in {pinlist} that has been connected to a DC Line and inputs it to the board ADC for value measurement.<br><br>To support this action:<br><br>- The values for {part2} are "ON" and "OFF".<br>- {pinlist} can contain only one pin or pins of different logical boards.<br><br>Putting pins of the same logical board in the {pinlist} will result in resource confliction errors, because they share the same one board ADC and this board ADC can measure only one of them each time. \|⏎\| act \| There are two actions which are not related to any af the DC measurement tasks: \| There are two actions which are not related to any af the DC measurement tasks: \|⏎\| act \| NOP \| This action does nothing at all. \|⏎\| act \| ACTI \| This action pushes the register setting into the hardware. It must be executed at the end of every measurement set, except when PMUM, VMUM, DVM1, or DVM2 is the only action within the set.<br><br>For the EXA Scale digital card, it must be followed by the SYNC action. \|⏎\| act \| SYNC \| This action pushes the register setting into the hardware, ensuring proper timing for the EXA Scale digital cards (the action does nothing for other digital cards). It must be executed right after ACTI, PMUM, VMUM, DVM1, or DVM2. \|⏎\| par1 \| First parameter for glob_act. The possible values depend on the act parameter. \| First parameter for glob_act. The possible values depend on the act parameter. \|⏎\| id_act \| The index of an individual action within the measurement set. \| The index of an individual action within the measurement set. \|⏎\| par2 \| Second parameter for glob_act. The possible values depend on the act parameter. \| Second parameter for glob_act. The possible values depend on the act parameter. \|⏎\| numOfSamples \| The number of samples to be taken for one measurement value. Depending on the specified waveform size, the number of samples is constraint.<br><br>If only one measurement is taken, the number of samples can be an integer between 1 and 4096.<br><br>If a waveform (waveformSize > 1) is captured, then the number of samples must be one. \| The number of samples to be taken for one measurement value. Depending on the specified waveform size, the number of samples is constraint.<br><br>If only one measurement is taken, the number of samples can be an integer between 1 and 4096.<br><br>If a waveform (waveformSize > 1) is captured, then the number of samples must be one. \|⏎\| waveformSize \| The number of measurements to be taken in the waveform. It is an integer between 1 and 4096. \| The number of measurements to be taken in the waveform. It is an integer between 1 and 4096. \|⏎\| samplePeriod \| The sampling period for the waveform to measure, specified as float64 in [s]. The valid range is from 4 us to 409.6 us. The first sample has an additional trigger delay of 14.5us.<br><br>The sampling period resolution is 25 ns. \| The sampling period for the waveform to measure, specified as float64 in [s]. The valid range is from 4 us to 409.6 us. The first sample has an additional trigger delay of 14.5us.<br><br>The sampling period resolution is 25 ns. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Target pin or pins for act. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. pinlist can contain digital pins configured as O, I, IO, or DC pin types. \| Target pin or pins for act. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. pinlist can contain digital pins configured as O, I, IO, or DC pin types. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98657.htm`。` |
| 56 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.170_OUTS__-__OUTput_Search.md` | `98677.htm` | #0 | 16×3 | 32 | 16 | `\| vector_no_1 \| This parameter is an absolute vector number or a range specifier depending on whether DPTH, SNGL, or RNG has been selected. \| This parameter is an absolute vector number or a range specifier depending on whether DPTH, SNGL, or RNG has been selected. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| vector_no_2 \| This parameter is an absolute vector number. \| This parameter is an absolute vector number. \|⏎\| vector_type \| The vector_type is one of the following: \| The vector_type is one of the following: \|⏎\| vector_type \| DPTH \| The depth option. The vector table is searched from the current start label (defined by the label parameter or by the SQSL command) for the number of vectors specified in the vector_no_1 parameter. The vector_no_2 parameter must be omitted. \|⏎\| vector_type \| SNGL \| The single option. Only the vectors specified in the vector_no_1 and vector_no_2 parameters are searched. \|⏎\| vector_type \| RNG \| The range option. The range of vectors between (and including) vector_no_1 and vector_no_2 are searched. \|⏎\| level_type \| The level_type is one of the following: \| The level_type is one of the following: \|⏎\| level_type \| HIGH \| If HIGH is selected, the system looks for a high level (logic 1 state). \|⏎\| level_type \| LOW \| If LOW is selected, the system looks for a low level (logic 0 state). \|⏎\| level_type \| BOTH \| If BOTH is selected, the system looks for both levels. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The pinlist can contain O, or IO pins. If all pins configured as outputs are to be searched, use the @ character. If more than one pin is included, the names must be separated by a comma. \| The pinlist can contain O, or IO pins. If all pins configured as outputs are to be searched, use the @ character. If more than one pin is included, the names must be separated by a comma. \|⏎\| port \| If you issue the query in a multi-port test, you must specify one of the pattern ports in order to determine the search range uniquely. \| If you issue the query in a multi-port test, you must specify one of the pattern ports in order to determine the search range uniquely. \|⏎\| cycle_no \| The cycle number of the first occurrence of a logic 0 or logic 1 state. \| The cycle number of the first occurrence of a logic 0 or logic 1 state. \|⏎\| output_level \| The output_level is one of the following: \| The output_level is one of the following: \|⏎\| output_level \| LOW \| This indicates that a logic 0 occurs at the given cycle. \|⏎\| output_level \| HIGH \| This indicates that a logic 1 occurs at the given cycle. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98677.htm`。` |
| 57 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.171_PACT__PACT__-__Parallel_ACTivities_control.md` | `98678.htm` | #0 | 7×3 | 16 | 5 | `\| control \| The control parameter is: \| The control parameter is: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\|  \| ACTIVE \| When a site is ACTIVE it receives all test data, and generates results for the tests. \|⏎\|  \| MASKED \| When a site is MASKED, it receives all test data, as with the ACTIVE parameter. However, the receivers for the site are disabled and no result information is available. \|⏎\| site \| A single integer representing a site number. \| A single integer representing a site number. \|⏎\| site_string \| A string consisting of "0" and "1". One character must be given for every site that is configured in the pin configuration. "0" indicates that the respective site is (to be) masked. "1" indicates that the respective site is (to be) activated. \| A string consisting of "0" and "1". One character must be given for every site that is configured in the pin configuration. "0" indicates that the respective site is (to be) masked. "1" indicates that the respective site is (to be) activated. \|⏎\| ALL \| means that all configured sites that are available for testing (that is, do not use a channel that is inactivated in the MCDIgnore file) are to be activated or masked.<br><br>For the MCDIgnore file see MCDIgnore usage and restrictions in Service Guide Part 1. \| means that all configured sites that are available for testing (that is, do not use a channel that is inactivated in the MCDIgnore file) are to be activated or masked.<br><br>For the MCDIgnore file see MCDIgnore usage and restrictions in Service Guide Part 1. \|⏎\| STR \| forces the query to return a site string instead of a single line for every site. \| forces the query to return a site string instead of a single line for every site. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98678.htm`。` |
| 58 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.174_PASS__-__PASS_or_fail_result.md` | `98681.htm` | #0 | 5×3 | 11 | 4 | `\| pin_list \| The last test result is provided for the pins specified in pin_list. However, if any sequencer program of the setup contains a[MEAS](<02.06.02.02.160_MEAS_-__execute_a_MEASurement_set.md>) instruction (see the [SQPG](<02.06.02.02.237_SQPG__SQPG__-__SeQuencer_ProGramming.md>) command), and the measurement set that is executed by this instruction is defined for an I pin, then the measurement result for this pin will be included in the reply of PASS? if the I pin is specified in pin_list. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| port_list \| The last test result is provided for the pins of a port specified in port_list. The global pass/fail result can be retrieved by a following [PRLT?](<02.06.02.02.183_PRLT__-__request_Parallel_ResuLTs.md>) query. \|⏎\| PRLT \| PRLT not specified: \| PRLT not specified: \|⏎\|  \|  \| If not specified, the measurement results are returned only for the site in result focus. The command returns individual results for the measurements defined by parameter spec and for the pins defined by pinlist. A following PRLT? query will return an error message. \|⏎\| spec \| The mandatory parameter defines the test specification that can be: \| The mandatory parameter defines the test specification that can be: \|⏎\| spec \| Fo_Se \| to check that the force/sense lines are correctly wired on your DUT board. This measurement must be made in disconnected state. \|⏎\| spec \| Ma_S1 \| to check the force connections on the ganging line. The force of master and all forces of slaves must be connected within a resistant of 20 Ω. PASS is returned if pin is not in ganging mode.<br><br>Only supported with the [DC Scale XPS256 card](<../02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.05.03_DC_Scale_XPS256_card.md>). \|⏎\| averages \| The extra parameter defines the number of averaged measurements to be performed.<br><br>The valid range of the integer parameter is 1 …128.<br><br>The default value is 1. \| The extra parameter defines the number of averaged measurements to be performed.<br><br>The valid range of the integer parameter is 1 …128.<br><br>The default value is 1. \|⏎\| pinlist \| pinlist must contain the names of DPS previously defined by the DFPS command (and used in DPS operating mode) or the names of DPS previously defined by the DFPN command (and used in PMU operating mode).<br><br>See [DFPS, DFPS? - (DeFine Power Supply)](<02.06.02.02.65_DFPS__DFPS__-__DeFine_Power_Supply.md>) and [DFPN, DFPN? - (DeFine Pin Name)](<02.06.02.02.63_DFPN__DFPN__-__DeFine_Pin_Name.md>) for more information.<br><br>If more than one pin is specified, the names must be separated by a comma. The character @ can be used to specify all DPS pins. \| pinlist must contain the names of DPS previously defined by the DFPS command (and used in DPS operating mode) or the names of DPS previously defined by the DFPN command (and used in PMU operating mode).<br><br>See [DFPS, DFPS? - (DeFine Power Supply)](<02.06.02.02.65_DFPS__DFPS__-__DeFine_Power_Supply.md>) and [DFPN, DFPN? - (DeFine Pin Name)](<02.06.02.02.63_DFPN__DFPN__-__DeFine_Pin_Name.md>) for more information.<br><br>If more than one pin is specified, the names must be separated by a comma. | 61 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.181_PRCF__PRCF__-__Pseudo_Random_bit_sequence_ConFiguration.md` | `118218.htm` | #0 | 8×3 | 16 | 8 | `\| PRBS_set_name \| Specifies the name of a PRBS set that must have been defined with PRDF. \| Specifies the name of a PRBS set that must have been defined with PRDF. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| property \| Specifies a property of the PRBS set. There are two properties to be configured: \| Specifies a property of the PRBS set. There are two properties to be configured: \|⏎\| property \| POLYNOMIAL \| This property defines the connection polynomial of the LFSR. It is coded as a bit string where the position of the bit identifies the degree of the addend, and its value defines the coefficient of this addend. The constant term of the polynomial, which is always 1, must be omitted. The last bit is supposed to determine the degree n of the polynomial. Therefore, the length of the string must be n, and its last bit must be "1". \|⏎\| property \| SEED \| This property defines the seed for the LFSR in the order in which it is output. This property is only required for PRBS generation. The seed for the PRBS check is taken from the received data. \|⏎\| ALL \| This value can be specified in the query to retrieve the configuration data of all PRBS sets. \| This value can be specified in the query to retrieve the configuration data of all PRBS sets. \|⏎\| PRM \| This value can be specified to modify or retrieve the configuration of the current primary PRBS set. \| This value can be specified to modify or retrieve the configuration of the current primary PRBS set. \|⏎\| property_value \| Specify a bit string for this parameter. Its meaning depends on the selected property. For POLYNOMIAL it encodes the connection polynomial. For SEED, it defines the initial state of the LFSR. The maximum length is 32 in both cases.<br><br>- The default for the polynomial is<br><br>11111110111010110011110000100101,<br><br>which is the polynomial<br><br>1+x+x2+x3+x4+x5+x6+x7+x9+x10+x11+x13+x15+x16+x19+x20+x21+x22+x27+x30+x32.<br><br>- The default for the seed is<br><br>00110010011011110001001101100111. \| Specify a bit string for this parameter. Its meaning depends on the selected property. For POLYNOMIAL it encodes the connection polynomial. For SEED, it defines the initial state of the LFSR. The maximum length is 32 in both cases.<br><br>- The default for the polynomial is<br><br>11111110111010110011110000100101,<br><br>which is the polynomial<br><br>1+x+x2+x3+x4+x5+x6+x7+x9+x10+x11+x13+x15+x16+x19+x20+x21+x22+x27+x30+x32.<br><br>- The default for the seed is<br><br>00110010011011110001001101100111. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Specifies the pins to which pin this configuration is to be applied. \| Specifies the pins to which pin this configuration is to be applied. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/118218.htm`。` |

If this parameter is omitted, this command returns the result of previous execution of the PSEE or PSIE command. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| mode \| This parameter defines the type of result. \| This parameter defines the type of result. \|⏎\| mode \| PVAL \| Binary data for each pin defined in the measurement will be returned. \|⏎\| mode \| PPF \| A per-pin pass/fail result will be returned. Only valid when a setnr is given. \|⏎\| mode \| BVAL \| Binary data mode.<br><br>4 Bytes header for the sample count<br><br>4 Byte for test points count<br><br>Test points (8 bytes per test point)<br><br>Sampler (channel count) is set to 1. \|⏎\| mode \| GPF \| A global pass/fail result will be returned. The result parameter contains a P (pass) or an F (fail) if the GPF or PPF option has been selected. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Only DPS pins and DC Scale pins configured with [DFPS](<02.06.02.02.65_DFPS__DFPS__-__DeFine_Power_Supply.md>) are supported. \| Only DPS pins and DC Scale pins configured with [DFPS](<02.06.02.02.65_DFPS__DFPS__-__DeFine_Power_Supply.md>) are supported. \|⏎\| binary_data \| The binary data result string. The string starts with the '#' character, followed by a digit in the range 1 to 9 which stipulates how many digits are used in the data length specifier. The data length specifier is next - up to 9 digits determine how many bytes of data will follow. Then comes the actual data string which appears as a list of values separated by a "," (comma). The first byte of data is an identifier which defines the format of the following data. For a waveform the first byte is '\\x00' with the waveform represented as FLOAT64 values. Each value is in Amps [A]. \| The binary data result string. The string starts with the '#' character, followed by a digit in the range 1 to 9 which stipulates how many digits are used in the data length specifier. The data length specifier is next - up to 9 digits determine how many bytes of data will follow. Then comes the actual data string which appears as a list of values separated by a "," (comma). The first byte of data is an identifier which defines the format of the following data. For a waveform the first byte is '\\x00' with the waveform represented as FLOAT64 values. This parameter defines then the sink current limit while the source current limit is defined by the above source_current parameter.<br><br>The parameter is not required if the current limits are programmed symmetrically to the same (absolute) value. Both, the source and the sink value are then defined by the source_current parameter.<br><br>The query answer shows the sink_current parameter only if the programmed sink current limit is not symmetrical to the source current limit. \| The extra parameter defines the sink current limit (ilimit_sink) in [A].<br><br>The parameter is required only if the source and sink current limits are set to different values. This parameter defines then the sink current limit while the source current limit is defined by the above source_current parameter.<br><br>The parameter is not required if the current limits are programmed symmetrically to the same (absolute) value. Both, the source and the sink value are then defined by the source_current parameter.<br><br>The query answer shows the sink_current parameter only if the programmed sink current limit is not symmetrical to the source current limit. \|⏎\| impedance \| The optional parameter sets the DPS output impedance. You can select:<br><br>- LOZ sets the DPS output impedance to low when the test system is in the disconnect state. The current limit remains at the same level as in the active state, while the force voltage is set to 0 V.<br>- HIZ sets the DPS output to high impedance when the test system is in the disconnect state. This sets the channel to the minimum possible current limit. HIZ is the default state for DPS-slot types. If it is applied to DC Scale cards, HIZ_R is used instead of HIZ.<br>- HIZ_R sets the DPS output to high impedance by switching the output relay off when the test system is in the disconnect state.<br><br>Note that relay switching is slower than with HIZ.<br><br>- LOZ_HIZ combines LOZ with HIZ. First, the DPS is switched to 0 V with an unchanged current limit (just like in LOZ mode). After the output voltage has reached 0 V (±50 mV) or after a timeout of 1 second, the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This combines the advantages of both modes. A capacitor on the load board is quickly discharged and then the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This is safer when changing the DUT or DUT board after a disconnect.<br>- LOZ_HIZ_R combines LOZ with HIZ_R. First, the DPS is switched to 0 V with an unchanged current limit (just like in LOZ mode). After the output voltage has reached 0 V (±50mV) or after a timeout of 1 second, the DPS pin is switched to open. This combines the advantages of both modes. A capacitor on the DUT board is quickly discharged and then the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This is safer when changing the DUT or the DUT board after a disconnect. \| The optional parameter sets the DPS output impedance. You can select:<br><br>- LOZ sets the DPS output impedance to low when the test system is in the disconnect state. The current limit remains at the same level as in the active state, while the force voltage is set to 0 V.<br>- HIZ sets the DPS output to high impedance when the test system is in the disconnect state. This sets the channel to the minimum possible current limit. HIZ is the default state for DPS-slot types. If it is applied to DC Scale cards, HIZ_R is used instead of HIZ.<br>- HIZ_R sets the DPS output to high impedance by switching the output relay off when the test system is in the disconnect state.<br><br>Note that relay switching is slower than with HIZ.<br><br>- LOZ_HIZ combines LOZ with HIZ. First, the DPS is switched to 0 V with an unchanged current limit (just like in LOZ mode). After the output voltage has reached 0 V (±50 mV) or after a timeout of 1 second, the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This combines the advantages of both modes. A capacitor on the load board is quickly discharged and then the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This is safer when changing the DUT or DUT board after a disconnect.<br>- LOZ_HIZ_R combines LOZ with HIZ_R. First, the DPS is switched to 0 V with an unchanged current limit (just like in LOZ mode). After the output voltage has reached 0 V (±50mV) or after a timeout of 1 second, the DPS pin is switched to open. This combines the advantages of both modes. A capacitor on the DUT board is quickly discharged and then the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This is safer when changing the DUT or the DUT board after a disconnect. \|⏎\| setup_time \| The optional parameter is an integer which specifies the settling time (in ms) of the DPS. All DPS's are synchronized so that they finish their settling periods at the same point in time. This is the point when the system is switched to the connect state. If there are, for example, two DPS's, one with a settling time of 10 ms, and the other with a settling time of 4 ms, then the second DPS will be started 6 ms after the first:<br><br>![Synchronizing Different DPS Settling Times](<../../../../raw/93235.png>)<br><br>The range is from -2147483647 up to 2147483646 ms.<br><br>The default value is 0.Note setup_time has a resolution of ms (floating point values are rounded to nearest integer). \| The optional parameter is an integer which specifies the settling time (in ms) of the DPS. All DPS's are synchronized so that they finish their settling periods at the same point in time. This is the point when the system is switched to the connect state. If there are, for example, two DPS's, one with a settling time of 10 ms, and the other with a settling time of 4 ms, then the second DPS will be started 6 ms after the first:<br><br>![Synchronizing Different DPS Settling Times](<../../../../raw/93235.png>)<br><br>The range is from -2147483647 up to 2147483646 ms.<br><br>The default value is 0.Note setup_time has a resolution of ms (floating point values are rounded to nearest integer). \|⏎\| pinlist \| pinlist must contain the names of pins previously defined by the [DFPS](<02.06.02.02.65_DFPS__DFPS__-__DeFine_Power_Supply.md>) command. If more than one pin is specified, the names must be separated by commas. The character @ can be used to set the levels for all DPS pins. \| pinlist must contain the names of pins previously defined by the [DFPS](<02.06.02.02.65_DFPS__DFPS__-__DeFine_Power_Supply.md>) command. If more than one pin is specified, the names must be separated by commas. The character @ can be used to set the levels for all DPS pins. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98702.htm`。` |

| 70 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.216_SAOF__SAOF__-__Set_Alternating_Operation_Features.md` | `98726.htm` | #0 | 7×3 | 14 | 7 | `\| mode \| The operation mode which can take the following values: \| The operation mode which can take the following values: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| mode \| ZERO \| Editing mode. This is the default. \|⏎\| mode \| APP \| This mode serves to append data to the sequencer and vector areas. In order to do that, you must first allocate memory for the respective area with the DMAS command. Now,in editing mode, the DMAS command defines the size of the area (it starts from address 0). If, however, the system is in APP mode, the existing area will be expanded by the size specified in the DMAS command.<br><br>Note that you cannot iterate this step. If you allocate memory in APP mode for a second time, the first expansion will be redefined. In order to further expand the area, you must first issue SAOFAPP again. \|⏎\| mode \| LOAD \| Load vectors. This mode improves performance of a vector download. \|⏎\| mode \| TMLD \| Load timing and/or pin attributes. This mode improves performance of a timing download. \|⏎\| mode \| EGRP \| Edit group definitions. \|⏎\| label \| If you call SAOF with a label name, you can generate a vector file for this label only. \| If you call SAOF with a label name, you can generate a vector file for this label only. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98726.htm`。` |

If no value is specified, all components of the respective type are processed. \|⏎\| multi-port_spec \| In a multi-port setup, enter the name of a specification instead of a component number, but only for spec_type TIM. In this case, the setup_comp_1 parameter will be ignored. \|⏎\| description \| Maximum 60 characters. Can be also a substring of a description, in which case all components containing this string in their description are returned. \| Maximum 60 characters. Can be also a substring of a description, in which case all components containing this string in their description are returned. \|⏎\| portlist \| With multi-port specifications, you can specify a list of ports. If you do, only those components will be displayed that are associated with one of the specified ports.<br><br>If you specify a port list, the setup_comp_1 parameter will be ignored. \| With multi-port specifications, you can specify a list of ports. | 75 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.223_SLDO_-__Switch_Level_and_Dps_Order.md` | `98735.htm` | #0 | 9×3 | 18 | 9 | `\| order \| This parameter sets the switching order mode: \| This parameter sets the switching order mode: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| order \| FDSL \| First DPS, second levels. If this parameter is specified, the DPS voltage values are always switched before the voltage values of the levels. \|⏎\| order \| CVDL \| There are three possibilities:<br><br>1. If DPS voltage values are increasing or stable, DPS voltages are switched before level voltages.<br><br>2. If DPS voltage values are decreasing or stable, level voltages are switched before DPS voltages.<br><br>3. If the voltages of some DPS pins are decreasing while the voltages of others are increasing (ambiguous case), DPS voltages are always switched first. \|⏎\| report \| This parameter sets the report mode, which can be one of the following: \| This parameter sets the report mode, which can be one of the following: \|⏎\| report \| ALL \| Reports a change in the switching order from DPS voltages (first) to level voltages (first) and vice versa. \|⏎\| report \| FLSD \| First levels, second DPS. Reports a change in the switching order to switching level voltages first. \|⏎\| report \| AMBI \| Ambiguous case. Reports an ambiguous case. \|⏎\| report \| NONE \| Nothing is reported. This is the default report mode. \|⏎\| operation \| This is the mode of operation. SAVE returns values for everything that can be set. \| This is the mode of operation. | 77 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.230_SQBV__SQBV__-__SeQuencer_Break_Vector_insertion.md` | `98745.htm` | #0 | 9×3 | 22 | 5 | `\| CTOF_align \| This parameter specifies the number of break vectors that are to be inserted before the last activation. If the specified number is not exactly divisible by 8, it will be autocorrected to the next multiple of 8. The default is 0. The maximum depends on the activ_type parameter.<br><br>Note that a value greate than 0 is not allowed for ports whose sequencing group does not admit any activation of primary sets ([FTCG](<02.06.02.02.125_FTCG__FTCG__-__Functional_Test_ConfiGure.md>) SKPR,ON). \| This parameter specifies the number of break vectors that are to be inserted before the last activation. If the specified number is not exactly divisible by 8, it will be autocorrected to the next multiple of 8. The default is 0. The maximum depends on the activ_type parameter.<br><br>Note that a value greate than 0 is not allowed for ports whose sequencing group does not admit any activation of primary sets ([FTCG](<02.06.02.02.125_FTCG__FTCG__-__Functional_Test_ConfiGure.md>) SKPR,ON). \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| pattern_align \| This parameter specifies the number of break vectors that are to be inserted before the first user vector. If the specified number is not exactly divisible by 8, it will be autocorrected to the next multiple of 8. The default is 0. The maximum depends on the activ_type parameter. \| This parameter specifies the number of break vectors that are to be inserted before the first user vector. If the specified number is not exactly divisible by 8, it will be autocorrected to the next multiple of 8. The default is 0. The maximum depends on the activ_type parameter. \|⏎\| activ_type \| The type of activation sequence which is to be compensated by the break vectors. The possible values are: \| The type of activation sequence which is to be compensated by the break vectors. The possible values are: \|⏎\|  \| NONE \| No primary sets are activated. This option can be used to compensate hardware specific delays. The maximum number for this type is 2 * 262136. \|⏎\|  \| TIM \| Only the timing is activated. The maximum number for this type, which must not be smaller than that specified for NONE, is the number for NONE plus 262136. \|⏎\|  \| TIM_LEV \| Timing and levels are activated. The maximum number for this type, which must not be smaller than that specified for TIM, is the number for TIM plus 262136. \|⏎\|  \| ALL \| All primary sets are activated. This is the default. The maximum number for this type, which must not be smaller than that specified for TIM_LEV, is the number for TIM_LEV plus 2 * 262136. \|⏎\| port \| The port where the break vectors are to be inserted. Note that the vectors are inserted into all patterns of this port. \| The port where the break vectors are to be inserted. Note that the vectors are inserted into all patterns of this port. \|⏎\| portlist \| The ports for which the settings are to be returned. Specify @@ to retrieve the settings of all ports. \| The ports for which the settings are to be returned. Specify @@ to retrieve the settings of all ports. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98745.htm`。` |
| 78 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.232_SQGB__SQGB__-__SeQuencer_GloBal.md` | `98746.htm` | #0 | 14×3 | 28 | 14 | `\| mode \| Possible values are: \| Possible values are: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| mode \| ACQF \| ACQuire Failure mode. Only failed cycles are recorded, starting after analyze_delay cycles. If analyze_delay is not a multiple of 8, recording starts after the nearest smaller multiple of 8. In this mode the Error Map is called the Acquire Failure Map.<br><br>As the test proceeds the Acquire Failure Map is filled with failure data until the test stops or the map becomes completely full, whichever occurs first. If the map stops recording before the test has finished, the sequencer, which is in standard mode, continues executing the test until the end.<br><br>This mode is only supported for compatibility reasons. Instead, you should use DCGM and DCRP to configure the Error Map. The effect of ACQF is reproduced by:<br><br>DCGM FAIL,STRT,NONE<br><br>DCRP EMAP,<error_map_pos>,<ref_port> \|⏎\| mode \| ACFF \| This mode is functionally identical to ACQF. It is obsolete and should not be used in new applications. \|⏎\| mode \| EL \| EndLess. The sequencer repeats the complete test, including primaries and multiport synchronization, in an endless loop and can only be stopped by Break.<br><br>The analyze_delay parameter is ignored. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented mode.<br><br>This mode is used for oscilloscope tests. The Error Map functions as a rolling buffer. After stopping the sequencer, the Error Map shows the last "buffer-full" of recorded cycles. \|⏎\| mode \| ELPT \| EndLess PaTtern. The sequencer repeats only the user program. This avoids critical holds during measurement.<br><br>In case of multiport testing, ports may run with different frequencies. Using the ELPT mode in this case, can lead to the situation, that for loop counts >1 the synchronization is no longer executed. \|⏎\| mode \| EMPS \| Error Map PoSitioning. The Error Map is in cycle oriented mode, the sequencer in default mode. The analyze_delay parameter specifies the end of the recorded area. If it is not a multiple of 8, the last cycle to be displayed is the largest multiple of 8 that is smaller than analyze_delay. The first cycle to be displayed is determined by the size of the Error Map (8k cycles).<br><br>This mode is only supported for compatibility reasons. Instead, you should use DCGM and DCRP to configure the Error Map. The effect of EMPS is reproduced by:<br><br>DCGM ALL,HALT,NONE<br><br>DCRP EMAP,<error_map_pos>,<ref_port> \|⏎\| mode \| SE \| Stop on Error. The sequencer runs from the beginning of the test and stops when an error is encountered. If no errors occur, the sequencer runs to the end of the test and stops. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM. The analyze_delay parameter is ignored. \|⏎\| mode \| SEEL \| Stop on Error and EndLess. The sequencer repeats the test in an endless loop, unless an error is encountered, whereupon it stops. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode. This mode is used to locate sporadic failures. \|⏎\| mode \| STSQ \| STop SeQuencer. Stops the sequencer after analyze_delay cycles. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM. See also the query [GETC? - (GET Cycles)](<02.06.02.02.131_GETC__-__GET_Cycles.md>) for the analyze_delay count. \|⏎\| mode \| STRD \| In the query reply, this mode is displayed if the current combination of recording mode, as set with DCGM, and interpretation of the Error Map position, as set with DCRP, is different from all the other SQGB modes. For example, the Error Map can be in cycle oriented recording mode, and analyze_delay be interpreted as a start value.<br><br>In the command, this option leaves it to SmarTest to select the appropriate recording mode (cycle oriented or acquire failure), unless the recording mode has been explicitly set with DCGM. \|⏎\| mode \| STSV \| STop Sequencer on Vector. Stops the sequencer at the logical vector number specified in the analyze_delay parameter before executing the vector. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM.<br><br>Note that the STSV option cannot be used if no start label has been defined with SQSL, or if the start label is a burst or multi-port burst label. \|⏎\| analyze_delay \| Sets the analyze delay. This parameter is mandatory for the STSQ and STSV modes. Its interpretation depends on the selected mode parameter. The number must be between 1 (0 for ACQF) and 281,474,976,710,655 = 2^48 - 1 (2^48 - 2 for ACQF).<br><br>If the mode parameter is set to STSV, three cases must be distinguished:<br><br>- If analyze_delay is an existing logical vector number of the actual start label, the sequencer will be stopped at this logical vector before executing the vector.<br>- If analyze_delay specifies the total number of logical vectors (= greatest logical vector number of the test + 1) the sequencer stops at the last valid vector.<br>- If analyze_delay is greater than the total number of logical vectors, an error message is issued. \| Sets the analyze delay. This parameter is mandatory for the STSQ and STSV modes. Its interpretation depends on the selected mode parameter. The number must be between 1 (0 for ACQF) and 281,474,976,710,655 = 2^48 - 1 (2^48 - 2 for ACQF).<br><br>If the mode parameter is set to STSV, three cases must be distinguished:<br><br>- If analyze_delay is an existing logical vector number of the actual start label, the sequencer will be stopped at this logical vector before executing the vector.<br>- If analyze_delay specifies the total number of logical vectors (= greatest logical vector number of the test + 1) the sequencer stops at the last valid vector.<br>- If analyze_delay is greater than the total number of logical vectors, an error message is issued. \|⏎\| ref_port \| This parameter accepts a single port. It must be specified in the following situation:<br><br>- The selected mode requires an analyze delay (but is different from STSV), and<br>- the start label is a multi-port burst.<br><br>It specifies the port which is used for counting the cycles specified with the analyze_delay parameter. When this reference port has completed the specified number of cycles, the sequencers will be stopped, or cycle recording started or stopped, for all ports. Note that the reference port must belong to the current start label (as defined by SQSL). \| This parameter accepts a single port. It must be specified in the following situation:<br><br>- The selected mode requires an analyze delay (but is different from STSV), and<br>- the start label is a multi-port burst.<br><br>It specifies the port which is used for counting the cycles specified with the analyze_delay parameter. When this reference port has completed the specified number of cycles, the sequencers will be stopped, or cycle recording started or stopped, for all ports. Note that the reference port must belong to the current start label (as defined by SQSL). \|⏎\| port_list \| If the current mode requires an analyze delay, you can specify a port list in the query. The reply will give the calculated analyze delays for those ports that are not used as the reference port. These delays are derived from the analyze delay of the reference port and the ratio between the period lengths of the reference port and the other port in the primary timing setup. If you specify @@, SQGB will return the settings for all ports of the current start label. \| If the current mode requires an analyze delay, you can specify a port list in the query. The reply will give the calculated analyze delays for those ports that are not used as the reference port. These delays are derived from the analyze delay of the reference port and the ratio between the period lengths of the reference port and the other port in the primary timing setup. If you specify @@, SQGB will return the settings for all ports of the current start label. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98746.htm`。` |

The default for this parameter is the default port @ to which all pins belong.<br><br>Specify @@ to display attributes for all labels of all ports. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/103621.htm`。` |
| 80 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.236_SQLB__SQLB__-__SeQuencer_LaBel_definition.md` | `98748.htm` | #0 | 19×3 | 37 | 20 | `\| label \| The label name. It identifies the label which is to be defined for the specified port, or whose definition is to be displayed. Enter a string enclosed in double quotes. The string must have a length of between 1 and 128 alpha-numeric characters.<br><br>If you enter an empty string in the command, the label specified by the start_cmd_no and the stop_cmd_no parameters is deleted. Note, however, that the respective lines of the sequencer area are not deleted.<br><br>See [SmarTest character and number limits](<../../01_Getting_started/01.05_SmarTest_character_and_number_limits/01.05_SmarTest_character_and_number_limits.md#_128029__max_labels_per_port>) for the maximum number of labels per port. \| The label name. It identifies the label which is to be defined for the specified port, or whose definition is to be displayed. Enter a string enclosed in double quotes. The string must have a length of between 1 and 128 alpha-numeric characters.<br><br>If you enter an empty string in the command, the label specified by the start_cmd_no and the stop_cmd_no parameters is deleted. Note, however, that the respective lines of the sequencer area are not deleted.<br><br>See [SmarTest character and number limits](<../../01_Getting_started/01.05_SmarTest_character_and_number_limits/01.05_SmarTest_character_and_number_limits.md#_128029__max_labels_per_port>) for the maximum number of labels per port. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| label \| For the query you can also enter: \| For the query you can also enter: \|⏎\| label \| ALL \| to read the configurations for all labels. \|⏎\| label \| SUR \| (SURround) to obtain configurations for the label selected for editing (in the Pattern Tool) by the TSTL (TeST Label) command, as well as the labels immediately preceding and following it. \|⏎\| label \| FRS \| (FiRSt) to return only the first label in the sequencer area of the specified port.<br><br>If the label you are querying does not exist, the firmware returns nothing. \|⏎\| type \| The label type. This parameter must always be present. It has the following options: \| The label type. This parameter must always be present. It has the following options: \|⏎\| type \| MAIN \| A MAIN program. A MAIN label can be used as a start label (see the [SQSL](<02.06.02.02.240_SQSL__SQSL__-__SeQuencer_Start_Label.md>) command) if it is defined for the default port @. It can call any type of subroutine. It can be called from a burst (type BRST) or multi-port burst label (type MPBU).<br><br>The rudimentary sequencer program that is created for this label type consists of the STVA, STSA, and STOP instructions. \|⏎\| type \| SVEC \| Vector subroutine. A subroutine that cannot serve as a start label. It must be called with the JSUB sequencer instruction from a MAIN label or another SVEC label. You can nest SVEC subroutines up to 7 levels deep.<br><br>The rudimentary sequencer program that is created for this label type consists of the STVA and the RSUB instructions. \|⏎\| type \| BRST \| Burst mode. Defines a burst label. A burst label executes a sequence of MAIN labels in burst mode. Its sequencer program consists of a sequence of CALL instructions for the individual MAIN labels, and is terminated by a BEND instruction. The BEND instruction is created automatically by SQLB. The same MAIN label can be called by several burst labels.<br><br>For more information on the subject and the dependencies that have to be taken into account see [Burst and Multi-Burst Labels](<02.06.07.01.08.05.03_Burst_labels_and_multi-port_burst_labels.md>). \|⏎\| type \| DSRC \| The digital source label. A type of label used only by SSRC and SRCV. A DSRC label is not executable. The contents of DSRC labels are determined by DVCU. See [DVCU, DVCU? - (Define Vector variable Configuration Usage](<02.06.02.02.96_DVCU__DVCU__-__Define_Vector_variable_Configuration_Usage.md>)) for more information. \|⏎\| type \| MPBU \| Multiport burst mode. A burst label that is executed simultaneously for several ports. You must define the multi-port burst label separately for all the required ports; this is the only case where the same label name can be used more than once.<br><br>The structure of a multiport burst is the same as that of a normal burst label. The parameters of the CALL instructions will be different for the same label in the individual ports.<br><br>Note that the ports for which a multiport burst is defined must not have any pins in common. This is not checked by the firmware. \|⏎\| type \| SJMP \| Jump subroutine. This label type is allowed for downward compatibility only. It should not be used in new applications. \|⏎\| type \| ALL \| For the query, you can type ALL to read the configurations for all label types. \|⏎\| start_cmd_no \| The start command number parameter identifies the position within the sequencer area where the label starts.<br><br>Note that the exact meaning of this number depends on the operation mode, as defined with the SAOF command. In standard mode (SAOF ZERO), the command lines are counted from the start of the sequencer area, but in append mode (SAOF APP) they are counted from the start of the expansion of the sequencer area previously defined with DMAS. See example 4, below. \| The start command number parameter identifies the position within the sequencer area where the label starts.<br><br>Note that the exact meaning of this number depends on the operation mode, as defined with the SAOF command. In standard mode (SAOF ZERO), the command lines are counted from the start of the sequencer area, but in append mode (SAOF APP) they are counted from the start of the expansion of the sequencer area previously defined with DMAS. See example 4, below. \|⏎\| stop_cmd_no \| The stop command number parameter identifies the position within the sequencer area where the label stops.<br><br>Note that the exact meaning of this number depends on the operation mode, as defined with the SAOF command. In standard mode (SAOF ZERO), the command lines are counted from the start of the sequencer area, but in append mode (SAOF APP) they are counted from the start of the expansion of the sequencer area previously defined with DMAS. See example 4, below. \| The stop command number parameter identifies the position within the sequencer area where the label stops.<br><br>Note that the exact meaning of this number depends on the operation mode, as defined with the SAOF command. In standard mode (SAOF ZERO), the command lines are counted from the start of the sequencer area, but in append mode (SAOF APP) they are counted from the start of the expansion of the sequencer area previously defined with DMAS. See example 4, below. \|⏎\| base_wf_set \| The base waveform set. This waveform set is used by the pattern tool to translate the physical waveform DC/DC power units of the vector area into device cycle names and data parameters, and vice versa. \| The base waveform set. This waveform set is used by the pattern tool to translate the physical waveform DC/DC power units of the vector area into device cycle names and data parameters, and vice versa. \|⏎\| wavetable \| The base wavetable. This wavetable is used by the pattern tool to translate the physical waveform DC/DC power units of the vector areas into device cycle names and data parameters, and vice versa. \| The base wavetable. This wavetable is used by the pattern tool to translate the physical waveform DC/DC power units of the vector areas into device cycle names and data parameters, and vice versa. \|⏎\| sync_grp \| If type has been set to MPBU, you can specify a synchronization (sequencing) group for the multi-port burst with this parameter. \| If type has been set to MPBU, you can specify a synchronization (sequencing) group for the multi-port burst with this parameter. \|⏎\| port \| The name of the port for which the label is defined. If you do not specify a port, the label will be defined for the default port @, to which all pins belong. \| The name of the port for which the label is defined. If you do not specify a port, the label will be defined for the default port @, to which all pins belong. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98748.htm`。` |
| 81 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.241_SQST__SQST__-__SeQuencer_STate.md` | `98752.htm` | #0 | 9×3 | 17 | 10 | `\| seq_state \| Gives the state of the sequencer: \| Gives the state of the sequencer: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| seq_state \| OFF \| In the OFF state, both the sequencers and the clock are off. All pins go into the high-z state.<br><br>This option can only be applied to all execution contexts. \|⏎\| seq_state \| HALT \| In the HALT state, both the sequencer and the clock are off, and the drivers keep their last level or remain in high-z state if already in high-z state.<br><br>Note that you cannot pass directly from HALT to OFF or from OFF to HALT. You must first pass to BRK in both cases.<br><br>This option can only be applied to all execution contexts. \|⏎\| seq_state \| BRK \| In the BRK state, the clock is on and the break waveform is applied to the DUT.<br><br>This option can only be applied to all execution contexts. \|⏎\| seq_state \| ABRT \| This is not a sequencer state, but an instruction to change the state of an execution context from RUN to BRK. ABRT requires that an execution context is specified. \|⏎\| seq_state \| CBRK \| This is not a sequencer state, but a conditional instruction. If no context is in BRK or RUN state, the state of the global context will be changed to BRK. If any context is in BRK or RUN state, nothing will be done. \|⏎\| seq_state \| RUN \| In the RUN state, the clock is on and the sequencer applies vectors to the DUT. This option can be applied to a specified execution context. \|⏎\| seq_state \| Note that if the sequencer state changes from OFF or BRK to RUN, the sequencer runs with the global settings that have previously been defined with [SQGB, SQGB? - (SeQuencer GloBal)](<02.06.02.02.232_SQGB__SQGB__-__SeQuencer_GloBal.md>). \| Note that if the sequencer state changes from OFF or BRK to RUN, the sequencer runs with the global settings that have previously been defined with [SQGB, SQGB? - (SeQuencer GloBal)](<02.06.02.02.232_SQGB__SQGB__-__SeQuencer_GloBal.md>). \|⏎\| exec_context \| An execution context. In the command, this parameter can only be added to the sequencer state RUN and the instruction ABRT. It must be added if ABRT is specified. For more information on execution contexts see [Using execution contexts](<02.06.07.01.08.05.03.02.03_Using_execution_contexts.md>) .<br><br>If the query is issued without a context, then RUN will be returned if there is at least one context that is in the RUN state. \| An execution context. In the command, this parameter can only be added to the sequencer state RUN and the instruction ABRT. It must be added if ABRT is specified. For more information on execution contexts see [Using execution contexts](<02.06.07.01.08.05.03.02.03_Using_execution_contexts.md>) .<br><br>If the query is issued without a context, then RUN will be returned if there is at least one context that is in the RUN state. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98752.htm`。` |
| 82 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.243_SREC__SREC__-__Set_RECeiver_mask.md` | `98754.htm` | #0 | 6×3 | 12 | 6 | `\| mask_value \| The mask value parameter specifies whether a pin mask will be applied: \| The mask value parameter specifies whether a pin mask will be applied: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| mask_value \| ACT \| If ACT is selected, the output components of the pins specified in the pin list become active (not masked). \|⏎\| mask_value \| X \| If X is selected, the output components of the pins specified in the pin list are masked. \|⏎\| mask_value \| STOR \| If STOR is selected, the output components of the pins specified in the pin list are stored. \|⏎\| mask_value \| RSTO \| If RSTO is selected, the output components of the pins specified in the pin list are restored. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The name of the pin or pins to be masked. This parameter may also include groups previously created by [DFGP, DFGP? - (DeFine GrouP)](<02.06.02.02.60_DFGP__DFGP__-__DeFine_GrouP.md>) or by [DFGE, DFGE? - (DeFine Group Expressions)](<02.06.02.02.59_DFGE__DFGE__-__DeFine_Group_by_Expressions.md>). This parameter may also include groups previously created by [DFGP, DFGP? - (DeFine GrouP)](<02.06.02.02.60_DFGP__DFGP__-__DeFine_GrouP.md>) or by [DFGE, DFGE? - (DeFine Group Expressions)](<02.06.02.02.59_DFGE__DFGE__-__DeFine_Group_by_Expressions.md>). | 84 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.246_SSCT__SSCT__-__Source_Synchronous_data__Combine_and_Transfer.md` | `98756.htm` | #0 | 11×3 | 21 | 12 | `\| exec_mode \| Possible execution modes are: \| Possible execution modes are: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| exec_mode \| IMM \| The command is executed immediately. \|⏎\| exec_mode \| ACC \| Command execution is delayed to the next FTST? call or the next SSCT IMM call (whichever comes first). \|⏎\| exec_mode \| DEL \| Deletes all SSCT instances that ar still pending for the specified destination pin and source pins. The other parameters are ignored. \|⏎\| read_mode \| Possible read modes are: \| Possible read modes are: \|⏎\| read_mode \| NORM \| Source data is processed as it is stored in the source channels. This is the default. \|⏎\| read_mode \| INV \| Source data is inverted internally before it is processed. The source data itself is not changed. \|⏎\| read_mode \| Note that the read modes of SSAC and SSCT are concatenated. If inversion has been set for a source channel with SSAC, and the read mode of SSCT is also set to INV then the data of the respective channel will be inverted twice, and therefore processed as it is stored. \| Note that the read modes of SSAC and SSCT are concatenated. If inversion has been set for a source channel with SSAC, and the read mode of SSCT is also set to INV then the data of the respective channel will be inverted twice, and therefore processed as it is stored. \|⏎\| no_of_results \| Specifies the number of results to be processed. The default is all results (as defined with SSAC). The maximum is the number of results that are configured by SSAC.<br><br>The number to be specified here must be calculated as `result_granularity * number_of_cycles`.<br><br>The result granularity is the number of results per cycle. It can be 1 (one global result), in which case you must specify the number of cycles, or 8 (one result for every edge), in which case you must specify `8 * number_of_cycles`.<br><br>The number of bytes required to store the specified number of results must be a multiple of 8. If this requirement is not satisfied, the number of results will be auto-corrected. \| Specifies the number of results to be processed. The default is all results (as defined with SSAC). The maximum is the number of results that are configured by SSAC.<br><br>The number to be specified here must be calculated as `result_granularity * number_of_cycles`.<br><br>The result granularity is the number of results per cycle. It can be 1 (one global result), in which case you must specify the number of cycles, or 8 (one result for every edge), in which case you must specify `8 * number_of_cycles`.<br><br>The number of bytes required to store the specified number of results must be a multiple of 8. If this requirement is not satisfied, the number of results will be auto-corrected. \|⏎\| dest_pin \| The target buffer pin into which the combined results are transferred. This should be an input pin, in order to avoid conflicts within the result area between the buffer function and pin specific result data. This pin must have been defined as a buffer pin with SSAC.<br><br>Note that the Error Map for this pin must be located in RDRAM (DCRT command). \| The target buffer pin into which the combined results are transferred. This should be an input pin, in order to avoid conflicts within the result area between the buffer function and pin specific result data. This pin must have been defined as a buffer pin with SSAC.<br><br>Note that the Error Map for this pin must be located in RDRAM (DCRT command). \|⏎\| source_pins \| The pins whose results are to be combined. Note that the Error Map for these pins must be located in RDRAM (DCRT command). \| The pins whose results are to be combined. Note that the Error Map for these pins must be located in RDRAM (DCRT command). \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98756.htm`。` |

The values recorded in the previous logging session will be reset to 0.<br><br>The values of the current logging session can be retrieved with the TMUL? query. \|⏎\|  \| OFF \| OFF \| Stops logging of memory usage including the DEBUG_WATERMARK and DEBUG_SEQUENCES options.<br><br>The values that were determined are kept until logging is started again with option ON.<br><br>Note When logging is stopped, all protocol patterns for the specified ports will be deleted. \|⏎\|  \| DEBUG_WATERMARK \| DEBUG_WATERMARK \| This option causes TMUD to write all changes of the maximum used memory to the report window (in bytes). If necessary, it also activates logging of memory usage (the ON state). \|⏎\|  \| DEBUG_ALL \| DEBUG_ALL \| This option combines DEBUG_WATERMARK and DEBUG_SEQUENCES. If necessary, it also activates logging of memory usage (the ON state). Both these activities are also stopped by setting logState to OFF. \|⏎\|  \| If the parameter is omitted, the current counter values will be reset to 0, but the log state will not be changed. \| If the parameter is omitted, the current counter values will be reset to 0, but the log state will not be changed. \| If the parameter is omitted, the current counter values will be reset to 0, but the log state will not be changed. \|⏎\| portList \| Is a comma separated list of ports.<br><br>To apply the command to all ports, use @@. \| Is a comma separated list of ports.<br><br>To apply the command to all ports, use @@. \| Is a comma separated list of ports.<br><br>To apply the command to all ports, use @@. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/125642.htm`。` |

This is the default. \|⏎\|  \| LB \| The left boundary of the pass area is stored as the offset. \|⏎\|  \| RB \| The right boundary of the pass area is stored as the offset. \|⏎\| resource_type \| This parameter selects the timing or level resource. Both the driver high and low levels will be moved by the same values. \|⏎\| Level resources \| VOOFFSET \| Receiver offsets.
