Therefore, a negative search is possible.<br><br>Units for timing and level searches are ns and mV. \|⏎\| resolution \| This parameter defines the resolution of the binary search. The binary search is stopped when the distance between the last two binary search steps is ≤resolution.<br><br>Units for timing and level searches are ns and mV. \| This parameter defines the resolution of the binary search. The binary search is stopped when the distance between the last two binary search steps is ≤resolution.<br><br>Units for timing and level searches are ns and mV. \|⏎\| min_pass_count \| The PASS area determined by the binary search may still contain isolated FAIL results at its start or end. This parameter activates an additional check that is intended to exclude this. It specifies how many consecutive PASS results must be present at the start (before the end) of the putative PASS region. If this requirement is not satisfied, the left boundary of the area is moved to the right (the right boundary moved to the left) until the required number of consecutive PASS results is found.<br><br>The default for min_pass_count is 1. \| The PASS area determined by the binary search may still contain isolated FAIL results at its start or end. This parameter activates an additional check that is intended to exclude this. It specifies how many consecutive PASS results must be present at the start (before the end) of the putative PASS region. If this requirement is not satisfied, the left boundary of the area is moved to the right (the right boundary moved to the left) until the required number of consecutive PASS results is found.<br><br>The default for min_pass_count is 1. \|⏎\| min_stable_error_count \| This and the histogram_step_width parameter define a histogram based search, which is performed after the binary search. It moves the left boundary of the PASS area to the left (the right boundary to the right) until the number of errors is the same for the number of search steps specified with min_stable_error_count. \|⏎\| histogram_step_width \| This parameter defines the step width of the histogram search. \| This parameter defines the step width of the histogram search. \|⏎\| transition_detection \| This parameter controls the criterion for a test to be considered as PASSED. Possible values are: \| This parameter controls the criterion for a test to be considered as PASSED. Possible values are: \|⏎\|  \| PF \| The test is considered to have passed only if no errors occurred at all. This is the default \|⏎\|  \| ECL \| The test is considered to have passed if the number of errors is smaller than error_count_limit. \|⏎\|  \| PF_FASTSTOP \| Like PF but, using the FASTSTOP mode, stops the linear search after the first PASS area is detected (see [Linear search modes](<02.06.09.01.02.01_Linear_search_modes.md>)). \|⏎\|  \| ECL_FASTSTOP \| Like ECL but, using the FASTSTOP mode, stops the linear search after the first PASS area is detected (see [Linear search modes](<02.06.09.01.02.01_Linear_search_modes.md>)). \|⏎\| error_count_limit \| This parameter defines the minimum number of errors that are not tolerated if transition_detection is set to ECL. \| This parameter defines the minimum number of errors that are not tolerated if transition_detection is set to ECL. \|⏎\| restore_flag \| The restore_flag parameter enables or disables the restore process after a search was executed: It allows you to suppress storing of the search result. \| The restore_flag parameter enables or disables the restore process after a search was executed: It allows you to suppress storing of the search result. \|⏎\|  \| YES \| Restores the timing / level offsets to the values it had before the offset search was executed. \|⏎\|  \| NO \| The new measured offset values are used in the edge position calculation.<br><br>This is the default configuration. \|⏎\| setup_pinlist \| This parameter defines the pins the edge positions of which are to be changed. A linear search is performed, with an additional binary search carried out in the interval between the last passed and the first failed test. \|⏎\| search_type \| B \| Specifies a binary search carried out between the start and the stop positions. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The name of the pin or pins on which the search is to take place. This parameter may also include groups previously created using [DFGP, DFGP? - (DeFine GrouP)](<02.06.02.02.60_DFGP__DFGP__-__DeFine_GrouP.md>). If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. Pinlist must only contain pins configured as outputs. \| The name of the pin or pins on which the search is to take place. This parameter may also include groups previously created using [DFGP, DFGP? - (DeFine GrouP)](<02.06.02.02.60_DFGP__DFGP__-__DeFine_GrouP.md>). If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. Pinlist must only contain pins configured as outputs. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| This parameter can be: \| This parameter can be: \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| LT \| (less than) is returned if the first functional test of a search in the positive direction failed. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| LE \| (less than or equal to) is returned if all the functional tests performed between start and stop pass. LE is only returned for a search in the negative direction. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| EQ \| (equal to) is returned if the first failure of the search occurs between the start and the stop positions. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| GE \| (greater than or equal to) is returned if all the tests carried out between start and stop pass. GE can only be returned for a search in the positive direction. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| GT \| (greater than) is returned if the first functional test performed during a search in the negative direction fails. \|⏎\| pass_value \| The pass_value is the time (in ns) at which the last passed test occurs. A pass value is only returned for if value_specifier is EQ, LE, or GE. \| The pass_value is the time (in ns) at which the last passed test occurs. A pass value is only returned for if value_specifier is EQ, LE, or GE. \|⏎\| fail_value \| The fail_value is the time (in ns) at which the last failed test occurs. A fail value is only returned for if value_specifier is EQ, LT, or GT. \| The fail_value is the time (in ns) at which the last failed test occurs. A fail value is only returned for if value_specifier is EQ, LT, or GT. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98819.htm`。` |

For valid pogo blocks, refer to [DUT interface layout](<../02.03_DUT_board_design_reference/02.03.03.01_DUT_interface_layout.md>) and to [Utility pogo block mapping](<../02.03_DUT_board_design_reference/02.03.06.08.02_Utility_pogo_block_-_Utility_Card__V93000_EXA_Scale_systems.md#_347642__s_Utility_pogo_block_mapping>) in [Utility pogo block - Utility Card (V93000 EXA Scale systems)](<../02.03_DUT_board_design_reference/02.03.06.08.02_Utility_pogo_block_-_Utility_Card__V93000_EXA_Scale_systems.md>).<br><br>The legacy single‑digit format 1 ... 8 is also supported, but the query always returns the three‑digit format. \| One unique utility pogo block number or a list enclosed in brackets containing utility pogo blocks separated by commas.<br><br>The pogo block or the list of pogo blocks uses three‑digit numbers. Utility Card (V93000 EXA Scale systems)](<../02.03_DUT_board_design_reference/02.03.06.08.02_Utility_pogo_block_-_Utility_Card__V93000_EXA_Scale_systems.md>).<br><br>The legacy single‑digit format 1 ... 8 is also supported, but the query always returns the three‑digit format. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| ALL \| Keyword to use all available utility blocks with the given mode. \| Keyword to use all available utility blocks with the given mode. \|⏎\| mode \| Mode to be applied to a single block or the ganged utility blocks.<br><br>Available modes are: \| Mode to be applied to a single block or the ganged utility blocks.<br><br>Available modes are: \|⏎\| mode \| RD \| Parallel output (with [UTOT](<02.06.02.02.311_UTOT__UTOT__-__UTility_line_OuTputs.md>)), parallel input (with [UTIX?](<02.06.02.02.310_UTIX__-__UTility_line_Inputs_eXtended.md>)) or serial input (via serial interface pin SOUT), turns ganging on, if off. \|⏎\| mode \| RW \| Serial output (via serial interface pin SINP), parallel input (with [UTIX?](<02.06.02.02.310_UTIX__-__UTility_line_Inputs_eXtended.md>)) or serial input (via serial interface pin SOUT), turns ganging on, if off. \|⏎\| mode \| OFF \| Parallel output (with [UTOT](<02.06.02.02.311_UTOT__UTOT__-__UTility_line_OuTputs.md>)), parallel input (with [UTIX?](<02.06.02.02.310_UTIX__-__UTility_line_Inputs_eXtended.md>)), turns ganging off. \|⏎\| maskMode \| The extra parameter sets the mask mode for the defined utility blocks.<br><br>Available modes are: \| The extra parameter sets the mask mode for the defined utility blocks.<br><br>Available modes are: \|⏎\| maskMode \| MASK \| Enables the mask mode. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/124715.htm`。` |
| 95 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.316_VALD__-__VALid_Data.md` | `98841.htm` | #0 | 14×3 | 28 | 14 | `\| map_id \| Determines the type of Error Map for which data is to be read out: \| Determines the type of Error Map for which data is to be read out: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| map_id \| ERRM \| The error map option reads the error map. \|⏎\| map_id \| OVEM \| The overview error map option reads the overview error map in FCM mode (defined by DCRM). If the overview error map was in FCM mode for the last executed test, an error message will be issued if LMAP is selected.<br><br>Note If you use the LMAP error map mode, [FLBL](<02.06.02.02.123_FLBL__-__Failing_LaBeLs.md>) ignores the RETC clearing flag for failures. \|⏎\| map_id \| RECD \| The receive data option reads the error map (compatibility with the [ERMP](<02.06.02.02.114_ERMP__-__ERror_MaP.md>) query). Equivalent to ERRM. \|⏎\| map_id \| DERR \| The per pin detailed error map option reads the detailed error map. Equivalent to ERRM. \|⏎\| map_id \| EOLY \| The errors only map option reads the per pin errors only in the error map. The state can be: \| Returns the current state of the error map. The state can be: \|⏎\| map_state \| INVL \| Invalid is returned if no error map details are available (the setup has been changed, but no functional test has been performed). \|⏎\| map_state \| INCS \| This is returned if the contents of the error map are inconsistent with the current setup. \|⏎\| map_state \| CONS \| This is returned after a functional test has been performed and indicates that the contents of the error map are consistent with the current setup. \|⏎\| start \| This is an integer that indicates the first cycle that was recorded, or, for the LMAP option of map_id, the first label call that was recorded. start will be a value between 0 and 2^48. \| This is an integer that indicates the first cycle that was recorded, or, for the LMAP option of map_id, the first label call that was recorded. start will be a value between 0 and 2^48. \|⏎\| number \| This is an integer that indicates the number of cycles recorded, or for the LMAP option of map_id, the number of label calls recorded. If ERRM, RECD or DERR have been selected, the range for this parameter depends on whether the internal or the external error map is used. For the internal error map, it is 0 to 8191; for the external error map the upper limit can be higher. When EOLY has been selected, the upper limit is 2^48.<br><br>For the [Pin Scale 5000 card](<../02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.04.02_Pin_Scale_5000_card.md>), the upper limit is 4096.<br><br>In the reply, this parameter indicates the number of cycles (or label calls) which have been read. If ERRM, RECD or DERR have been selected, the range for this parameter depends on whether the internal or the external error map is used. For the internal error map, it is 0 to 8191; for the external error map the upper limit can be higher. When EOLY has been selected, the upper limit is 2^48.<br><br>For the [Pin Scale 5000 card](<../02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.04.02_Pin_Scale_5000_card.md>), the upper limit is 4096.<br><br>In the reply, this parameter indicates the number of cycles (or label calls) which have been read. Possible values are: \| The parameter specifies the target area of the command, and the source area of the query. Possible values are: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| applic \| PARA \| For ‘PARAllel test'. \|⏎\| applic \| PPSQ \| For ‘Per-Pin SeQuencer test'. \|⏎\| applic \| For DC Scale channels, only PPSQ is allowed. \| For DC Scale channels, only PPSQ is allowed. \|⏎\| type \| The type of memory. This can be<br><br>- SM for sequencer memory (the standard type), or<br>- SHMEM, a special kind of SM only for multi-site setups, or<br>- ALL (only in the query) for both SM and SHMEM. \| The type of memory. This can be<br><br>- SM for sequencer memory (the standard type), or<br>- SHMEM, a special kind of SM only for multi-site setups, or<br>- ALL (only in the query) for both SM and SHMEM. \|⏎\| port \| This parameter is required if the command is applied to a 7.x setup, where the STVA instructions are port specific (address specified in param_2).<br><br>If the parameter is omitted for an older setup (so that the address parameter refers to the one global vector area), VECC will find the correct location of the data by analyzing the STVA addresses of the setup. \| This parameter is required if the command is applied to a 7.x setup, where the STVA instructions are port specific (address specified in param_2).<br><br>If the parameter is omitted for an older setup (so that the address parameter refers to the one global vector area), VECC will find the correct location of the data by analyzing the STVA addresses of the setup. \|⏎\| port_list \| In the query, you can specify a port list for a 7.x setup. You will get an answer for every pin in pinlist that belongs to one of the specified ports. \| In the query, you can specify a port list for a 7.x setup. You will get an answer for every pin in pinlist that belongs to one of the specified ports. \|⏎\| address \| The address of the target area where the data transfer should start. This must be a multiple of 8. If a port is specified (which is required for a 7.x setup), the address refers to the specific vector area of this port. If no port is specified (which is only allowed for older setups), it refers to the one global vector area.<br><br>For the command, the start address may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. In this case, VECC extends the size of the target area accordingly; the new last address will be at address + length. \| The address of the target area where the data transfer should start. This must be a multiple of 8. If a port is specified (which is required for a 7.x setup), the address refers to the specific vector area of this port. If no port is specified (which is only allowed for older setups), it refers to the one global vector area.<br><br>For the command, the start address may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. In this case, VECC extends the size of the target area accordingly; the new last address will be at address + length. \|⏎\| length \| The length of the data that is to be down- or uploaded. This must be a multiple of 8.<br><br>In the command, address + length may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. In this case, VECC extends the size of the target area accordingly; the new last address will be at address + length.<br><br>This parameter is optional for the query. If you enter a length, the contents of the specified part of the vector area are returned. If you omit it, the contents of the memory from address to the current end address of the target area are returned. \| The length of the data that is to be down- or uploaded. This must be a multiple of 8.<br><br>In the command, address + length may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. In this case, VECC extends the size of the target area accordingly; the new last address will be at address + length.<br><br>This parameter is optional for the query. If you enter a length, the contents of the specified part of the vector area are returned. If you omit it, the contents of the memory from address to the current end address of the target area are returned. \|⏎\| pin_or_port \| The name of the pin whose vectors are to be down- or uploaded. If you specify a port, the vectors will be downloaded to all pins of this port.<br><br>Note that if the query is issued for a port, the answer will be given for the individual pins. \| The name of the pin whose vectors are to be down- or uploaded. If you specify a port, the vectors will be downloaded to all pins of this port.<br><br>Note that if the query is issued for a port, the answer will be given for the individual pins. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The pins for which vector data is to be returned from the vector area.<br><br>Note: You can either type the pin number or the pin alias. \| The pins for which vector data is to be returned from the vector area.<br><br>Note: You can either type the pin number or the pin alias. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| Vector data in binary format. This parameter allows you to define the length of data (in bytes) to be transferred and must be used as follows:<br><br>Type # to mark the start of the data block.<br><br>Type the number of digits for the length specifier (max. 9 digits) and then type the number of bytes to be transferred.<br><br>The compressed vector bytes are sent immediately after the length specifier and terminated by a linefeed character (`\\x0A`). \| Vector data in binary format. This parameter allows you to define the length of data (in bytes) to be transferred and must be used as follows:<br><br>Type # to mark the start of the data block.<br><br>Type the number of digits for the length specifier (max. 9 digits) and then type the number of bytes to be transferred.<br><br>The compressed vector bytes are sent immediately after the length specifier and terminated by a linefeed character (`\\x0A`). \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98850.htm`。` |

| 100 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.328_VRCS__-__Valid_ReConStruction.md` | `98861.htm` | #0 | 18×3 | 46 | 8 | `\| reconstr_type \| The reconstruction type determines how test cycles are counted. \| The reconstruction type determines how test cycles are counted. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| reconstr_type \| STRT \| Start analysis. Specifies that cycles are counted from the start of the test. \|⏎\| reconstr_type \| STOP \| Stop analysis. Specifies that cycles are counted from the end of the test. \|⏎\| reconstr_cond \| The reconstruction condition defines how VRCS? reacts to ambiguous sequencer instructions, that is, instructions that may generate a varying number of cycles from one test execution to the next. Examples for such instructions are JMPE or multiple counted match loops. \| The reconstruction condition defines how VRCS? reacts to ambiguous sequencer instructions, that is, instructions that may generate a varying number of cycles from one test execution to the next. Examples for such instructions are JMPE or multiple counted match loops. \|⏎\|  \| STRD \| Reconstruction stops at the first ambiguous instruction. The result may be incomplete, but is reliable. \|⏎\|  \| FULL \| Reconstruction continues even if ambiguous instructions are present. The maximum possible number of cycles is assumed for the ambiguous instructions. The result may be complete, but possible not completely reliable. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. pinlist can include pins configured as I, O and IO types. \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. pinlist can include pins configured as I, O and IO types. \|⏎\| reconstr_state \| This parameter returns the state of the reconstruction. \| This parameter returns the state of the reconstruction. \|⏎\|  \| INVL \| If INVL is returned, no valid cycles exist. This occurs if you have hit BREAK before any vectors are generated. \|⏎\|  \| PRED \| If PRED is returned, all returned values are predicted (a change to the setup has been made, but a test has not yet been executed). \|⏎\|  \| REAL \| If REAL is returned, all values given are values from the last executed test. \|⏎\| reconstr_oper \| OKAY \| The reconstruction was successful, no problems encountered. \|⏎\|  \| MMRV \| Multiple Matchloop Restriction Violation:<br><br>The reconstruction stopped at the first match loop because there are multiple match loops in the pattern, and the match loop execution counter, which is needed for the reconstruction, can only hold the count from the last match loop execution. \|⏎\|  \| JMPE \| The reconstruction stopped at a jump on error instruction because execution path of the sequencer is unknown (for example, whether the jump was taken or not). \|⏎\|  \| RETC \| The reconstruction stopped at a return conditional instruction because execution path of the sequencer is unknown (for example, whether the jump was taken or not).<br><br>Note If you use the LMAP error map mode, [FLBL](<02.06.02.02.123_FLBL__-__Failing_LaBeLs.md>) ignores the RETC clearing flag for failures. \|⏎\|  \| ABRT \| The pattern was empty or there was an unspecified problem during the reconstruction. \|⏎\| start_cycle \| The start cycle is an integer which identifies the first cycle for which results exist. \| The start cycle is an integer which identifies the first cycle for which results exist. \|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This parameter is an integer which defines the total number of valid cycles. \| This parameter is an integer which defines the total number of valid cycles. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98861.htm`。` |

| 104 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.336_WFEX__-__WaveForm_EXpected_data.md` | `98873.htm` | #0 | 39×3 | 68 | 49 | `\| start_cycle \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle for which information is to be returned. The scanning method and the range of values this parameter can take depend on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode, enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis mode, enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for stop-on-error tests. \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle for which information is to be returned. The scanning method and the range of values this parameter can take depend on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode, enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis mode, enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for stop-on-error tests. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles for which information is to be returned. The range for this parameter is 0 to 2^48.<br><br>If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles for which information is to be returned. The range for this parameter is 0 to 2^48.<br><br>If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \|⏎\| timing_set \| This parameter is an integer that represents the timing set used in the given cycle range. \| This parameter is an integer that represents the timing set used in the given cycle range. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. You can use the @ character to read the error data for all pins. \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. You can use the @ character to read the error data for all pins. \|⏎\| err_type \| This parameter is always of the type NOER since this query only reports expected data. \| This parameter is always of the type NOER since this query only reports expected data. \|⏎\| err_type \| NOER \| Error information is not available. The reported range lies outside the range covered by the Error Map. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| Edge action data in binary format. This parameter reports the length of data (in bytes) being transferred, followed by the reconstructed data. The data is returned for each pin separately. For each cycle of the reported cycle range 6 bytes are returned. Each byte of reconstructed data is to be decoded as follows: \| Edge action data in binary format. This parameter reports the length of data (in bytes) being transferred, followed by the reconstructed data. The data is returned for each pin separately. For each cycle of the reported cycle range 6 bytes are returned. Each byte of reconstructed data is to be decoded as follows: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 7: Error flag. \| bit 7: Error flag. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| No error on receive edge, or a drive / unused edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Cannot occur since only expected data are reported. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 6, 5: Data parameter \| bits 6, 5: Data parameter \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Fixed value \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Data parameter 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Data parameter 2 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 4: Edge direction \| bit 4: Edge direction \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| Drive / tri-state edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Receive edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Edge action \| bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Edge action \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| If bit 4 is a 0, then drive / tri-state data: \| If bit 4 is a 0, then drive / tri-state data: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 3, 2: Tri-state data \| bits 3, 2: Tri-state data \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Tri-state off \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Tri-state on \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 1, 0: Drive data \| bits 1, 0: Drive data \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Drive 0 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Drive 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| If bit 4 is a 1, then expected data: \| If bit 4 is a 1, then expected data: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 3: Compare type \| bit 3: Compare type \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| Edge compare \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Window compare \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 2, 1, 0: Compare method \| bits 2, 1, 0: Compare method \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 010 \| Compare to 0 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 011 \| Compare to 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 001 \| Compare to intermediate \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 100 \| Compare to mask \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 101 \| Compare to unstable \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 000 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 111 \| Window close \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98873.htm`。` |

The scanning method and the range of values this parameter can take depend on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode, enter a positive value between 0 and 248. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis mode, enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles for which information is to be returned. The range for this parameter is 0 to 2^48.<br><br>If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles for which information is to be returned. The range for this parameter is 0 to 2^48.<br><br>If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \|⏎\| timing_set \| This parameter is an integer that represents the timing set used in the given cycle range. \| This parameter is an integer that represents the timing set used in the given cycle range. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. You can use the @ character to read the error data for all pins. \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. The reported range lies outside the range covered by the Error Map. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| Edge action data in binary format. This parameter reports the length of data (in bytes) being transferred, followed by the reconstructed data. The data is returned for each pin separately. For each cycle of the reported cycle range 6 bytes are returned. Each of these bytes has to be decoded as follows: \| Edge action data in binary format. This parameter reports the length of data (in bytes) being transferred, followed by the reconstructed data. The data is returned for each pin separately. For each cycle of the reported cycle range 6 bytes are returned. (The system returns this only if err_type is ERON). In case of a failing window compare, both the window open edge and the window close edge are marked. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 6, 5: Data parameter \| bits 6, 5: Data parameter \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Fixed value \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Data parameter 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Data parameter 2 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 4: Edge direction \| bit 4: Edge direction \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| Drive / tri-state edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Receive edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Edge action \| bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Edge action \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| If bit 4 is a 0, then drive / tri-state data: \| If bit 4 is a 0, then drive / tri-state data: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 3, 2: Tri-state data \| bits 3, 2: Tri-state data \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Tri-state off \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Tri-state on \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 1, 0: Drive data \| bits 1, 0: Drive data \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Drive 0 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Drive 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| If bit 4 is a 1, then receive data: \| If bit 4 is a 1, then receive data: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 3: Compare type \| bit 3: Compare type \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| Edge compare \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Window compare \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 2, 1, 0: Compare method \| bits 2, 1, 0: Compare method \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 010 \| Compare to 0 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 011 \| Compare to 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 001 \| Compare to intermediate \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 100 \| Compare to mask \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 101 \| Compare to unstable \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 000 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 110 \| Reconstruction not possible \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 111 \| Window close \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98874.htm`。` |

| 122 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.79_DMAS__DMAS__-__Define_Memory_Area_Size.md` | `98566.htm` | #0 | 29×3 | 71 | 16 | `\| area \| The area parameter specifies the type of data for which a memory area is to be set or read. It can be one of the following: \| The area parameter specifies the type of data for which a memory area is to be set or read. It can be one of the following: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\|  \| PARA \| Only the DMAS? PARA (PARAllel vector area) query is supported. It returns the current end of the vector areas for the different memory types. Note that in case of a multiport setup, values are only returned if the PER_PORT option is explicitly set as the third parameter. \|⏎\|  \| PPSQ \| Only the DMAS? PPSQ (Per Pin SeQuencer subroutine) query is supported. It returns the space currently used for per pin sequencer subroutines. It can only be applied to the default port @. \|⏎\|  \| RSLT \| The DMAS? RSLT(ReSuLT area) query returns how much memory is available for result data (Error Map in the external memory or digital capture data). \|⏎\|  \| SAVE \| The DMAS? SAVE query returns the size of the currently used memory for the area types PARA, PPSQ, and SQPG.<br><br>Note that in case of a multiport setup, PARA values are only returned if the PER_PORT option is set explicitly as the third parameter. \|⏎\|  \| SQPG \| The SQPG (Sequencer ProGramming area) option allocates memory for a number of sequencer instructions. The allocated command lines are filled with NOP instructions.<br><br>The DMAS? SQPG query returns the current end of the sequencer areas for the specified ports. \|⏎\|  \| MFRE \| The MFRE (Memory Free RangE) option can only be used in the query. It reports the actual maximum number of vectors that can be added to each of the specified ports, pins or channels. \|⏎\|  \| MFRP \| The MFRP (Memory Free Range per Pool) option can only be used in the query. It reports the free memory space for the specified ports from the pool (test processor, channel group) perspective. \|⏎\|  \| ALL \| The ALL (ALL areas) option can only be used in a query. It returns the size of the currently used memory for the area types PARA, PPSQ, and SQPG, and in addition also the space that is free for result data (area type RSLT).<br><br>Note that in case of a multiport setup, PARA values are only returned if the PER_PORT option is set explicitly as the third parameter. \|⏎\| size \| In the command, the size parameter defines the number of sequencer instructions for which memory is to be allocated on the specified port (area type SQPG). One sequencer instruction requires the space of 8 vectors. Enter a number of instructions between 0 and MAX_FREE_MEM/ 8 vectors.<br><br>In the query replies, this parameter either specifies the used space (for example, with area type PARA) or the available free space (with area types RSLT, MFRE, and MFRP). \| In the command, the size parameter defines the number of sequencer instructions for which memory is to be allocated on the specified port (area type SQPG). One sequencer instruction requires the space of 8 vectors. Enter a number of instructions between 0 and MAX_FREE_MEM/ 8 vectors.<br><br>In the query replies, this parameter either specifies the used space (for example, with area type PARA) or the available free space (with area types RSLT, MFRE, and MFRP). \|⏎\| size_1 \| This query reply parameter specifies the amount of free vector memory at the end of a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \| This query reply parameter specifies the amount of free vector memory at the end of a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \|⏎\| size_2 \| This query reply parameter specifies the largest block of free vector memory on a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \| This query reply parameter specifies the largest block of free vector memory on a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \|⏎\| size_3 \| This query reply parameter specifies the total amount of free vector memory on a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \| This query reply parameter specifies the total amount of free vector memory on a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \|⏎\| pool_size \| This query reply parameter specifies the total amount of free vector memory on a pool (channel group, test processor) which shares a channel with a specified port for the PER_POOL option of MFRP. \| This query reply parameter specifies the total amount of free vector memory on a pool (channel group, test processor) which shares a channel with a specified port for the PER_POOL option of MFRP. \|⏎\| memory \| Specifies the type of memory which is acted upon: \| Specifies the type of memory which is acted upon: \|⏎\|  \| SM \| Sequencer memory. This is the default. \|⏎\|  \| SHMEM \| A special kind of SM for memory sharing in multisite setups. For details see [Selecting the memory type](<02.06.07.01.04.01_Selecting_the_memory_type.md>). \|⏎\|  \| HSER \| A special kind of SM for patterns used in SmartLoop testing. For details see [Defining a compliance pattern for pattern‑based SmartLoop testing](<02.06.17.04.01_Defining_a_compliance_pattern_for_pattern__ased_SmartLoop_testing.md>). \|⏎\|  \| SMEXT \| A special kind of memory for vectors, used only when himem is enabled in the .technology file. See [Technology file for a device - himem](<../../01_Getting_started/01.01_SmarTest_Work_Center/01.01.07.02.01.02_Technology_file_for_a_device.md#_49363__row_himem>) for details. This option can only be used in the query with MFRE or MFRP. \|⏎\|  \| ALL \| All types of memory. This option can only be used in the query. \|⏎\| PER_PORT \| This query option requires the answer to be given per port. This is the default.<br><br>Note however that this option must be specified for area type PARA; otherwise DMAS? will return no answer. This also applies to the PARA values for area types SAVE and ALL. \| This query option requires the answer to be given per port. This is the default.<br><br>Note however that this option must be specified for area type PARA; otherwise DMAS? will return no answer. This also applies to the PARA values for area types SAVE and ALL. \|⏎\| PER_PIN \| This query option is only supported by area types MFRE and RSLT, Its point is that the amount of free vector space or result space can be different for different pins of the same port. \| This query option is only supported by area types MFRE and RSLT, Its point is that the amount of free vector space or result space can be different for different pins of the same port. \|⏎\| PER_CHANNEL \| This option is only supported by the MFRE and the MFRP area types. With area type MFRE, it causes DMAS? to return three values for each specified channel in the following order:<br><br>- size_1: The amount of memory that is available at the end of the vector memory<br>- size_2: The size of the largest free vector memory block<br>- size_3: The sum of all free blocks<br><br>With area type MFRP, the same three values are returned, but for all channels of any pool that supplies channels to one of the specified ports. \| This option is only supported by the MFRE and the MFRP area types. With area type MFRE, it causes DMAS? to return three values for each specified channel in the following order:<br><br>- size_1: The amount of memory that is available at the end of the vector memory<br>- size_2: The size of the largest free vector memory block<br>- size_3: The sum of all free blocks<br><br>With area type MFRP, the same three values are returned, but for all channels of any pool that supplies channels to one of the specified ports. \|⏎\| PER_POOL \| This option is only supported by the MFRP area type. It causes DMAS? to return the total free space of all pools (channel groups, test processors) that supply channels for the specified ports. \| This option is only supported by the MFRP area type. It causes DMAS? to return the total free space of all pools (channel groups, test processors) that supply channels for the specified ports. \|⏎\| port \| The port for which areas are to be defined or for which information is requested. The default port, to which all pins belong, is denoted by the @ character. \| The port for which areas are to be defined or for which information is requested. The default port, to which all pins belong, is denoted by the @ character. \|⏎\| port_list \| The ports for which information is to be retrieved. \| The ports for which information is to be retrieved. \|⏎\| pin, pin_list \| The pins for which information is requested or returned. \| The pins for which information is requested or returned. \|⏎\| channel, channel_list \| The channels for which information is requested or returned. \| The channels for which information is requested or returned. \|⏎\| pool_channels \| The channels of the pool to which the channels of those ports belong that are specified in the query. \| The channels of the pool to which the channels of those ports belong that are specified in the query. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98566.htm`。` |

| 125 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.88_DRLS__-__DRive_Level_Sensitivity.md` | `98576.htm` | #0 | 15×3 | 30 | 15 | `\| test \| This parameter is one of the following: \| This parameter is one of the following: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| test \| H \| This parameter must be entered to determine the high input voltage threshold (VIH,min). VIH,min is the minimum input level which is recognized as logic 1 by the DUT. \|⏎\| test \| L \| This parameter must be entered to determine the low input voltage threshold (VIL,max). VIL,max is the maximum input level which is recognized as logic 0 by the DUT. \|⏎\| pass_value \| For a simple pass or fail test (limit parameter omitted), the pass value defines the expected pass level (in mV) for the test. For value measurements, this parameter defines the start value of the measurement window; set it to a value that ensures a pass result for the functional test. pass_value must be in the range of the drive level in the channel board (see [DRLV, DRLV? - (DRive LeVel)](<02.06.02.02.89_DRLV__DRLV__-__DRive_LeVel.md>)). \| For a simple pass or fail test (limit parameter omitted), the pass value defines the expected pass level (in mV) for the test. For value measurements, this parameter defines the start value of the measurement window; set it to a value that ensures a pass result for the functional test. pass_value must be in the range of the drive level in the channel board (see [DRLV, DRLV? - (DRive LeVel)](<02.06.02.02.89_DRLV__DRLV__-__DRive_LeVel.md>)). \|⏎\| limit \| The limit parameter is an integer which defines the upper (for a low level search) and lower (for a high level search) limits for the measurement window of a value measurement. The limit must be specified in mV. This parameter must be omitted if only a pass/fail result for the test is required. The range limitations for this parameter are as follows:<br><br>- when checking the high threshold, the limit parameter must be between the minimum drive level of the channel board (see [DRLV, DRLV? - (DRive LeVel](<02.06.02.02.89_DRLV__DRLV__-__DRive_LeVel.md>)) and the specified pass value;<br>- when checking the low level threshold, the limit parameter must be between the specified pass value and the maximum drive level of the channel board. \| The limit parameter is an integer which defines the upper (for a low level search) and lower (for a high level search) limits for the measurement window of a value measurement. The limit must be specified in mV. This parameter must be omitted if only a pass/fail result for the test is required. The range limitations for this parameter are as follows:<br><br>- when checking the high threshold, the limit parameter must be between the minimum drive level of the channel board (see [DRLV, DRLV? - (DRive LeVel](<02.06.02.02.89_DRLV__DRLV__-__DRive_LeVel.md>)) and the specified pass value;<br>- when checking the low level threshold, the limit parameter must be between the specified pass value and the maximum drive level of the channel board. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The name of the pin or pins that you want to test. This parameter may also include groups previously created using the DFGP command. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. pinlist must only contain pins configured as inputs. \| The name of the pin or pins that you want to test. This parameter may also include groups previously created using the DFGP command. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. pinlist must only contain pins configured as inputs. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| This reply parameter is one of the following: \| This reply parameter is one of the following: \|⏎\| value_specifier \| LT \| (less than) is returned if a VIL,max measurement was performed and already the first test (with the pass_value parameter) failed. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| LE \| (less than or equal to) is returned if a VIH,min measurement was performed and all the functional tests performed within the measurement window passed. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| EQ \| (equal to) is returned if a value measurement was performed and the first failed functional test occurred within the measurement window. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| GE \| (greater than or equal to) is returned if a VIL,max measurement was performed and all tests performed within the measurement window passed. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| GT \| (greater than) is returned if a VIH,min measurement has been performed and the first functional test performed within the measurement window failed. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| P \| This indicates that the functional test performed passed. This is only returned if the limit parameter is omitted. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| F \| This indicates that the functional test performed failed. This is only returned if the limit parameter is omitted. \|⏎\| result_value \| The result value is the level (in mV) of the last passed test. \| The result value is the level (in mV) of the last passed test. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98576.htm`。` |
| 126 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.97_EAEX__-__Edge_Action_EXpected_data.md` | `98581.htm` | #0 | 9×3 | 18 | 9 | `\| start_cycle \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle for which information is to be returned. The scanning method and the range of values this parameter can take depend on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode, enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis mode, enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle for which information is to be returned. The scanning method and the range of values this parameter can take depend on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode, enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis mode, enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles for which information is to be returned.<br><br>If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles for which information is to be returned.<br><br>If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \|⏎\| timing_set \| This parameter is an integer that represents the timing set used in the given cycle range. If the timing set was changed within the requested cycle range, more than one answer is returned. \| This parameter is an integer that represents the timing set used in the given cycle range. If the timing set was changed within the requested cycle range, more than one answer is returned. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. You can use the @ character to read the edge actions for all pins. \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. You can use the @ character to read the edge actions for all pins. \|⏎\| err_type \| This parameter is optional and specifies what type of error information is available. It is always NOER since this query only reports expected data. \| This parameter is optional and specifies what type of error information is available. It is always NOER since this query only reports expected data. \|⏎\| err_type \| NOER \| Error information is not available. The reported range lies outside the range covered by the Error Map. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| Edge action data in binary format. This parameter reports the length of data (in bytes) being transferred, followed by the reconstructed data. The data is returned for each pin separately. Edges without action are not reported. \| Edge action data in binary format. This parameter reports the length of data (in bytes) being transferred, followed by the reconstructed data. The data is returned for each pin separately. Edges without action are not reported. \|⏎\|  \| The data is to be decoded as follows:<br><br>Each cycle of the reported cycle range is preceded by a two byte header information:<br><br>- First byte, bits 7, 6, 5, 4: Always zero, reserved for future use.<br>- First byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Always 1: number of waveforms contained in the device cycle.<br>- Second byte: Number of edge actions following (0 - 140) \| The data is to be decoded as follows:<br><br>Each cycle of the reported cycle range is preceded by a two byte header information:<br><br>- First byte, bits 7, 6, 5, 4: Always zero, reserved for future use.<br>- First byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Always 1: number of waveforms contained in the device cycle.<br>- Second byte: Number of edge actions following (0 - 140) \|⏎\|  \| Each of the following edge actions is described by two bytes:<br><br>- First byte, bit 7: Channel selector for differential pins. Displays the settings of the actual enabled DPS set. \|⏎\| dps_set_id \| Needed for equation-based setup.<br><br>This parameter is derived as follows:<br><br>`100000*equation_set_no + 1000*specset_no+1` \| Needed for equation-based setup.<br><br>This parameter is derived as follows:<br><br>`100000*equation_set_no + 1000*specset_no+1` \|⏎\| voltage \| The optional parameter voltage defines the output voltage (in V) of the DPS. Applicable value depends on the DPS type. \| The optional parameter voltage defines the output voltage (in V) of the DPS. Applicable value depends on the DPS type. \|⏎\| source_current \| The optional parameter defines the source current limit (ilimit_source) in [A]. The applicable value depends on the DPS type.<br><br>The parameter defines the source and the sink current limit if both values are equal.<br><br>The parameter defines only the source current limit if the sink_current parameter is used. \| The optional parameter defines the source current limit (ilimit_source) in [A]. The applicable value depends on the DPS type.<br><br>The parameter defines the source and the sink current limit if both values are equal.<br><br>The parameter defines only the source current limit if the sink_current parameter is used. \|⏎\| sink_current \| The extra parameter defines the sink current limit (ilimit_sink) in [A].<br><br>The parameter is required only if the source and sink current limits are set to different values. This parameter defines then the sink current limit while the source current limit is defined by the above source_current parameter.<br><br>The parameter is not required if the current limits are programmed symmetrically to the same (absolute) value. Both, the source and the sink value are then defined by the source_current parameter.<br><br>The query answer shows the sink_current parameter only if the programmed sink current limit is not symmetrical to the source current limit. \| The extra parameter defines the sink current limit (ilimit_sink) in [A].<br><br>The parameter is required only if the source and sink current limits are set to different values. This parameter defines then the sink current limit while the source current limit is defined by the above source_current parameter.<br><br>The parameter is not required if the current limits are programmed symmetrically to the same (absolute) value. Both, the source and the sink value are then defined by the source_current parameter.<br><br>The query answer shows the sink_current parameter only if the programmed sink current limit is not symmetrical to the source current limit. \|⏎\| impedance \| The optional parameter sets the DPS output impedance. You can select:<br><br>- LOZ sets the DPS output impedance to low when the test system is in the disconnect state. The current limit remains at the same level as in the active state, while the force voltage is set to 0 V.<br>- HIZ sets the DPS output to high impedance when the test system is in the disconnect state. This sets the channel to the minimum possible current limit. HIZ is the default state for DPS-slot types. If it is applied to DC Scale cards, HIZ_R is used instead of HIZ.<br>- HIZ_R sets the DPS output to high impedance by switching the output relay off when the test system is in the disconnect state.<br><br>Note that relay switching is slower than with HIZ.<br><br>- LOZ_HIZ combines LOZ with HIZ. First, the DPS is switched to 0 V with an unchanged current limit (just like in LOZ mode). After the output voltage has reached 0 V (±50 mV) or after a timeout of 1 second, the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This combines the advantages of both modes. A capacitor on the load board is quickly discharged and then the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This is safer when changing the DUT or DUT board after a disconnect.<br>- LOZ_HIZ_R combines LOZ with HIZ_R. First, the DPS is switched to 0 V with an unchanged current limit (just like in LOZ mode). After the output voltage has reached 0 V (±50mV) or after a timeout of 1 second, the DPS pin is switched to open. This combines the advantages of both modes. A capacitor on the DUT board is quickly discharged and then the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This is safer when changing the DUT or the DUT board after a disconnect. \| The optional parameter sets the DPS output impedance. You can select:<br><br>- LOZ sets the DPS output impedance to low when the test system is in the disconnect state. The current limit remains at the same level as in the active state, while the force voltage is set to 0 V.<br>- HIZ sets the DPS output to high impedance when the test system is in the disconnect state. This sets the channel to the minimum possible current limit. HIZ is the default state for DPS-slot types. If it is applied to DC Scale cards, HIZ_R is used instead of HIZ.<br>- HIZ_R sets the DPS output to high impedance by switching the output relay off when the test system is in the disconnect state.<br><br>Note that relay switching is slower than with HIZ.<br><br>- LOZ_HIZ combines LOZ with HIZ. First, the DPS is switched to 0 V with an unchanged current limit (just like in LOZ mode). After the output voltage has reached 0 V (±50 mV) or after a timeout of 1 second, the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This combines the advantages of both modes. A capacitor on the load board is quickly discharged and then the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This is safer when changing the DUT or DUT board after a disconnect.<br>- LOZ_HIZ_R combines LOZ with HIZ_R. First, the DPS is switched to 0 V with an unchanged current limit (just like in LOZ mode). After the output voltage has reached 0 V (±50mV) or after a timeout of 1 second, the DPS pin is switched to open. This combines the advantages of both modes. A capacitor on the DUT board is quickly discharged and then the DPS pin is switched to high impedance. This is safer when changing the DUT or the DUT board after a disconnect. \|⏎\| setup_time \| The optional parameter is an integer which specifies the settling time (in ms) of the DPS. All DPS's are synchronized so that they finish their settling periods at the same point in time. This is the point when the system is switched to the connect state. If there are, for example, two DPS's, one with a settling time of 10 ms, and the other with a settling time of 4 ms, then the second DPS will be started 6 ms after the first:<br><br>![Synchronizing Different DPS Settling Times](<../../../../raw/93235.png>)<br><br>The range is from -2147483647 up to 2147483646 ms.<br><br>The default value is 0.Note setup_time has a resolution of ms (floating point values are rounded to nearest integer). \| The optional parameter is an integer which specifies the settling time (in ms) of the DPS. All DPS's are synchronized so that they finish their settling periods at the same point in time. This is the point when the system is switched to the connect state. If there are, for example, two DPS's, one with a settling time of 10 ms, and the other with a settling time of 4 ms, then the second DPS will be started 6 ms after the first:<br><br>![Synchronizing Different DPS Settling Times](<../../../../raw/93235.png>)<br><br>The range is from -2147483647 up to 2147483646 ms.<br><br>The default value is 0.Note setup_time has a resolution of ms (floating point values are rounded to nearest integer). \|⏎\| pinlist \| pinlist must contain the names of pins previously defined by the [DFPS](<02.06.02.02.65_DFPS__DFPS__-__DeFine_Power_Supply.md>) command. If more than one pin is specified, the names must be separated by commas. The character @ can be used to set the levels for all DPS pins. \| pinlist must contain the names of pins previously defined by the [DFPS](<02.06.02.02.65_DFPS__DFPS__-__DeFine_Power_Supply.md>) command. If more than one pin is specified, the names must be separated by commas. | 148 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.05.02.21_SLDO_-__Switch_Level_and_Dps_Order.md` | `98735_2.htm` | #0 | 9×3 | 18 | 9 | `\| order \| This parameter sets the switching order mode: \| This parameter sets the switching order mode: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| order \| FDSL \| First DPS, second levels. If this parameter is specified, the DPS voltage values are always switched before the voltage values of the levels. \|⏎\| order \| CVDL \| There are three possibilities:<br><br>1. If DPS voltage values are increasing or stable, DPS voltages are switched before level voltages.<br><br>2. If DPS voltage values are decreasing or stable, level voltages are switched before DPS voltages.<br><br>3. If the voltages of some DPS pins are decreasing while the voltages of others are increasing (ambiguous case), DPS voltages are always switched first. \|⏎\| report \| This parameter sets the report mode, which can be one of the following: \| This parameter sets the report mode, which can be one of the following: \|⏎\| report \| ALL \| Reports a change in the switching order from DPS voltages (first) to level voltages (first) and vice versa. \|⏎\| report \| FLSD \| First levels, second DPS. Reports a change in the switching order to switching level voltages first. \|⏎\| report \| AMBI \| Ambiguous case. Reports an ambiguous case. \|⏎\| report \| NONE \| Nothing is reported. This is the default report mode. \|⏎\| operation \| This is the mode of operation. SAVE returns values for everything that can be set. \| This is the mode of operation. SAVE returns values for everything that can be set. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98735_2.htm`。` |

Note that you cannot retrieve the pin specific values with SVLR?.<br><br>Note You can use the pinlist parameter to modify the value of a spec variable of an individual pin, temporarily. | 153 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.06.02.04_CDCP__-__Cycle_Device_Cycle_Parameter.md` | `98499_2.htm` | #0 | 23×3 | 46 | 23 | `\| start_cycle \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle to be analyzed. The range of values for this parameter depends on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle to be analyzed. The range of values for this parameter depends on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| cycle \| In the query, the consecutive number of the displayed cycle. \| In the query, the consecutive number of the displayed cycle. \|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles to be analyzed. The range for this parameter is 0 to 1000. \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles to be analyzed. The range for this parameter is 0 to 1000. \|⏎\| strt_disp \| This parameter defines the offset of your range of interest from start_cycle. The actual display starts with the last device cycle that does not begin before start_cycle and does not end before start_cycle + strt_disp.<br><br>The purpose of this construction is to enable a correct device cycle reconstruction even if the first cycle you are interested in does not lie at a device cycle boundary (a device cycle can generate up to 16 cycles). \| This parameter defines the offset of your range of interest from start_cycle. The actual display starts with the last device cycle that does not begin before start_cycle and does not end before start_cycle + strt_disp.<br><br>The purpose of this construction is to enable a correct device cycle reconstruction even if the first cycle you are interested in does not lie at a device cycle boundary (a device cycle can generate up to 16 cycles). \|⏎\| disp_length \| Specifies the display length in cycles. This parameter determines the end of your range of interest. The actual display stops with the last device cycle that starts before start_cycle + strt_disp + disp_length.<br><br>If this parameter is larger than no_of_cycles, it is ignored. \| Specifies the display length in cycles. This parameter determines the end of your range of interest. The actual display stops with the last device cycle that starts before start_cycle + strt_disp + disp_length.<br><br>If this parameter is larger than no_of_cycles, it is ignored. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The pin name or names for which a device cycle data transfer is to be made. If more than one pin is required, the names must be separated with a comma. The @ character can be used to download data for all configured pins. \| The pin name or names for which a device cycle data transfer is to be made. If more than one pin is required, the names must be separated with a comma. The @ character can be used to download data for all configured pins. \|⏎\| dev_cyc_name \| A reply parameter, giving the name of the reconstructed device cycle. \| A reply parameter, giving the name of the reconstructed device cycle. \|⏎\| dev_cyc_len \| A reply parameter, giving the length of the reconstructed device cycle. For SmarTest 7.10 this is always 1. \| A reply parameter, giving the length of the reconstructed device cycle. For SmarTest 7.10 this is always 1. \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| Drive Data parameters. They contain values for both drive data and tri-state. The values the system returns are: \| Drive Data parameters. They contain values for both drive data and tri-state. The values the system returns are: \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F00 \| drive 0, tri-state off \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F10 \| drive 1, tri-state off \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F0Z \| drive 0, tri-state on \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F1Z \| drive 1, tri-state on \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| N \| Not used. Returned if no drive data parameter is set to a drive and tri-state value. \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| UC \| Unclear. The system may return this value to indicate that the interpretation of the device cycle for that parameter is unclear, that is, the physical waveforms are inconsistent for the device cycle. \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| Expected Data Parameters. The values the system returns are: \| Expected Data Parameters. The values the system returns are: \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| F0 \| compare to 0 \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| F1 \| compare to 1 \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FI \| compare to intermediate \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FX \| compare to mask \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FU \| compare to unstable \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| N \| Not used. Returned if no expected data parameter is set to a compare value. \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| UC \| Unclear. The system may return this value to indicate that the interpretation of the device cycle for that parameter is unclear, that is, the physical waveforms are inconsistent for the device cycle. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98499_2.htm`。` |

| 169 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.07.02.02_APTS_-__APpend_TeSt.md` | `98316_2.htm` | #0 | 10×3 | 20 | 10 | `\| label \| Defines the name of the new label. Enter an alphanumeric string in double quotes containing 1 to 128 characters. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| type \| The label type. It has the following options: \| The label type. It has the following options: \|⏎\| type \| MAIN \| The MAIN program. You can make calls to both types of subroutine (SJMP and SVEC), execute commands and sequencer instructions, generate vectors and use SSRC and SRCV instructions. \|⏎\| type \| SVEC \| Vector subroutine. A subroutine where you can execute commands and sequencer instructions and generate vectors. You can nest SVEC subroutines up to 7 levels deep. \|⏎\| type \| DSRC \| The digital source label. A type of label used only by SSRC and SRCV (see [SQPG, SQPG? - (SeQuencer ProGramming)](<02.06.02.02.237_SQPG__SQPG__-__SeQuencer_ProGramming.md>)). A DSRC label is not executable. The contents of the DSRC label are determined by the DVCU command. See [DVCU, DVCU? - (Define Vector variable Configuration Usage)](<02.06.02.02.96_DVCU__DVCU__-__Define_Vector_variable_Configuration_Usage.md>) \|⏎\| base_wf_set \| Defines a base waveform set. | 171 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.07.02.13_DMAS__DMAS__-__Define_Memory_Area_Size.md` | `98566_2.htm` | #0 | 29×3 | 71 | 16 | `\| area \| The area parameter specifies the type of data for which a memory area is to be set or read. It can be one of the following: \| The area parameter specifies the type of data for which a memory area is to be set or read. It can be one of the following: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\|  \| PARA \| Only the DMAS? PARA (PARAllel vector area) query is supported. It returns the current end of the vector areas for the different memory types. Note that in case of a multiport setup, values are only returned if the PER_PORT option is explicitly set as the third parameter. \|⏎\|  \| PPSQ \| Only the DMAS? PPSQ (Per Pin SeQuencer subroutine) query is supported. It returns the space currently used for per pin sequencer subroutines. It can only be applied to the default port @. \|⏎\|  \| RSLT \| The DMAS? RSLT(ReSuLT area) query returns how much memory is available for result data (Error Map in the external memory or digital capture data). \|⏎\|  \| SAVE \| The DMAS? SAVE query returns the size of the currently used memory for the area types PARA, PPSQ, and SQPG.<br><br>Note that in case of a multiport setup, PARA values are only returned if the PER_PORT option is set explicitly as the third parameter. \|⏎\|  \| SQPG \| The SQPG (Sequencer ProGramming area) option allocates memory for a number of sequencer instructions. The allocated command lines are filled with NOP instructions.<br><br>The DMAS? SQPG query returns the current end of the sequencer areas for the specified ports. \|⏎\|  \| MFRE \| The MFRE (Memory Free RangE) option can only be used in the query. It reports the actual maximum number of vectors that can be added to each of the specified ports, pins or channels. \|⏎\|  \| MFRP \| The MFRP (Memory Free Range per Pool) option can only be used in the query. It reports the free memory space for the specified ports from the pool (test processor, channel group) perspective. \|⏎\|  \| ALL \| The ALL (ALL areas) option can only be used in a query. It returns the size of the currently used memory for the area types PARA, PPSQ, and SQPG, and in addition also the space that is free for result data (area type RSLT).<br><br>Note that in case of a multiport setup, PARA values are only returned if the PER_PORT option is set explicitly as the third parameter. \|⏎\| size \| In the command, the size parameter defines the number of sequencer instructions for which memory is to be allocated on the specified port (area type SQPG). One sequencer instruction requires the space of 8 vectors. Enter a number of instructions between 0 and MAX_FREE_MEM/ 8 vectors.<br><br>In the query replies, this parameter either specifies the used space (for example, with area type PARA) or the available free space (with area types RSLT, MFRE, and MFRP). \| In the command, the size parameter defines the number of sequencer instructions for which memory is to be allocated on the specified port (area type SQPG). One sequencer instruction requires the space of 8 vectors. Enter a number of instructions between 0 and MAX_FREE_MEM/ 8 vectors.<br><br>In the query replies, this parameter either specifies the used space (for example, with area type PARA) or the available free space (with area types RSLT, MFRE, and MFRP). \|⏎\| size_1 \| This query reply parameter specifies the amount of free vector memory at the end of a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \| This query reply parameter specifies the amount of free vector memory at the end of a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \|⏎\| size_2 \| This query reply parameter specifies the largest block of free vector memory on a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \| This query reply parameter specifies the largest block of free vector memory on a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \|⏎\| size_3 \| This query reply parameter specifies the total amount of free vector memory on a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \| This query reply parameter specifies the total amount of free vector memory on a specified channel for the PER_CHANNEL option of MFRE and MFRP. \|⏎\| pool_size \| This query reply parameter specifies the total amount of free vector memory on a pool (channel group, test processor) which shares a channel with a specified port for the PER_POOL option of MFRP. \| This query reply parameter specifies the total amount of free vector memory on a pool (channel group, test processor) which shares a channel with a specified port for the PER_POOL option of MFRP. \|⏎\| memory \| Specifies the type of memory which is acted upon: \| Specifies the type of memory which is acted upon: \|⏎\|  \| SM \| Sequencer memory. This is the default. \|⏎\|  \| SHMEM \| A special kind of SM for memory sharing in multisite setups. For details see [Selecting the memory type](<02.06.07.01.04.01_Selecting_the_memory_type.md>). \|⏎\|  \| HSER \| A special kind of SM for patterns used in SmartLoop testing. For details see [Defining a compliance pattern for pattern‑based SmartLoop testing](<02.06.17.04.01_Defining_a_compliance_pattern_for_pattern__ased_SmartLoop_testing.md>). \|⏎\|  \| SMEXT \| A special kind of memory for vectors, used only when himem is enabled in the .technology file. See [Technology file for a device - himem](<../../01_Getting_started/01.01_SmarTest_Work_Center/01.01.07.02.01.02_Technology_file_for_a_device.md#_49363__row_himem>) for details. This option can only be used in the query with MFRE or MFRP. \|⏎\|  \| ALL \| All types of memory. This option can only be used in the query. \|⏎\| PER_PORT \| This query option requires the answer to be given per port. This is the default.<br><br>Note however that this option must be specified for area type PARA; otherwise DMAS? will return no answer. This also applies to the PARA values for area types SAVE and ALL. \| This query option requires the answer to be given per port. This is the default.<br><br>Note however that this option must be specified for area type PARA; otherwise DMAS? will return no answer. This also applies to the PARA values for area types SAVE and ALL. \|⏎\| PER_PIN \| This query option is only supported by area types MFRE and RSLT, Its point is that the amount of free vector space or result space can be different for different pins of the same port. \| This query option is only supported by area types MFRE and RSLT, Its point is that the amount of free vector space or result space can be different for different pins of the same port. \|⏎\| PER_CHANNEL \| This option is only supported by the MFRE and the MFRP area types. With area type MFRE, it causes DMAS? to return three values for each specified channel in the following order:<br><br>- size_1: The amount of memory that is available at the end of the vector memory<br>- size_2: The size of the largest free vector memory block<br>- size_3: The sum of all free blocks<br><br>With area type MFRP, the same three values are returned, but for all channels of any pool that supplies channels to one of the specified ports. \| This option is only supported by the MFRE and the MFRP area types. With area type MFRE, it causes DMAS? to return three values for each specified channel in the following order:<br><br>- size_1: The amount of memory that is available at the end of the vector memory<br>- size_2: The size of the largest free vector memory block<br>- size_3: The sum of all free blocks<br><br>With area type MFRP, the same three values are returned, but for all channels of any pool that supplies channels to one of the specified ports. \|⏎\| PER_POOL \| This option is only supported by the MFRP area type. It causes DMAS? to return the total free space of all pools (channel groups, test processors) that supply channels for the specified ports. \| This option is only supported by the MFRP area type. It causes DMAS? to return the total free space of all pools (channel groups, test processors) that supply channels for the specified ports. \|⏎\| port \| The port for which areas are to be defined or for which information is requested. The default port, to which all pins belong, is denoted by the @ character. \| The port for which areas are to be defined or for which information is requested. The default port, to which all pins belong, is denoted by the @ character. \|⏎\| port_list \| The ports for which information is to be retrieved. \| The ports for which information is to be retrieved. \|⏎\| pin, pin_list \| The pins for which information is requested or returned. \| The pins for which information is requested or returned. \|⏎\| channel, channel_list \| The channels for which information is requested or returned. \| The channels for which information is requested or returned. \|⏎\| pool_channels \| The channels of the pool to which the channels of those ports belong that are specified in the query. \| The channels of the pool to which the channels of those ports belong that are specified in the query. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98566_2.htm`。` |
