| 174 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.07.02.22_RSET_-__ReSET.md` | `98723_4.htm` | #0 | 10×3 | 20 | 10 | `\| specifier \| The specifier can be: \| The specifier can be: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| specifier \| DPS \| Loads the default power supply setup for standard DPSs. \|⏎\| specifier \| FCR \| Loads the default clock routing. \|⏎\| specifier \| LEV \| Loads the default power supply setup for standard DPSs.<br><br>Level: 0 V<br><br>Current: 50 mA<br><br>Off state: minimum current<br><br>Setup time: 0 ms<br><br>This default states are:<br><br>UHC4T<br><br>- Connect state: normal<br>- Level: 0 V<br>- Range: 4 V<br>- Clamp: 4 A<br>- Clamp-Range: 40 A<br>- Disconnect state: HIZ<br>- Voltage drop force: 250 mV<br>- Voltage drop return: 250 mV<br>- Clamp mode: limit (see [UHCM](<02.06.02.02.303_UHCM__UHCM__-__Ultra_High_Current_power_supply_Modes.md>))<br>- DUT capacitor mode: off<br><br>Fast preload: 0 ms \|⏎\| specifier \| TIM \| Loads the default timing setup. The settings are:<br><br>Timing Sets<br><br>- Defined Timing Sets: 1<br>- Allowed Timing Sets: 256<br><br>Waveform Sets<br><br>- Defined Waveform Sets: 1<br>- Allowed Waveform Sets: 16<br><br>Waveforms: not defined<br><br>Breakwaveform: defined<br><br>Events<br><br>- Action: NOP<br>- Timing: 0 ns<br><br>Min Period: 4 ns<br><br>- This is dependent on the test system hardware configuration.<br><br>Device Cycles: not defined<br><br>Default Device Cycle: "" \|⏎\| specifier \| VEC \| Loads the default vector setup. The Vector Memory contains:<br><br>No vectors<br><br>No tests (and therefore no labels)<br><br>No comments<br><br>No sequencer programs (no instructions) \|⏎\| specifier \| ATR \| Loads the default edge attributes.<br><br>Pin attribute: set to 0 \|⏎\| specifier \| PDL \| Loads the default path delay attributes.<br><br>Cable assembly delay attribute: set to 0<br><br>Fixture delay attribute: set to 0<br><br>System attribute: set to 0 \|⏎\| specifier \| CAL \| Loads the default calibration data.<br><br>Level calibration data<br><br>Timing calibration data \|⏎\| specifier \| MTPP \| Resets memory test and per-pin sequencer subroutine data. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98723_4.htm`。` |
| 175 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.07.02.23_SAOF__SAOF__-__Set_Alternating_Operation_Features.md` | `98726_2.htm` | #0 | 7×3 | 14 | 7 | `\| mode \| The operation mode which can take the following values: \| The operation mode which can take the following values: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| mode \| ZERO \| Editing mode. This is the default. \|⏎\| mode \| APP \| This mode serves to append data to the sequencer and vector areas. In order to do that, you must first allocate memory for the respective area with the DMAS command. Now,in editing mode, the DMAS command defines the size of the area (it starts from address 0). If, however, the system is in APP mode, the existing area will be expanded by the size specified in the DMAS command.<br><br>Note that you cannot iterate this step. If you allocate memory in APP mode for a second time, the first expansion will be redefined. In order to further expand the area, you must first issue SAOFAPP again. \|⏎\| mode \| LOAD \| Load vectors. This mode improves performance of a vector download. \|⏎\| mode \| TMLD \| Load timing and/or pin attributes. This mode improves performance of a timing download. \|⏎\| mode \| EGRP \| Edit group definitions. \|⏎\| label \| If you call SAOF with a label name, you can generate a vector file for this label only. \| If you call SAOF with a label name, you can generate a vector file for this label only. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98726_2.htm`。` |
| 176 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.07.02.25_SQGB__SQGB__-__SeQuencer_GloBal.md` | `98746_2.htm` | #0 | 14×3 | 28 | 14 | `\| mode \| Possible values are: \| Possible values are: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| mode \| ACQF \| ACQuire Failure mode. Only failed cycles are recorded, starting after analyze_delay cycles. If analyze_delay is not a multiple of 8, recording starts after the nearest smaller multiple of 8. In this mode the Error Map is called the Acquire Failure Map.<br><br>As the test proceeds the Acquire Failure Map is filled with failure data until the test stops or the map becomes completely full, whichever occurs first. If the map stops recording before the test has finished, the sequencer, which is in standard mode, continues executing the test until the end.<br><br>This mode is only supported for compatibility reasons. Instead, you should use DCGM and DCRP to configure the Error Map. The effect of ACQF is reproduced by:<br><br>DCGM FAIL,STRT,NONE<br><br>DCRP EMAP,<error_map_pos>,<ref_port> \|⏎\| mode \| ACFF \| This mode is functionally identical to ACQF. It is obsolete and should not be used in new applications. \|⏎\| mode \| EL \| EndLess. The sequencer repeats the complete test, including primaries and multiport synchronization, in an endless loop and can only be stopped by Break.<br><br>The analyze_delay parameter is ignored. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented mode.<br><br>This mode is used for oscilloscope tests. The Error Map functions as a rolling buffer. After stopping the sequencer, the Error Map shows the last "buffer-full" of recorded cycles. \|⏎\| mode \| ELPT \| EndLess PaTtern. The sequencer repeats only the user program. This avoids critical holds during measurement.<br><br>In case of multiport testing, ports may run with different frequencies. Using the ELPT mode in this case, can lead to the situation, that for loop counts >1 the synchronization is no longer executed. \|⏎\| mode \| EMPS \| Error Map PoSitioning. The Error Map is in cycle oriented mode, the sequencer in default mode. The analyze_delay parameter specifies the end of the recorded area. If it is not a multiple of 8, the last cycle to be displayed is the largest multiple of 8 that is smaller than analyze_delay. The first cycle to be displayed is determined by the size of the Error Map (8k cycles).<br><br>This mode is only supported for compatibility reasons. Instead, you should use DCGM and DCRP to configure the Error Map. The effect of EMPS is reproduced by:<br><br>DCGM ALL,HALT,NONE<br><br>DCRP EMAP,<error_map_pos>,<ref_port> \|⏎\| mode \| SE \| Stop on Error. The sequencer runs from the beginning of the test and stops when an error is encountered. If no errors occur, the sequencer runs to the end of the test and stops. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM. The analyze_delay parameter is ignored. \|⏎\| mode \| SEEL \| Stop on Error and EndLess. The sequencer repeats the test in an endless loop, unless an error is encountered, whereupon it stops. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode. This mode is used to locate sporadic failures. \|⏎\| mode \| STSQ \| STop SeQuencer. Stops the sequencer after analyze_delay cycles. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM. See also the query [GETC? - (GET Cycles)](<02.06.02.02.131_GETC__-__GET_Cycles.md>) for the analyze_delay count. \|⏎\| mode \| STRD \| In the query reply, this mode is displayed if the current combination of recording mode, as set with DCGM, and interpretation of the Error Map position, as set with DCRP, is different from all the other SQGB modes. For example, the Error Map can be in cycle oriented recording mode, and analyze_delay be interpreted as a start value.<br><br>In the command, this option leaves it to SmarTest to select the appropriate recording mode (cycle oriented or acquire failure), unless the recording mode has been explicitly set with DCGM. \|⏎\| mode \| STSV \| STop Sequencer on Vector. Stops the sequencer at the logical vector number specified in the analyze_delay parameter before executing the vector. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM.<br><br>Note that the STSV option cannot be used if no start label has been defined with SQSL, or if the start label is a burst or multi-port burst label. \|⏎\| analyze_delay \| Sets the analyze delay. This parameter is mandatory for the STSQ and STSV modes. Its interpretation depends on the selected mode parameter. The number must be between 1 (0 for ACQF) and 281,474,976,710,655 = 2^48 - 1 (2^48 - 2 for ACQF).<br><br>If the mode parameter is set to STSV, three cases must be distinguished:<br><br>- If analyze_delay is an existing logical vector number of the actual start label, the sequencer will be stopped at this logical vector before executing the vector.<br>- If analyze_delay specifies the total number of logical vectors (= greatest logical vector number of the test + 1) the sequencer stops at the last valid vector.<br>- If analyze_delay is greater than the total number of logical vectors, an error message is issued. \| Sets the analyze delay. This parameter is mandatory for the STSQ and STSV modes. Its interpretation depends on the selected mode parameter. The number must be between 1 (0 for ACQF) and 281,474,976,710,655 = 2^48 - 1 (2^48 - 2 for ACQF).<br><br>If the mode parameter is set to STSV, three cases must be distinguished:<br><br>- If analyze_delay is an existing logical vector number of the actual start label, the sequencer will be stopped at this logical vector before executing the vector.<br>- If analyze_delay specifies the total number of logical vectors (= greatest logical vector number of the test + 1) the sequencer stops at the last valid vector.<br>- If analyze_delay is greater than the total number of logical vectors, an error message is issued. \|⏎\| ref_port \| This parameter accepts a single port. It must be specified in the following situation:<br><br>- The selected mode requires an analyze delay (but is different from STSV), and<br>- the start label is a multi-port burst.<br><br>It specifies the port which is used for counting the cycles specified with the analyze_delay parameter. When this reference port has completed the specified number of cycles, the sequencers will be stopped, or cycle recording started or stopped, for all ports. Note that the reference port must belong to the current start label (as defined by SQSL). \| This parameter accepts a single port. It must be specified in the following situation:<br><br>- The selected mode requires an analyze delay (but is different from STSV), and<br>- the start label is a multi-port burst.<br><br>It specifies the port which is used for counting the cycles specified with the analyze_delay parameter. When this reference port has completed the specified number of cycles, the sequencers will be stopped, or cycle recording started or stopped, for all ports. Note that the reference port must belong to the current start label (as defined by SQSL). \|⏎\| port_list \| If the current mode requires an analyze delay, you can specify a port list in the query. The reply will give the calculated analyze delays for those ports that are not used as the reference port. These delays are derived from the analyze delay of the reference port and the ratio between the period lengths of the reference port and the other port in the primary timing setup. If you specify @@, SQGB will return the settings for all ports of the current start label. \| If the current mode requires an analyze delay, you can specify a port list in the query. The reply will give the calculated analyze delays for those ports that are not used as the reference port. These delays are derived from the analyze delay of the reference port and the ratio between the period lengths of the reference port and the other port in the primary timing setup. If you specify @@, SQGB will return the settings for all ports of the current start label. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98746_2.htm`。` |

| 178 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.07.02.27_SQLB__SQLB__-__SeQuencer_LaBel_definition.md` | `98748_2.htm` | #0 | 19×3 | 37 | 20 | `\| label \| The label name. It identifies the label which is to be defined for the specified port, or whose definition is to be displayed. Enter a string enclosed in double quotes. The string must have a length of between 1 and 128 alpha-numeric characters.<br><br>If you enter an empty string in the command, the label specified by the start_cmd_no and the stop_cmd_no parameters is deleted. Note, however, that the respective lines of the sequencer area are not deleted.<br><br>See [SmarTest character and number limits](<../../01_Getting_started/01.05_SmarTest_character_and_number_limits/01.05_SmarTest_character_and_number_limits.md#_128029__max_labels_per_port>) for the maximum number of labels per port. \| The label name. It identifies the label which is to be defined for the specified port, or whose definition is to be displayed. Enter a string enclosed in double quotes. The string must have a length of between 1 and 128 alpha-numeric characters.<br><br>If you enter an empty string in the command, the label specified by the start_cmd_no and the stop_cmd_no parameters is deleted. Note, however, that the respective lines of the sequencer area are not deleted.<br><br>See [SmarTest character and number limits](<../../01_Getting_started/01.05_SmarTest_character_and_number_limits/01.05_SmarTest_character_and_number_limits.md#_128029__max_labels_per_port>) for the maximum number of labels per port. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| label \| For the query you can also enter: \| For the query you can also enter: \|⏎\| label \| ALL \| to read the configurations for all labels. \|⏎\| label \| SUR \| (SURround) to obtain configurations for the label selected for editing (in the Pattern Tool) by the TSTL (TeST Label) command, as well as the labels immediately preceding and following it. \|⏎\| label \| FRS \| (FiRSt) to return only the first label in the sequencer area of the specified port.<br><br>If the label you are querying does not exist, the firmware returns nothing. \|⏎\| type \| The label type. This parameter must always be present. It has the following options: \| The label type. This parameter must always be present. It has the following options: \|⏎\| type \| MAIN \| A MAIN program. A MAIN label can be used as a start label (see the [SQSL](<02.06.02.02.240_SQSL__SQSL__-__SeQuencer_Start_Label.md>) command) if it is defined for the default port @. It can call any type of subroutine. It can be called from a burst (type BRST) or multi-port burst label (type MPBU).<br><br>The rudimentary sequencer program that is created for this label type consists of the STVA, STSA, and STOP instructions. \|⏎\| type \| SVEC \| Vector subroutine. A subroutine that cannot serve as a start label. It must be called with the JSUB sequencer instruction from a MAIN label or another SVEC label. You can nest SVEC subroutines up to 7 levels deep.<br><br>The rudimentary sequencer program that is created for this label type consists of the STVA and the RSUB instructions. \|⏎\| type \| BRST \| Burst mode. Defines a burst label. A burst label executes a sequence of MAIN labels in burst mode. Its sequencer program consists of a sequence of CALL instructions for the individual MAIN labels, and is terminated by a BEND instruction. The BEND instruction is created automatically by SQLB. The same MAIN label can be called by several burst labels.<br><br>For more information on the subject and the dependencies that have to be taken into account see [Burst and Multi-Burst Labels](<02.06.07.01.08.05.03_Burst_labels_and_multi-port_burst_labels.md>). \|⏎\| type \| DSRC \| The digital source label. A type of label used only by SSRC and SRCV. A DSRC label is not executable. The contents of DSRC labels are determined by DVCU. See [DVCU, DVCU? - (Define Vector variable Configuration Usage](<02.06.02.02.96_DVCU__DVCU__-__Define_Vector_variable_Configuration_Usage.md>)) for more information. \|⏎\| type \| MPBU \| Multiport burst mode. A burst label that is executed simultaneously for several ports. You must define the multi-port burst label separately for all the required ports; this is the only case where the same label name can be used more than once.<br><br>The structure of a multiport burst is the same as that of a normal burst label. The parameters of the CALL instructions will be different for the same label in the individual ports.<br><br>Note that the ports for which a multiport burst is defined must not have any pins in common. This is not checked by the firmware. \|⏎\| type \| SJMP \| Jump subroutine. This label type is allowed for downward compatibility only. It should not be used in new applications. \|⏎\| type \| ALL \| For the query, you can type ALL to read the configurations for all label types. \|⏎\| start_cmd_no \| The start command number parameter identifies the position within the sequencer area where the label starts.<br><br>Note that the exact meaning of this number depends on the operation mode, as defined with the SAOF command. In standard mode (SAOF ZERO), the command lines are counted from the start of the sequencer area, but in append mode (SAOF APP) they are counted from the start of the expansion of the sequencer area previously defined with DMAS. See example 4, below. \| The start command number parameter identifies the position within the sequencer area where the label starts.<br><br>Note that the exact meaning of this number depends on the operation mode, as defined with the SAOF command. In standard mode (SAOF ZERO), the command lines are counted from the start of the sequencer area, but in append mode (SAOF APP) they are counted from the start of the expansion of the sequencer area previously defined with DMAS. See example 4, below. \|⏎\| stop_cmd_no \| The stop command number parameter identifies the position within the sequencer area where the label stops.<br><br>Note that the exact meaning of this number depends on the operation mode, as defined with the SAOF command. In standard mode (SAOF ZERO), the command lines are counted from the start of the sequencer area, but in append mode (SAOF APP) they are counted from the start of the expansion of the sequencer area previously defined with DMAS. See example 4, below. \| The stop command number parameter identifies the position within the sequencer area where the label stops.<br><br>Note that the exact meaning of this number depends on the operation mode, as defined with the SAOF command. In standard mode (SAOF ZERO), the command lines are counted from the start of the sequencer area, but in append mode (SAOF APP) they are counted from the start of the expansion of the sequencer area previously defined with DMAS. See example 4, below. \|⏎\| base_wf_set \| The base waveform set. This waveform set is used by the pattern tool to translate the physical waveform DC/DC power units of the vector area into device cycle names and data parameters, and vice versa. \| The base waveform set. This waveform set is used by the pattern tool to translate the physical waveform DC/DC power units of the vector area into device cycle names and data parameters, and vice versa. \|⏎\| wavetable \| The base wavetable. This wavetable is used by the pattern tool to translate the physical waveform DC/DC power units of the vector areas into device cycle names and data parameters, and vice versa. \| The base wavetable. This wavetable is used by the pattern tool to translate the physical waveform DC/DC power units of the vector areas into device cycle names and data parameters, and vice versa. \|⏎\| sync_grp \| If type has been set to MPBU, you can specify a synchronization (sequencing) group for the multi-port burst with this parameter. \| If type has been set to MPBU, you can specify a synchronization (sequencing) group for the multi-port burst with this parameter. \|⏎\| port \| The name of the port for which the label is defined. If you do not specify a port, the label will be defined for the default port @, to which all pins belong. \| The name of the port for which the label is defined. If you do not specify a port, the label will be defined for the default port @, to which all pins belong. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98748_2.htm`。` |
| 179 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.07.02.30_SQST__SQST__-__SeQuencer_STate.md` | `98752_2.htm` | #0 | 9×3 | 17 | 10 | `\| seq_state \| Gives the state of the sequencer: \| Gives the state of the sequencer: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| seq_state \| OFF \| In the OFF state, both the sequencers and the clock are off. All pins go into the high-z state.<br><br>This option can only be applied to all execution contexts. \|⏎\| seq_state \| HALT \| In the HALT state, both the sequencer and the clock are off, and the drivers keep their last level or remain in high-z state if already in high-z state.<br><br>Note that you cannot pass directly from HALT to OFF or from OFF to HALT. You must first pass to BRK in both cases.<br><br>This option can only be applied to all execution contexts. \|⏎\| seq_state \| BRK \| In the BRK state, the clock is on and the break waveform is applied to the DUT.<br><br>This option can only be applied to all execution contexts. \|⏎\| seq_state \| ABRT \| This is not a sequencer state, but an instruction to change the state of an execution context from RUN to BRK. ABRT requires that an execution context is specified. \|⏎\| seq_state \| CBRK \| This is not a sequencer state, but a conditional instruction. If no context is in BRK or RUN state, the state of the global context will be changed to BRK. If any context is in BRK or RUN state, nothing will be done. \|⏎\| seq_state \| RUN \| In the RUN state, the clock is on and the sequencer applies vectors to the DUT. This option can be applied to a specified execution context. \|⏎\| seq_state \| Note that if the sequencer state changes from OFF or BRK to RUN, the sequencer runs with the global settings that have previously been defined with [SQGB, SQGB? - (SeQuencer GloBal)](<02.06.02.02.232_SQGB__SQGB__-__SeQuencer_GloBal.md>). \| Note that if the sequencer state changes from OFF or BRK to RUN, the sequencer runs with the global settings that have previously been defined with [SQGB, SQGB? - (SeQuencer GloBal)](<02.06.02.02.232_SQGB__SQGB__-__SeQuencer_GloBal.md>). \|⏎\| exec_context \| An execution context. In the command, this parameter can only be added to the sequencer state RUN and the instruction ABRT. It must be added if ABRT is specified. For more information on execution contexts see [Using execution contexts](<02.06.07.01.08.05.03.02.03_Using_execution_contexts.md>) .<br><br>If the query is issued without a context, then RUN will be returned if there is at least one context that is in the RUN state. \| An execution context. In the command, this parameter can only be added to the sequencer state RUN and the instruction ABRT. It must be added if ABRT is specified. For more information on execution contexts see [Using execution contexts](<02.06.07.01.08.05.03.02.03_Using_execution_contexts.md>) .<br><br>If the query is issued without a context, then RUN will be returned if there is at least one context that is in the RUN state. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98752_2.htm`。` |

This must be a multiple of 8.<br><br>In the command, address + length may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. This can be<br><br>- SM for sequencer memory (the standard type), or<br>- SHMEM, a special kind of SM only for multi-site setups, or<br>- ALL (only in the query) for both SM and SHMEM. \|⏎\| port \| This parameter is required if the command is applied to a 7.x setup, where the STVA instructions are port specific (address specified in param_2).<br><br>If the parameter is omitted for an older setup (so that the address parameter refers to the one global vector area), VECD will find the correct location of the data by analyzing the STVA addresses of the setup. \| This parameter is required if the command is applied to a 7.x setup, where the STVA instructions are port specific (address specified in param_2).<br><br>If the parameter is omitted for an older setup (so that the address parameter refers to the one global vector area), VECD will find the correct location of the data by analyzing the STVA addresses of the setup. \|⏎\| port_list \| In the query, you can specify a port list for a 7.x setup. You will get an answer for every pin in pinlist that belongs to one of the specified ports. \| In the query, you can specify a port list for a 7.x setup. You will get an answer for every pin in pinlist that belongs to one of the specified ports. \|⏎\| address \| The address of the target area where the data transfer should start. This must be a multiple of 8. If a port is specified (which is required for a 7.x setup), the address refers to the specific vector area of this port. If no port is specified (which is only allowed for older setups), it refers to the one global vector area.<br><br>For the command, the start address may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. In this case, VECD extends the size of the target area accordingly; the new last address will be at address + length. \| The address of the target area where the data transfer should start. This must be a multiple of 8. If a port is specified (which is required for a 7.x setup), the address refers to the specific vector area of this port. If no port is specified (which is only allowed for older setups), it refers to the one global vector area.<br><br>For the command, the start address may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. In this case, VECD extends the size of the target area accordingly; the new last address will be at address + length. \|⏎\| length \| The length of the data that is to be down- or uploaded. This must be a multiple of 8.<br><br>In the command, address + length may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. In this case, VECD extends the size of the target area accordingly; the new last address will be at address + length.<br><br>This parameter is optional for the query. If you enter a length, the contents of the specified part of the vector area are returned. If you omit it, the contents of the memory from address to the current end address of the target area are returned. \| The length of the data that is to be down- or uploaded. This must be a multiple of 8.<br><br>In the command, address + length may be greater than the actual last address of the target area. In this case, VECD extends the size of the target area accordingly; the new last address will be at address + length.<br><br>This parameter is optional for the query. If you enter a length, the contents of the specified part of the vector area are returned. If you omit it, the contents of the memory from address to the current end address of the target area are returned. \|⏎\| pin_or_port \| The name of the pin whose vectors are to be down- or uploaded. If you specify a port, the vectors will be downloaded to all pins of this port.<br><br>Note that if the query is issued for a port, the answer will be given for the individual pins. \| The name of the pin whose vectors are to be down- or uploaded. If you specify a port, the vectors will be downloaded to all pins of this port.<br><br>Note that if the query is issued for a port, the answer will be given for the individual pins. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The pins for which vector data is to be returned from the vector area. \| The pins for which vector data is to be returned from the vector area. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| Vector data in binary format. This parameter allows you to define the length of data (in bytes) to be transferred and must be used as follows:<br><br>Type # to mark the start of the data block.<br><br>Type the number of digits for the length specifier (max. 9 digits) and then type the number of bytes to be transferred.<br><br>The uncompressed vector bytes are sent immediately after the length specifier and terminated by a linefeed character. One byte represents one vector. \| Vector data in binary format. This parameter allows you to define the length of data (in bytes) to be transferred and must be used as follows:<br><br>Type # to mark the start of the data block.<br><br>Type the number of digits for the length specifier (max. 9 digits) and then type the number of bytes to be transferred.<br><br>The uncompressed vector bytes are sent immediately after the length specifier and terminated by a linefeed character. One byte represents one vector. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98853_2.htm`。` |

| 185 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.09.02.01_CYRM__CYRM__-__CYcle_Resolution_Mode.md` | `98515_2.htm` | #0 | 5×3 | 10 | 5 | `\| resolution_mode \| Defines the resolution mode. This can be: \| Defines the resolution mode. This can be: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| resolution_mode \| CYCL \| This is the normal operation mode. In spite of its name (which was coined at a time when the normal operation mode was one result per cycle), it simply means that the result resolution as defined with DCRM is to be applied. This is the default. \|⏎\| resolution_mode \| EDGE \| This option<br><br>- activates a mechanism by which a test that failed on the cycle level is re-executed in order to enable error map recording on the edge level. For details see [Retrieving edge information by repeating the test](<02.06.11.01.02.02.01_Retrieving_edge_information_by_repeating_the_test.md>).<br><br>Note also that re-execution of the test can be blocked with the CYRP command.<br><br>- prevents acceptance of any DCRM configuration that combines more than one edge result into one recorded result, except DCRM 1,PFC.<br><br>Note that the EDGE option is not available for pins which take part in a match loop. \|⏎\| error_limit \| CURRENTLY NOT USED. \| CURRENTLY NOT USED. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The pin or pins to which this mode will be applied. You can also specify one or more pin groups or ports. \| The pin or pins to which this mode will be applied. You can also specify one or more pin groups or ports. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98515_2.htm`。` |
| 186 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.09.02.03_DRLS__-__DRive_Level_Sensitivity.md` | `98576_2.htm` | #0 | 15×3 | 30 | 15 | `\| test \| This parameter is one of the following: \| This parameter is one of the following: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| test \| H \| This parameter must be entered to determine the high input voltage threshold (VIH,min). VIH,min is the minimum input level which is recognized as logic 1 by the DUT. \|⏎\| test \| L \| This parameter must be entered to determine the low input voltage threshold (VIL,max). VIL,max is the maximum input level which is recognized as logic 0 by the DUT. \|⏎\| pass_value \| For a simple pass or fail test (limit parameter omitted), the pass value defines the expected pass level (in mV) for the test. For value measurements, this parameter defines the start value of the measurement window; set it to a value that ensures a pass result for the functional test. pass_value must be in the range of the drive level in the channel board (see [DRLV, DRLV? - (DRive LeVel)](<02.06.02.02.89_DRLV__DRLV__-__DRive_LeVel.md>)). \| For a simple pass or fail test (limit parameter omitted), the pass value defines the expected pass level (in mV) for the test. For value measurements, this parameter defines the start value of the measurement window; set it to a value that ensures a pass result for the functional test. pass_value must be in the range of the drive level in the channel board (see [DRLV, DRLV? - (DRive LeVel)](<02.06.02.02.89_DRLV__DRLV__-__DRive_LeVel.md>)). \|⏎\| limit \| The limit parameter is an integer which defines the upper (for a low level search) and lower (for a high level search) limits for the measurement window of a value measurement. The limit must be specified in mV. This parameter must be omitted if only a pass/fail result for the test is required. The range limitations for this parameter are as follows:<br><br>- when checking the high threshold, the limit parameter must be between the minimum drive level of the channel board (see [DRLV, DRLV? - (DRive LeVel](<02.06.02.02.89_DRLV__DRLV__-__DRive_LeVel.md>)) and the specified pass value;<br>- when checking the low level threshold, the limit parameter must be between the specified pass value and the maximum drive level of the channel board. \| The limit parameter is an integer which defines the upper (for a low level search) and lower (for a high level search) limits for the measurement window of a value measurement. The limit must be specified in mV. This parameter must be omitted if only a pass/fail result for the test is required. The range limitations for this parameter are as follows:<br><br>- when checking the high threshold, the limit parameter must be between the minimum drive level of the channel board (see [DRLV, DRLV? - (DRive LeVel](<02.06.02.02.89_DRLV__DRLV__-__DRive_LeVel.md>)) and the specified pass value;<br>- when checking the low level threshold, the limit parameter must be between the specified pass value and the maximum drive level of the channel board. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The name of the pin or pins that you want to test. This parameter may also include groups previously created using the DFGP command. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. pinlist must only contain pins configured as inputs. \| The name of the pin or pins that you want to test. This parameter may also include groups previously created using the DFGP command. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. pinlist must only contain pins configured as inputs. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| This reply parameter is one of the following: \| This reply parameter is one of the following: \|⏎\| value_specifier \| LT \| (less than) is returned if a VIL,max measurement was performed and already the first test (with the pass_value parameter) failed. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| LE \| (less than or equal to) is returned if a VIH,min measurement was performed and all the functional tests performed within the measurement window passed. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| EQ \| (equal to) is returned if a value measurement was performed and the first failed functional test occurred within the measurement window. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| GE \| (greater than or equal to) is returned if a VIL,max measurement was performed and all tests performed within the measurement window passed. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| GT \| (greater than) is returned if a VIH,min measurement has been performed and the first functional test performed within the measurement window failed. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| P \| This indicates that the functional test performed passed. This is only returned if the limit parameter is omitted. \|⏎\| value_specifier \| F \| This indicates that the functional test performed failed. This is only returned if the limit parameter is omitted. \|⏎\| result_value \| The result value is the level (in mV) of the last passed test. \| The result value is the level (in mV) of the last passed test. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98576_2.htm`。` |

| 189 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.09.02.14_SQGB__SQGB__-__SeQuencer_GloBal.md` | `98746_3.htm` | #0 | 14×3 | 28 | 14 | `\| mode \| Possible values are: \| Possible values are: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| mode \| ACQF \| ACQuire Failure mode. Only failed cycles are recorded, starting after analyze_delay cycles. If analyze_delay is not a multiple of 8, recording starts after the nearest smaller multiple of 8. In this mode the Error Map is called the Acquire Failure Map.<br><br>As the test proceeds the Acquire Failure Map is filled with failure data until the test stops or the map becomes completely full, whichever occurs first. If the map stops recording before the test has finished, the sequencer, which is in standard mode, continues executing the test until the end.<br><br>This mode is only supported for compatibility reasons. Instead, you should use DCGM and DCRP to configure the Error Map. The effect of ACQF is reproduced by:<br><br>DCGM FAIL,STRT,NONE<br><br>DCRP EMAP,<error_map_pos>,<ref_port> \|⏎\| mode \| ACFF \| This mode is functionally identical to ACQF. It is obsolete and should not be used in new applications. \|⏎\| mode \| EL \| EndLess. The sequencer repeats the complete test, including primaries and multiport synchronization, in an endless loop and can only be stopped by Break.<br><br>The analyze_delay parameter is ignored. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented mode.<br><br>This mode is used for oscilloscope tests. The Error Map functions as a rolling buffer. After stopping the sequencer, the Error Map shows the last "buffer-full" of recorded cycles. \|⏎\| mode \| ELPT \| EndLess PaTtern. The sequencer repeats only the user program. This avoids critical holds during measurement.<br><br>In case of multiport testing, ports may run with different frequencies. Using the ELPT mode in this case, can lead to the situation, that for loop counts >1 the synchronization is no longer executed. \|⏎\| mode \| EMPS \| Error Map PoSitioning. The Error Map is in cycle oriented mode, the sequencer in default mode. The analyze_delay parameter specifies the end of the recorded area. If it is not a multiple of 8, the last cycle to be displayed is the largest multiple of 8 that is smaller than analyze_delay. The first cycle to be displayed is determined by the size of the Error Map (8k cycles).<br><br>This mode is only supported for compatibility reasons. Instead, you should use DCGM and DCRP to configure the Error Map. The effect of EMPS is reproduced by:<br><br>DCGM ALL,HALT,NONE<br><br>DCRP EMAP,<error_map_pos>,<ref_port> \|⏎\| mode \| SE \| Stop on Error. The sequencer runs from the beginning of the test and stops when an error is encountered. If no errors occur, the sequencer runs to the end of the test and stops. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM. The analyze_delay parameter is ignored. \|⏎\| mode \| SEEL \| Stop on Error and EndLess. The sequencer repeats the test in an endless loop, unless an error is encountered, whereupon it stops. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode. This mode is used to locate sporadic failures. \|⏎\| mode \| STSQ \| STop SeQuencer. Stops the sequencer after analyze_delay cycles. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM. See also the query [GETC? - (GET Cycles)](<02.06.02.02.131_GETC__-__GET_Cycles.md>) for the analyze_delay count. \|⏎\| mode \| STRD \| In the query reply, this mode is displayed if the current combination of recording mode, as set with DCGM, and interpretation of the Error Map position, as set with DCRP, is different from all the other SQGB modes. For example, the Error Map can be in cycle oriented recording mode, and analyze_delay be interpreted as a start value.<br><br>In the command, this option leaves it to SmarTest to select the appropriate recording mode (cycle oriented or acquire failure), unless the recording mode has been explicitly set with DCGM. \|⏎\| mode \| STSV \| STop Sequencer on Vector. Stops the sequencer at the logical vector number specified in the analyze_delay parameter before executing the vector. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM.<br><br>Note that the STSV option cannot be used if no start label has been defined with SQSL, or if the start label is a burst or multi-port burst label. \|⏎\| analyze_delay \| Sets the analyze delay. This parameter is mandatory for the STSQ and STSV modes. Its interpretation depends on the selected mode parameter. The number must be between 1 (0 for ACQF) and 281,474,976,710,655 = 2^48 - 1 (2^48 - 2 for ACQF).<br><br>If the mode parameter is set to STSV, three cases must be distinguished:<br><br>- If analyze_delay is an existing logical vector number of the actual start label, the sequencer will be stopped at this logical vector before executing the vector.<br>- If analyze_delay specifies the total number of logical vectors (= greatest logical vector number of the test + 1) the sequencer stops at the last valid vector.<br>- If analyze_delay is greater than the total number of logical vectors, an error message is issued. \| Sets the analyze delay. This parameter is mandatory for the STSQ and STSV modes. Its interpretation depends on the selected mode parameter. The number must be between 1 (0 for ACQF) and 281,474,976,710,655 = 2^48 - 1 (2^48 - 2 for ACQF).<br><br>If the mode parameter is set to STSV, three cases must be distinguished:<br><br>- If analyze_delay is an existing logical vector number of the actual start label, the sequencer will be stopped at this logical vector before executing the vector.<br>- If analyze_delay specifies the total number of logical vectors (= greatest logical vector number of the test + 1) the sequencer stops at the last valid vector.<br>- If analyze_delay is greater than the total number of logical vectors, an error message is issued. \|⏎\| ref_port \| This parameter accepts a single port. It must be specified in the following situation:<br><br>- The selected mode requires an analyze delay (but is different from STSV), and<br>- the start label is a multi-port burst.<br><br>It specifies the port which is used for counting the cycles specified with the analyze_delay parameter. When this reference port has completed the specified number of cycles, the sequencers will be stopped, or cycle recording started or stopped, for all ports. Note that the reference port must belong to the current start label (as defined by SQSL). \| This parameter accepts a single port. It must be specified in the following situation:<br><br>- The selected mode requires an analyze delay (but is different from STSV), and<br>- the start label is a multi-port burst.<br><br>It specifies the port which is used for counting the cycles specified with the analyze_delay parameter. When this reference port has completed the specified number of cycles, the sequencers will be stopped, or cycle recording started or stopped, for all ports. Note that the reference port must belong to the current start label (as defined by SQSL). \|⏎\| port_list \| If the current mode requires an analyze delay, you can specify a port list in the query. The reply will give the calculated analyze delays for those ports that are not used as the reference port. These delays are derived from the analyze delay of the reference port and the ratio between the period lengths of the reference port and the other port in the primary timing setup. If you specify @@, SQGB will return the settings for all ports of the current start label. \| If the current mode requires an analyze delay, you can specify a port list in the query. The reply will give the calculated analyze delays for those ports that are not used as the reference port. These delays are derived from the analyze delay of the reference port and the ratio between the period lengths of the reference port and the other port in the primary timing setup. If you specify @@, SQGB will return the settings for all ports of the current start label. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98746_3.htm`。` |

If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. Pins in pinlist can be O or IO pin types. Both the driver high and low levels will be moved by the same values. \|⏎\| Level resources \| VOOFFSET \| Receiver offsets. Both the receiver high and low levels will be moved by the same values. \|⏎\| Level resources \| VTH \| Receiver thresholds are moved. \|⏎\| Level resources \| VOSWING \| Receiver swings are moved. \|⏎\| start \| This parameter defines the start of the search range relative to the edge or level positions as defined in the current primary timing or level. The binary search is stopped when the distance between the last two binary search steps is ≤resolution.<br><br>Units for timing and level searches are ns and mV. \| This parameter defines the resolution of the binary search. The binary search is stopped when the distance between the last two binary search steps is ≤resolution.<br><br>Units for timing and level searches are ns and mV. \|⏎\| min_pass_count \| The PASS area determined by the binary search may still contain isolated FAIL results at its start or end. This parameter activates an additional check that is intended to exclude this. It specifies how many consecutive PASS results must be present at the start (before the end) of the putative PASS region. If this requirement is not satisfied, the left boundary of the area is moved to the right (the right boundary moved to the left) until the required number of consecutive PASS results is found.<br><br>The default for min_pass_count is 1. \| The PASS area determined by the binary search may still contain isolated FAIL results at its start or end. This parameter activates an additional check that is intended to exclude this. It specifies how many consecutive PASS results must be present at the start (before the end) of the putative PASS region. If this requirement is not satisfied, the left boundary of the area is moved to the right (the right boundary moved to the left) until the required number of consecutive PASS results is found.<br><br>The default for min_pass_count is 1. \|⏎\| min_stable_error_count \| This and the histogram_step_width parameter define a histogram based search, which is performed after the binary search. It moves the left boundary of the PASS area to the left (the right boundary to the right) until the number of errors is the same for the number of search steps specified with min_stable_error_count. \|⏎\| histogram_step_width \| This parameter defines the step width of the histogram search. \| This parameter defines the step width of the histogram search. \|⏎\| transition_detection \| This parameter controls the criterion for a test to be considered as PASSED. Possible values are: \| This parameter controls the criterion for a test to be considered as PASSED. Possible values are: \|⏎\|  \| PF \| The test is considered to have passed only if no errors occurred at all. This is the default \|⏎\|  \| ECL \| The test is considered to have passed if the number of errors is smaller than error_count_limit. \|⏎\|  \| PF_FASTSTOP \| Like PF but, using the FASTSTOP mode, stops the linear search after the first PASS area is detected (see [Linear search modes](<02.06.09.01.02.01_Linear_search_modes.md>)). \|⏎\|  \| ECL_FASTSTOP \| Like ECL but, using the FASTSTOP mode, stops the linear search after the first PASS area is detected (see [Linear search modes](<02.06.09.01.02.01_Linear_search_modes.md>)). \|⏎\| error_count_limit \| This parameter defines the minimum number of errors that are not tolerated if transition_detection is set to ECL. \| This parameter defines the minimum number of errors that are not tolerated if transition_detection is set to ECL. \|⏎\| restore_flag \| The restore_flag parameter enables or disables the restore process after a search was executed: It allows you to suppress storing of the search result. \| The restore_flag parameter enables or disables the restore process after a search was executed: It allows you to suppress storing of the search result. \|⏎\|  \| YES \| Restores the timing / level offsets to the values it had before the offset search was executed. \|⏎\|  \| NO \| The new measured offset values are used in the edge position calculation.<br><br>This is the default configuration. \|⏎\| setup_pinlist \| This parameter defines the pins the edge positions of which are to be changed. If an input pin is specified, the O or IO pins on which the outcome of a search step is to depend must be defined with TOCR.<br><br>Note also that the edge type specified with the edge_type parameter must be in line with the pin type. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/124942_2.htm`。` |
| 192 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.09.02.20_TORN__-__Timing_level_Offset_search_RuN.md` | `125309_2.htm` | #0 | 6×3 | 15 | 3 | `\| pinlist \| The pins for which the embedded search is to be executed. You can specify @ for all pins.<br><br>Note the difference between a list of pins and @ in a multi-site setup. In the former case, all channels that are associated with these pins must have the embedded timing/level search capability and must have been configured with TOCF. If this is not the case, TORN? will return the status FAILED. But if @ is specified, channels that do not fulfill both these conditions will simply be ignored. \| The pins for which the embedded search is to be executed. If this is not the case, TORN? will return the status FAILED. But if @ is specified, channels that do not fulfill both these conditions will simply be ignored. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| exec_context \| An execution context. For more information on execution contexts see [Using execution contexts](<02.06.07.01.08.05.03.02.03_Using_execution_contexts.md>) . \| An execution context. For more information on execution contexts see [Using execution contexts](<02.06.07.01.08.05.03.02.03_Using_execution_contexts.md>) . \|⏎\| status \| TORN? returns one of the following three status codes: \| TORN? returns one of the following three status codes: \|⏎\|  \| SUCCEEDED \| The search was successfully finished for all active sites. \|⏎\|  \| FAILED \| The search failed for at least one channel. \|⏎\|  \| ABORTED \| The search was canceled by the user. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/125309_2.htm`。` |
| 193 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.09.02.25_TTRS__-__Time_TRansition_Search.md` | `98819_2.htm` | #0 | 15×3 | 30 | 15 | `\| start \| The start parameter defines the point in time (in ns), relative to the start of the cycle, where you want the search to begin. \| The start parameter defines the point in time (in ns), relative to the start of the cycle, where you want the search to begin. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| stop \| The stop parameter defines the point in time (in ns), relative to the start of the cycle, where you want the search to end. \| The stop parameter defines the point in time (in ns), relative to the start of the cycle, where you want the search to end. \|⏎\| search_type \| The search type is one of the following: \| The search type is one of the following: \|⏎\| search_type \| L \| Specifies a linear search from the start of the window, at intervals defined by the ESGB command. The search will terminate after the first failed test or after the search limit specified by the stop parameter has been reached. \|⏎\| search_type \| LB \| Specifies a combined linear and binary search using the step width defined by the ESGB command. A linear search is performed, with an additional binary search carried out in the interval between the last passed and the first failed test. \|⏎\| search_type \| B \| Specifies a binary search carried out between the start and the stop positions. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The name of the pin or pins on which the search is to take place. This parameter may also include groups previously created using [DFGP, DFGP? - (DeFine GrouP)](<02.06.02.02.60_DFGP__DFGP__-__DeFine_GrouP.md>). If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. Pinlist must only contain pins configured as outputs. \| The name of the pin or pins on which the search is to take place. This parameter may also include groups previously created using [DFGP, DFGP? - (DeFine GrouP)](<02.06.02.02.60_DFGP__DFGP__-__DeFine_GrouP.md>). If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. Pinlist must only contain pins configured as outputs. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| This parameter can be: \| This parameter can be: \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| LT \| (less than) is returned if the first functional test of a search in the positive direction failed. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| LE \| (less than or equal to) is returned if all the functional tests performed between start and stop pass. LE is only returned for a search in the negative direction. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| EQ \| (equal to) is returned if the first failure of the search occurs between the start and the stop positions. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| GE \| (greater than or equal to) is returned if all the tests carried out between start and stop pass. GE can only be returned for a search in the positive direction. \|⏎\| value_<br><br>specifier \| GT \| (greater than) is returned if the first functional test performed during a search in the negative direction fails. \|⏎\| pass_value \| The pass_value is the time (in ns) at which the last passed test occurs. A pass value is only returned for if value_specifier is EQ, LE, or GE. \| The pass_value is the time (in ns) at which the last passed test occurs. A pass value is only returned for if value_specifier is EQ, LE, or GE. \|⏎\| fail_value \| The fail_value is the time (in ns) at which the last failed test occurs. A fail value is only returned for if value_specifier is EQ, LT, or GT. \| The fail_value is the time (in ns) at which the last failed test occurs. A fail value is only returned for if value_specifier is EQ, LT, or GT. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98819_2.htm`。` |

| 196 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.10.02.08_MSET__MSET__-__definition_of_Measurement_SET.md` | `98657_2.htm` | #0 | 53×3 | 101 | 58 | `\| id_dc \| The DC set identification number. The number is an integer in the range 1 to 256. A DC set consists of up to 10 measurement sets. For the query, you can specify all the defined DC sets with the ALL specifier. \| The DC set identification number. The number is an integer in the range 1 to 256. A DC set consists of up to 10 measurement sets. For the query, you can specify all the defined DC sets with the ALL specifier. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| id_mset \| The measurement set identification number. The number is an integer in the range 1 to 10. For the query, you can specify all the defined measurement sets with the ALL specifier. \| The measurement set identification number. The number is an integer in the range 1 to 10. For the query, you can specify all the defined measurement sets with the ALL specifier. \|⏎\| glob_act \| Global action in the measurement set. The allowed values are: \| Global action in the measurement set. The allowed values are: \|⏎\| glob_act \| LEN \| This action creates a default measurement set of the length specified with par1. par2 must be omitted. LEN must be executed before you can define any individual action.<br><br>This action can only be executed for all configured pins (@). \|⏎\| glob_act \| WAIT \| This action defines the waiting time for the sequencers after execution of the measurement set. The actual time is specified with the par2 parameter (in ms). par1 must be omitted.<br><br>Note that it takes 0.32/0.58/0.40 ms respectively for the PS1600 (DC-relays only)/PS9G/pin scale[1)](<#fntarg_1>) cards to open or close relays. Further, 4.0/2.8/1.0 ms respectively is needed for the PS1600/PS9G/pin scale cards as settling time of PMUs after closing relays. Therefore, for PS1600 for example, you must set at least 4.32 ms (for connecting relays) or 0.32 ms (for disconnecting relays) for the action for opening and closing relays.<br><br>This action can only be executed for all configured pins (@). \|⏎\| glob_act \| SWAIT \| This action defines the waiting time for the sequencers before execution of the measurement set. The actual time is specified with the par2 parameter (in ms). par1 must be omitted.<br><br>The default is 0, in which case the sequencers start the measurement immediately.<br><br>A possible reason for defining such a wait time is to ensure that the vectors needed for preparing the measurement have actually been shifted out of the data queue. This is ensured if<br><br>wait_time = 28 * user_period.<br><br>This action can only be executed for all configured pins (@).<br><br>Note This action is only supported for DC measurements that are started from within the sequencer program with the MEAS instruction. It cannot be used with the MEAS firmware command. \|⏎\| glob_act \| RSET \| This action undefines the specified measurement set. par1 and par2 must be omitted.<br><br>RSET can only be executed for all configured pins (@). \|⏎\| glob_act \| ACT \| This action activates the specified measurement set for execution. This is the default. \|⏎\| glob_act \| X \| This action inactivates the specified measurement set for execution. \|⏎\| act \| Individual action in the measurement set. The allowed values for the measurement setup are: \| Individual action in the measurement set. The allowed values for the measurement setup are: \|⏎\| act \| SEL \| Selects the working mode of the PMU. Set par2 to CURR for current measurements, or to VOLT for voltage measurements. \|⏎\| act \| IRNG \| Specifies the current range with par2 that is. You can specify a value in µA or one of the following codes:<br><br>IRA = +/- 10 µA range<br><br>IRB = +/- 100 µA range<br><br>IRC = +/- 1 mA range<br><br>IRD = +/- 40 mA range<br><br>If a value is specified, the smallest range that does not exceed the value will be set. \|⏎\| act \| CRNG \| Specifies the current range for the clamp with par2. The possible options are the same as for IRNG. The range for CRNG and IRNG must be identical. \|⏎\| act \| Note that SEL and IRNG must have been defined before any of the following setup actions can be executed. Also the follwing actions must be redefined after a change of SEL or IRNG. \| Note that SEL and IRNG must have been defined before any of the following setup actions can be executed. Also the follwing actions must be redefined after a change of SEL or IRNG. \|⏎\| act \| IFOR \| Specifies a force current with par2 in µA when the working mode (SEL action) is set to voltage measurement. \|⏎\| act \| UFOR \| Specifies a force voltage with par2 in mV when the working mode (SEL action) is set to current measurement. \|⏎\| act \| PMUL \| Specifies the lower pass limit of the DC test with par2 in µA for current measurements, or in mV for voltage measurements. This value is used for pass/fail judgment and is set to the PPMU hardware. \|⏎\| act \| PMUH \| Specifies the higher pass limit of the DC test with par2 in µA for current measurement, or in mV for voltage measurement. This value is used for pass/fail judgment and is set to the PPMU hardware. \|⏎\| act \| You can control the AC relay, the PPMU relay and the DC relay if the used hardware supports these relays. The allowed values for relay control are: \| You can control the AC relay, the PPMU relay and the DC relay if the used hardware supports these relays. The allowed values for relay control are: \|⏎\| act \| AC \| The AC relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The other two relays are always open. \|⏎\| act \| PPMU \| The PPMU relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The other two relays are always open. \|⏎\| act \| DC \| The DC relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The other two relays are always open. \|⏎\| act \| ACDC \| The AC and DC relays can be closed or opened simultaneously with ON/OFF for par2. The PPMU relay is always open. \|⏎\| act \| ACPM \| The AC and PPMU relays can be closed or opened simultaneously with ON/OFF for par2. The DC relay is always open. \|⏎\| act \| DCPM \| The PPMU and DC relays can be closed or opened simultaneously with ON/OFF for par2. The AC relay is always open. \|⏎\| act \| ALL \| All three relays can be closed or opened simultaneously with ON/OFF for par2. \|⏎\| act \| OAC \| The AC relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The state of the other two relays remains unchanged. \|⏎\| act \| OPM \| The PPMU relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The state of the other two relays remains unchanged. \|⏎\| act \| ODC \| The DC relay can be closed or opened with ON/OFF for par2. The state of the other two relays remains unchanged. \|⏎\| act \| The following actions set up the voltage clamp for Pin Scale systems: \| The following actions set up the voltage clamp for Pin Scale systems: \|⏎\| act \| CLPL \| Specifies the clamp low voltage with par2 in mV. \|⏎\| act \| CLPH \| Specifies the clamp high voltage with par2 in mV.<br><br>This clamp high voltage must be greater than the clamp low voltage. The setting range is same as for CLPL. \|⏎\| act \| CLMP \| Enables or disables the PPMU clamp with par2. ON enables the clamps, OFF disables them.<br><br>The clamp operates for both current force (IVOR action) and voltage force (UFOR action).<br><br>Before setting this parameter to ON, both the clamp low and high voltage (current) should be defined by CLPL (ICPL) and CLPH (ICPH) to prevent that unknown clamp voltages will be set momentarily. \|⏎\| act \| ICPL \| Specifies the clamp low current with par2 in µA. \|⏎\| act \| ICPH \| Specifies the clamp high current with par2 in µA. \|⏎\| act \| The following actions define the measurement mode: \| The following actions define the measurement mode: \|⏎\| act \| PMUM \| This action provides a PASS/FAIL result. This depends on how the measured value is compared with the limits. The compare mode can be set as par2 with the following specifiers:<br><br>I (for Intermediate): PASS is returned if the measured value lies between the lower and the higher limit. This is the default.<br><br>H (for High): PASS is returned if the measured value is not smaller than the higher limit.<br><br>L (for Low): PASS is returned if the measured value is not greater than the lower limit.<br><br>X: Mask (Always returns PASS).Note For EXA Scale digital cards, to push the register setting into the hardware, the SYNC action must be executed immediately after this action. \|⏎\| act \| VMUM \| This action provides a measurement value, but also a PASS/FAIL result. For the latter a compare mode must be specified with par2. The possible options are same as for PMUM.<br><br>Note This action is only supported for DC measurements that are started within the sequencer program with the [MEAS](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.16.02.06.10.02_Sequencer_instructions.md#_99880__row_MEAS>) instruction. It cannot be used with the MEAS firmware command. Note For EXA Scale digital cards, to push the register setting into the hardware, the SYNC action must be executed immediately after this action. \|⏎\| act \| DVM1 \| This action provides the first value for a delta measurement. It also returns a PASS/FAIL result, for which a compare mode must be specified with par2. The possible options are same as for PMUM.<br><br>Note This action is only supported for DC measurements that are started within the sequencer program with the [MEAS](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.16.02.06.10.02_Sequencer_instructions.md#_99880__row_MEAS>) instruction. It cannot be used with the MEAS firmware command. Note For EXA Scale digital cards, to push the register setting into the hardware, the SYNC action must be executed immediately after this action. \|⏎\| act \| DVM2 \| This action<br><br>- provides the second value for a delta measurement,<br>- calculates the difference between this value and the one provided by DVM1,<br>- creates a PASS/FAIL result from this difference by comparing it with the limits (PMUL and PMUH).<br><br>For the last step, a compare mode must be specified with par2. The possible options are same as for PMUM.<br><br>Note This action is only supported for DC measurements that are started within the sequencer program with the [MEAS](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.16.02.06.10.02_Sequencer_instructions.md#_99880__row_MEAS>) instruction. It cannot be used with the MEAS firmware command. Note For EXA Scale digital cards, to push the register setting into the hardware, the SYNC action must be executed immediately after this action. \|⏎\| act \| MUX_PPMU_BADC<br><br>(Available on PS1600) \| Supports using the board ADC to measure voltage/current on pins during a pattern running in a functional test.<br><br>This action controls the multilplexer before a board ADC to select the signal coming from the pin in {pinlist} via its PPMU and inputs it to the board ADC for value measurement.<br><br>To support this action:<br><br>- The values for {part2} are "ON" and "OFF".<br>- {pinlist} can contain only one pin or pins of different logical boards.<br><br>Putting a number of pins of the same logical board in the {pinlist} will result in resource confliction errors, because they share the same one board ADC and this board ADC can measure only one of them each time. \|⏎\| act \| MUX_VM_HIZ<br><br>(Available on PS1600) \| Supports using the board ADC to measure high impedance or high voltage on pins during a pattern running in a functional test.<br><br>This action controls the multiplexer before a board ADC to select the voltage signal coming from the pin in {pinlist} that has been connected to a DC Line and inputs it to the board ADC for value measurement.<br><br>To support this action:<br><br>- The values for {part2} are "ON" and "OFF".<br>- {pinlist} can contain only one pin or pins of different logical boards.<br><br>Putting pins of the same logical board in the {pinlist} will result in resource confliction errors, because they share the same one board ADC and this board ADC can measure only one of them each time. \|⏎\| act \| There are two actions which are not related to any af the DC measurement tasks: \| There are two actions which are not related to any af the DC measurement tasks: \|⏎\| act \| NOP \| This action does nothing at all. \|⏎\| act \| ACTI \| This action pushes the register setting into the hardware. It must be executed at the end of every measurement set, except when PMUM, VMUM, DVM1, or DVM2 is the only action within the set.<br><br>For the EXA Scale digital card, it must be followed by the SYNC action. \|⏎\| act \| SYNC \| This action pushes the register setting into the hardware, ensuring proper timing for the EXA Scale digital cards (the action does nothing for other digital cards). It must be executed right after ACTI, PMUM, VMUM, DVM1, or DVM2. \|⏎\| par1 \| First parameter for glob_act. The possible values depend on the act parameter. \| First parameter for glob_act. The possible values depend on the act parameter. \|⏎\| id_act \| The index of an individual action within the measurement set. \| The index of an individual action within the measurement set. \|⏎\| par2 \| Second parameter for glob_act. The possible values depend on the act parameter. \| Second parameter for glob_act. The possible values depend on the act parameter. \|⏎\| numOfSamples \| The number of samples to be taken for one measurement value. Depending on the specified waveform size, the number of samples is constraint.<br><br>If only one measurement is taken, the number of samples can be an integer between 1 and 4096.<br><br>If a waveform (waveformSize > 1) is captured, then the number of samples must be one. \| The number of samples to be taken for one measurement value. Depending on the specified waveform size, the number of samples is constraint.<br><br>If only one measurement is taken, the number of samples can be an integer between 1 and 4096.<br><br>If a waveform (waveformSize > 1) is captured, then the number of samples must be one. \|⏎\| waveformSize \| The number of measurements to be taken in the waveform. It is an integer between 1 and 4096. \| The number of measurements to be taken in the waveform. It is an integer between 1 and 4096. \|⏎\| samplePeriod \| The sampling period for the waveform to measure, specified as float64 in [s]. The valid range is from 4 us to 409.6 us. The first sample has an additional trigger delay of 14.5us.<br><br>The sampling period resolution is 25 ns. \| The sampling period for the waveform to measure, specified as float64 in [s]. The valid range is from 4 us to 409.6 us. The first sample has an additional trigger delay of 14.5us.<br><br>The sampling period resolution is 25 ns. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Target pin or pins for act. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. pinlist can contain digital pins configured as O, I, IO, or DC pin types. \| Target pin or pins for act. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. pinlist can contain digital pins configured as O, I, IO, or DC pin types. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98657_2.htm`。` |

The data length specifier is next - up to 9 digits determine how many bytes of data will follow. Then comes the actual data string which appears as a list of values separated by a "," (comma). The first byte of data is an identifier which defines the format of the following data. For a waveform the first byte is '\\x00' with the waveform represented as FLOAT64 values. Each value is in Amps [A]. \| The binary data result string. The data length specifier is next - up to 9 digits determine how many bytes of data will follow. Then comes the actual data string which appears as a list of values separated by a "," (comma). The first byte of data is an identifier which defines the format of the following data. For a waveform the first byte is '\\x00' with the waveform represented as FLOAT64 values. | 201 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.01.02.01.03.01_Detailed_Error_Map.md` | `59177.htm` | #0 | 10×4 | 29 | 11 | `\| Sequencer termination \| Recording mode \| Recording position \| Notes \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| standard termination \| cycle oriented \| start position<br><br>or<br><br>position ignored \| Recording will only stop at the end of the test, so that the results for the region of interest may be overwritten by wrapping around. \|⏎\| standard termination \| cycle oriented \| stop position \| Recording will stop at the specified position. No complications can arise. \|⏎\| standard termination \| acquire failure \| start position<br><br>or<br><br>position ignored \| Recording will stop when the Error Map is full, which may happen before the region of interest is reached. \|⏎\| standard termination \| acquire failure \| stop position \| Recording will stop at the specified position or when the Error Map is full. In the latter case, the results for the region of interest may never be recorded.<br><br>If you let SmarTest select the recording mode, and if the stop position is greater than the number of cycles that can be stored in the detailed Error Map, SmarTest will switch to cycle oriented mode. \|⏎\| non-standard termination with EL, SE, SEEL, STSQ, or STSV \| cycle oriented \| start position \| Not allowed!<br><br>FTST? and SQST RUN (which should be used for EL and SEEL) will not execute a functional test for these combinations. \|⏎\| non-standard termination with EL, SE, SEEL, STSQ, or STSV \| cycle oriented \| position ignored \| Recording will be stopped by stopping the sequencers. \|⏎\| non-standard termination with EL, SE, SEEL, STSQ, or STSV \| cycle oriented \| stop position \| Not allowed!<br><br>The recording stop for the STSQ and STSV modes is determined by the sequencer stop as defined by the analyze_delay parameter of SQGB. It is not possible to define a different recording stop position for these termination modes.<br><br>FTST? and SQST RUN (which should be used for EL and SEEL) will not execute a functional test for these combinations. \|⏎\| non-standard termination with EL, SE, SEEL, STSQ, or STSV \| acquire failure \| start position<br><br>or<br><br>stop position \| Not allowed!<br><br>FTST? and SQST RUN (which should be used for EL and SEEL) will not execute a functional test for this combination. \|⏎\| non-standard termination with EL, SE, SEEL, STSQ, or STSV \|  \| position ignored \| Recording will be stopped by stopping the sequencers or when the Error Map is full. A warning will be issued that the Error Map may be full before the region of interest is reached. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/59177.htm`。` |

| 203 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.02.01_CDCP__-__Cycle_Device_Cycle_Parameter.md` | `98499_3.htm` | #0 | 23×3 | 46 | 23 | `\| start_cycle \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle to be analyzed. The range of values for this parameter depends on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle to be analyzed. The range of values for this parameter depends on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| cycle \| In the query, the consecutive number of the displayed cycle. \| In the query, the consecutive number of the displayed cycle. \|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles to be analyzed. The range for this parameter is 0 to 1000. \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles to be analyzed. The range for this parameter is 0 to 1000. \|⏎\| strt_disp \| This parameter defines the offset of your range of interest from start_cycle. The actual display starts with the last device cycle that does not begin before start_cycle and does not end before start_cycle + strt_disp.<br><br>The purpose of this construction is to enable a correct device cycle reconstruction even if the first cycle you are interested in does not lie at a device cycle boundary (a device cycle can generate up to 16 cycles). \| This parameter defines the offset of your range of interest from start_cycle. The actual display starts with the last device cycle that does not begin before start_cycle and does not end before start_cycle + strt_disp.<br><br>The purpose of this construction is to enable a correct device cycle reconstruction even if the first cycle you are interested in does not lie at a device cycle boundary (a device cycle can generate up to 16 cycles). \|⏎\| disp_length \| Specifies the display length in cycles. This parameter determines the end of your range of interest. The actual display stops with the last device cycle that starts before start_cycle + strt_disp + disp_length.<br><br>If this parameter is larger than no_of_cycles, it is ignored. \| Specifies the display length in cycles. This parameter determines the end of your range of interest. The actual display stops with the last device cycle that starts before start_cycle + strt_disp + disp_length.<br><br>If this parameter is larger than no_of_cycles, it is ignored. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The pin name or names for which a device cycle data transfer is to be made. If more than one pin is required, the names must be separated with a comma. The @ character can be used to download data for all configured pins. \| The pin name or names for which a device cycle data transfer is to be made. If more than one pin is required, the names must be separated with a comma. The @ character can be used to download data for all configured pins. \|⏎\| dev_cyc_name \| A reply parameter, giving the name of the reconstructed device cycle. \| A reply parameter, giving the name of the reconstructed device cycle. \|⏎\| dev_cyc_len \| A reply parameter, giving the length of the reconstructed device cycle. For SmarTest 7.10 this is always 1. \| A reply parameter, giving the length of the reconstructed device cycle. For SmarTest 7.10 this is always 1. \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| Drive Data parameters. They contain values for both drive data and tri-state. The values the system returns are: \| Drive Data parameters. They contain values for both drive data and tri-state. The values the system returns are: \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F00 \| drive 0, tri-state off \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F10 \| drive 1, tri-state off \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F0Z \| drive 0, tri-state on \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F1Z \| drive 1, tri-state on \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| N \| Not used. Returned if no drive data parameter is set to a drive and tri-state value. \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| UC \| Unclear. The system may return this value to indicate that the interpretation of the device cycle for that parameter is unclear, that is, the physical waveforms are inconsistent for the device cycle. \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| Expected Data Parameters. The values the system returns are: \| Expected Data Parameters. The values the system returns are: \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| F0 \| compare to 0 \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| F1 \| compare to 1 \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FI \| compare to intermediate \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FX \| compare to mask \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FU \| compare to unstable \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| N \| Not used. Returned if no expected data parameter is set to a compare value. \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| UC \| Unclear. The system may return this value to indicate that the interpretation of the device cycle for that parameter is unclear, that is, the physical waveforms are inconsistent for the device cycle. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98499_3.htm`。` |
