The data is returned for each pin separately. Edges without action are not reported. \|⏎\|  \| The data is to be decoded as follows:<br><br>Each cycle of the reported cycle range is preceded by a two byte header information:<br><br>- First byte, bits 7, 6, 5, 4: Always zero, reserved for future use.<br>- First byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Always 1: number of waveforms contained in the device cycle.<br>- Second byte: Number of edge actions following (0 - 140) \| The data is to be decoded as follows:<br><br>Each cycle of the reported cycle range is preceded by a two byte header information:<br><br>- First byte, bits 7, 6, 5, 4: Always zero, reserved for future use.<br>- First byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Always 1: number of waveforms contained in the device cycle.<br>- Second byte: Number of edge actions following (0 - 140) \|⏎\|  \| Each of the following edge actions is described by two bytes:<br><br>- First byte, bit 7: Channel selector for differential pins. The first channel has the value 0, the second the value 1.<br>- First byte, bits 6, 5, 4, 3: Waveform index (0 - 9)<br>- First byte, bits 2, 1, 0: Edge index (0 - 7)<br>- Second byte, bit 7: Error flag<br><br>0: No error on receive edge, or a drive edge<br><br>1: Cannot occur since only expected data are reported<br><br>- Second byte, bits 6, 5: Data parameter<br><br>00: Fixed value<br><br>10: Data parameter 1<br><br>11: Data parameter 2<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>- Second byte, bit 4: Edge direction<br><br>0: Drive/tri-state edge<br><br>1: Receive edge<br><br>- Second byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0<br><br>If bit 4 is a 0, then drive/tri-state data:<br><br>Bits 3, 2: Tri-state data<br><br>00: Not used<br><br>10: Tri-state off<br><br>11: Tri-state on<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>Bits 1, 0: Drive data<br><br>00: Not used<br><br>10: Drive 0<br><br>11: Drive 1<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>- Second byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0<br><br>If bit 4 is a 1, then receive data compare method:<br><br>0001: Edge compare to intermediate<br><br>0010: Edge compare to 0<br><br>0011: Edge compare to 1<br><br>0100: Edge compare to mask<br><br>0101: Edge compare to unstable<br><br>1001: Window compare to intermediate<br><br>1010: Window compare to 0<br><br>1011: Window compare to 1<br><br>1100: Window compare to mask<br><br>1101: Window compare to unstable<br><br>1111: Window close<br><br>0000: Reserved for future use<br><br>0111: Reserved for future use<br><br>1000: Reserved for future use \| Each of the following edge actions is described by two bytes:<br><br>- First byte, bit 7: Channel selector for differential pins. This byte is returned only once in the reply. \|⏎\|  \|  \|  \| bits 7-4: Pin multiplexing factor. Specifies the number of Pin Scale pins that are mapped to one HXA pin or differential pin. 1 stands for differential pins or 2-multiplexed HXA pins, 3 for 4-multiplexed HXA pins.<br><br>The parameter is set to 0 for normal pins. \|⏎\|  \|  \|  \| bits 3-0: Channel selector. If the pin multiplexing factor is > 0, this field specifies the logical position of the pin in the multiplexing order. \|⏎\|  \|  \|  \| Information relating to HXA multiplexing is only returned if the MUX flag is set. \|⏎\|  \| Byte 1: \| Byte 1: \| Always 1. This byte is returned for every cycle. \|⏎\|  \| Byte 2,3: \| Byte 2,3: \| The number of actions to follow. The maximum, for an HXA pin mapped to 4 Pin Scale pins, is 640. This byte is returned for every cycle. \|⏎\|  \| Every edge action is described in 3 bytes. \| Every edge action is described in 3 bytes. \| Every edge action is described in 3 bytes. \|⏎\|  \| Byte A: \| Byte A: \| Normal action or OOB action. Possible values are:<br><br>0: Normal action<br><br>1: OOB action \|⏎\|  \| Byte B: \| Byte B: \| Edge index (0-7 for normal pins, up to 31 for HXA pins). \|⏎\|  \| Byte C: \| Byte C: \| Action data \|⏎\|  \|  \|  \| bit 7: Action type. The following values can occur:<br><br>0: Drive action<br><br>1: Receive action \|⏎\|  \|  \|  \| bit 6-4: Received value, 0 for drive actions. For receive actions, the following codes can occur:<br><br>000: No error (expected = received)<br><br>001: Intermediate<br><br>010: 0<br><br>011: 1<br><br>101: Unstable<br><br>110: Reconstruction not possible \|⏎\|  \|  \|  \| bit 3-0: Expected compare/drive action.<br><br>The following values can occur for drive actions:<br><br>1010: Drive to 0, tri-state off<br><br>1011: Drive to 1, tri-state off<br><br>1110: Drive to 0, tri-state on<br><br>1111: Drive to 1, tri-state on<br><br>The following values can occur for receive actions:<br><br>0001: Edge compare to intermediate<br><br>0010: Edge compare to 0<br><br>0011: Edge compare to 1<br><br>0100: Edge compare to mask<br><br>0101: Edge compare to unstable<br><br>0110: Edge compare, reconstruction not possible<br><br>1001: Window compare to intermediate<br><br>1010: Window compare to 0<br><br>1011: Window compare to 1<br><br>1100: Window compare to mask<br><br>1101: Window compare to unstable<br><br>1110: Window compare, reconstruction not possible<br><br>1111: Window close \|⏎\|  \|  \|  \| The codes for "reconstruction not possible" are only returned if the edge failed in single-ended standard mode with one bit results. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98582_2.htm`。` |

Edges without action are not reported. \|⏎\|  \| The data is to be decoded as follows:<br><br>Each cycle of the reported cycle range is preceded by a two byte header information:<br><br>- First byte, bits 7, 6, 5, 4: Always zero, reserved for future use.<br>- First byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Always 1: number of waveforms contained in the device cycle.<br>- Second byte: Number of edge actions following (0 - 140) \| The data is to be decoded as follows:<br><br>Each cycle of the reported cycle range is preceded by a two byte header information:<br><br>- First byte, bits 7, 6, 5, 4: Always zero, reserved for future use.<br>- First byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Always 1: number of waveforms contained in the device cycle.<br>- Second byte: Number of edge actions following (0 - 140) \|⏎\|  \| Each of the following edge actions is described by two bytes:<br><br>- First byte, bit 7: Channel selector for differential pins. The first channel has the value 0, the second the value 1.<br>- First byte, bits 6, 5, 4, 3: Waveform index (always 0)<br>- First byte, bits 2, 1, 0: Edge index (0 - 7)<br>- Second byte, bit 7: Error flag<br><br>0: No error on receive edge, or a drive edge<br><br>1: Error on receive edge. (The system returns this only if err_type is ERON.) In case of a failing window compare, both the window open edge and the window close edge are marked.<br><br>- Second byte, bits 6, 5: Data parameter<br><br>00: Fixed value<br><br>10: Data parameter 1<br><br>11: Data parameter 2<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>- Second byte, bit 4: Edge direction<br><br>0: Drive/tri-state edge<br><br>1: Receive edge<br><br>- Second byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0<br><br>If bit 4 is a 0, then drive/tri-state data:<br><br>Bits 3, 2: Tri-state data<br><br>00: Not used<br><br>10: Tri-state off<br><br>11: Tri-state on<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>Bits 1, 0: Drive data<br><br>00: Not used<br><br>10: Drive 0<br><br>11: Drive 1<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>- Second byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0<br><br>If bit 4 is a 1, then receive data compare method:<br><br>0001: Edge compare to intermediate<br><br>0010: Edge compare to 0<br><br>0011: Edge compare to 1<br><br>0100: Edge compare to mask<br><br>0101: Edge compare to unstable<br><br>0110: Edge compare, reconstruction not possible<br><br>1001: Window compare to intermediate<br><br>1010: Window compare to 0<br><br>1011: Window compare to 1<br><br>1100: Window compare to mask<br><br>1101: Window compare to unstable<br><br>1110: Window compare, reconstruction not possible<br><br>1111: Window close<br><br>0000: Reserved for future use<br><br>0111: Reserved for future use<br><br>1000: Reserved for future use \| Each of the following edge actions is described by two bytes:<br><br>- First byte, bit 7: Channel selector for differential pins. The first channel has the value 0, the second the value 1.<br>- First byte, bits 6, 5, 4, 3: Waveform index (always 0)<br>- First byte, bits 2, 1, 0: Edge index (0 - 7)<br>- Second byte, bit 7: Error flag<br><br>0: No error on receive edge, or a drive edge<br><br>1: Error on receive edge. (The system returns this only if err_type is ERON.) In case of a failing window compare, both the window open edge and the window close edge are marked.<br><br>- Second byte, bits 6, 5: Data parameter<br><br>00: Fixed value<br><br>10: Data parameter 1<br><br>11: Data parameter 2<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>- Second byte, bit 4: Edge direction<br><br>0: Drive/tri-state edge<br><br>1: Receive edge<br><br>- Second byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0<br><br>If bit 4 is a 0, then drive/tri-state data:<br><br>Bits 3, 2: Tri-state data<br><br>00: Not used<br><br>10: Tri-state off<br><br>11: Tri-state on<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>Bits 1, 0: Drive data<br><br>00: Not used<br><br>10: Drive 0<br><br>11: Drive 1<br><br>01: Reserved for future use<br><br>- Second byte, bits 3, 2, 1, 0<br><br>If bit 4 is a 1, then receive data compare method:<br><br>0001: Edge compare to intermediate<br><br>0010: Edge compare to 0<br><br>0011: Edge compare to 1<br><br>0100: Edge compare to mask<br><br>0101: Edge compare to unstable<br><br>0110: Edge compare, reconstruction not possible<br><br>1001: Window compare to intermediate<br><br>1010: Window compare to 0<br><br>1011: Window compare to 1<br><br>1100: Window compare to mask<br><br>1101: Window compare to unstable<br><br>1110: Window compare, reconstruction not possible<br><br>1111: Window close<br><br>0000: Reserved for future use<br><br>0111: Reserved for future use<br><br>1000: Reserved for future use \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98583_2.htm`。` |

For differential pins, the maximum of the specified pin and its counterpart is returned. \|⏎\| type \| ERCG \| Error count global, which requests a sum of all error counts reported by ERCP. No map_mode information is returned. \|⏎\| type \| EOLY \| The Errors OnLY option delivers the numbers of failed cycles per pin from the detailed Error Map. The map_mode is F160. For differential pins, the numbers of those cycles will be returned where at least one of the pins produced an error. \|⏎\| valid_code \| This parameter tells you if the error count the system returns is valid. The system may return: \| This parameter tells you if the error count the system returns is valid. The system may return: \|⏎\| valid_code \| VALD \| The error count is valid. (This reply is always returned in offline mode.) \|⏎\| valid_code \| OVFL \| Signifies error counter overflow. The hardware error counter has a range of 2^31. \|⏎\| no_of_errors \| This parameter returns the total number of results containing errors for one of the specified pins. For the ERCG type, it returns the sum of all the error counts for the individual pins. \| This parameter returns the total number of results containing errors for one of the specified pins. For the ERCG type, it returns the sum of all the error counts for the individual pins. \|⏎\| map_mode \| The system returns the Error Map mode. The number of cycles making up the window is therefore negative.<br><br>In cases where cycle-boundary crossing takes place, the window_len parameter is useful when re-constructing in which cycle the error actually occurred. \| Window length is an optional parameter returned only if you have specified the error type EXP, REC or ALL and the cycle containing an error occurred during a window compare. This will usually be the case when testing in X mode. \|⏎\| data_type \| Data Type optional parameter. Returned only if the selected data type is EXP, REC or ALL. | 220 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.02.30_PASS__-__PASS_or_fail_result.md` | `98681_2.htm` | #0 | 5×3 | 11 | 4 | `\| pin_list \| The last test result is provided for the pins specified in pin_list. If more than one pin list is included, the names must be separated by a comma.<br><br>Normally, only O or IO pins in the list are taken into account; I pins are ignored. However, if any sequencer program of the setup contains a[MEAS](<02.06.02.02.160_MEAS_-__execute_a_MEASurement_set.md>) instruction (see the [SQPG](<02.06.02.02.237_SQPG__SQPG__-__SeQuencer_ProGramming.md>) command), and the measurement set that is executed by this instruction is defined for an I pin, then the measurement result for this pin will be included in the reply of PASS? if the I pin is specified in pin_list. \| The last test result is provided for the pins specified in pin_list. If more than one pin list is included, the names must be separated by a comma.<br><br>Normally, only O or IO pins in the list are taken into account; I pins are ignored. However, if any sequencer program of the setup contains a[MEAS](<02.06.02.02.160_MEAS_-__execute_a_MEASurement_set.md>) instruction (see the [SQPG](<02.06.02.02.237_SQPG__SQPG__-__SeQuencer_ProGramming.md>) command), and the measurement set that is executed by this instruction is defined for an I pin, then the measurement result for this pin will be included in the reply of PASS? if the I pin is specified in pin_list. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| port_list \| The last test result is provided for the pins of a port specified in port_list. If more than one port is included, the names must be separated by a comma. \| The last test result is provided for the pins of a port specified in port_list. If more than one port is included, the names must be separated by a comma. \|⏎\| Pins \| The last test result is provided per each pin specified in pins individually. If more than one pin is included , the names must be separated by a comma. \| The last test result is provided per each pin specified in pins individually. If more than one pin is included , the names must be separated by a comma. \|⏎\| res_flag \| F \| If this result is returned, at least one pin in pin_list or port failed the last test. In the case of per pin query (using PPIN command), a fail is returned if the individual pin specified in pins failed the last test. \|⏎\| res_flag \| P \| If this result is returned, all pins in pin_list or port passed the last test. In the case of per pin query (using PPIN command) , a pass is returned if the individual pin specified in pins passed the last test. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98681_2.htm`。` |
| 221 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.02.31_SQGB__SQGB__-__SeQuencer_GloBal.md` | `98746_4.htm` | #0 | 14×3 | 28 | 14 | `\| mode \| Possible values are: \| Possible values are: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| mode \| ACQF \| ACQuire Failure mode. Only failed cycles are recorded, starting after analyze_delay cycles. If analyze_delay is not a multiple of 8, recording starts after the nearest smaller multiple of 8. In this mode the Error Map is called the Acquire Failure Map.<br><br>As the test proceeds the Acquire Failure Map is filled with failure data until the test stops or the map becomes completely full, whichever occurs first. If the map stops recording before the test has finished, the sequencer, which is in standard mode, continues executing the test until the end.<br><br>This mode is only supported for compatibility reasons. Instead, you should use DCGM and DCRP to configure the Error Map. The effect of ACQF is reproduced by:<br><br>DCGM FAIL,STRT,NONE<br><br>DCRP EMAP,<error_map_pos>,<ref_port> \|⏎\| mode \| ACFF \| This mode is functionally identical to ACQF. It is obsolete and should not be used in new applications. \|⏎\| mode \| EL \| EndLess. The sequencer repeats the complete test, including primaries and multiport synchronization, in an endless loop and can only be stopped by Break.<br><br>The analyze_delay parameter is ignored. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented mode.<br><br>This mode is used for oscilloscope tests. The Error Map functions as a rolling buffer. After stopping the sequencer, the Error Map shows the last "buffer-full" of recorded cycles. \|⏎\| mode \| ELPT \| EndLess PaTtern. The sequencer repeats only the user program. This avoids critical holds during measurement.<br><br>In case of multiport testing, ports may run with different frequencies. Using the ELPT mode in this case, can lead to the situation, that for loop counts >1 the synchronization is no longer executed. \|⏎\| mode \| EMPS \| Error Map PoSitioning. The Error Map is in cycle oriented mode, the sequencer in default mode. The analyze_delay parameter specifies the end of the recorded area. If it is not a multiple of 8, the last cycle to be displayed is the largest multiple of 8 that is smaller than analyze_delay. The first cycle to be displayed is determined by the size of the Error Map (8k cycles).<br><br>This mode is only supported for compatibility reasons. Instead, you should use DCGM and DCRP to configure the Error Map. The effect of EMPS is reproduced by:<br><br>DCGM ALL,HALT,NONE<br><br>DCRP EMAP,<error_map_pos>,<ref_port> \|⏎\| mode \| SE \| Stop on Error. The sequencer runs from the beginning of the test and stops when an error is encountered. If no errors occur, the sequencer runs to the end of the test and stops. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM. The analyze_delay parameter is ignored. \|⏎\| mode \| SEEL \| Stop on Error and EndLess. The sequencer repeats the test in an endless loop, unless an error is encountered, whereupon it stops. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode. This mode is used to locate sporadic failures. \|⏎\| mode \| STSQ \| STop SeQuencer. Stops the sequencer after analyze_delay cycles. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM. See also the query [GETC? - (GET Cycles)](<02.06.02.02.131_GETC__-__GET_Cycles.md>) for the analyze_delay count. \|⏎\| mode \| STRD \| In the query reply, this mode is displayed if the current combination of recording mode, as set with DCGM, and interpretation of the Error Map position, as set with DCRP, is different from all the other SQGB modes. For example, the Error Map can be in cycle oriented recording mode, and analyze_delay be interpreted as a start value.<br><br>In the command, this option leaves it to SmarTest to select the appropriate recording mode (cycle oriented or acquire failure), unless the recording mode has been explicitly set with DCGM. \|⏎\| mode \| STSV \| STop Sequencer on Vector. Stops the sequencer at the logical vector number specified in the analyze_delay parameter before executing the vector. The Error Map is set to cycle oriented recording mode, but this can be changed with DCGM.<br><br>Note that the STSV option cannot be used if no start label has been defined with SQSL, or if the start label is a burst or multi-port burst label. \|⏎\| analyze_delay \| Sets the analyze delay. This parameter is mandatory for the STSQ and STSV modes. Its interpretation depends on the selected mode parameter. The number must be between 1 (0 for ACQF) and 281,474,976,710,655 = 2^48 - 1 (2^48 - 2 for ACQF).<br><br>If the mode parameter is set to STSV, three cases must be distinguished:<br><br>- If analyze_delay is an existing logical vector number of the actual start label, the sequencer will be stopped at this logical vector before executing the vector.<br>- If analyze_delay specifies the total number of logical vectors (= greatest logical vector number of the test + 1) the sequencer stops at the last valid vector.<br>- If analyze_delay is greater than the total number of logical vectors, an error message is issued. \| Sets the analyze delay. This parameter is mandatory for the STSQ and STSV modes. Its interpretation depends on the selected mode parameter. The number must be between 1 (0 for ACQF) and 281,474,976,710,655 = 2^48 - 1 (2^48 - 2 for ACQF).<br><br>If the mode parameter is set to STSV, three cases must be distinguished:<br><br>- If analyze_delay is an existing logical vector number of the actual start label, the sequencer will be stopped at this logical vector before executing the vector.<br>- If analyze_delay specifies the total number of logical vectors (= greatest logical vector number of the test + 1) the sequencer stops at the last valid vector.<br>- If analyze_delay is greater than the total number of logical vectors, an error message is issued. \|⏎\| ref_port \| This parameter accepts a single port. It must be specified in the following situation:<br><br>- The selected mode requires an analyze delay (but is different from STSV), and<br>- the start label is a multi-port burst.<br><br>It specifies the port which is used for counting the cycles specified with the analyze_delay parameter. When this reference port has completed the specified number of cycles, the sequencers will be stopped, or cycle recording started or stopped, for all ports. Note that the reference port must belong to the current start label (as defined by SQSL). \| This parameter accepts a single port. It must be specified in the following situation:<br><br>- The selected mode requires an analyze delay (but is different from STSV), and<br>- the start label is a multi-port burst.<br><br>It specifies the port which is used for counting the cycles specified with the analyze_delay parameter. When this reference port has completed the specified number of cycles, the sequencers will be stopped, or cycle recording started or stopped, for all ports. Note that the reference port must belong to the current start label (as defined by SQSL). \|⏎\| port_list \| If the current mode requires an analyze delay, you can specify a port list in the query. The reply will give the calculated analyze delays for those ports that are not used as the reference port. These delays are derived from the analyze delay of the reference port and the ratio between the period lengths of the reference port and the other port in the primary timing setup. If you specify @@, SQGB will return the settings for all ports of the current start label. \| If the current mode requires an analyze delay, you can specify a port list in the query. The reply will give the calculated analyze delays for those ports that are not used as the reference port. These delays are derived from the analyze delay of the reference port and the ratio between the period lengths of the reference port and the other port in the primary timing setup. If you specify @@, SQGB will return the settings for all ports of the current start label. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98746_4.htm`。` |
| 222 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.02.33_VALD__-__VALid_Data.md` | `98841_2.htm` | #0 | 14×3 | 28 | 14 | `\| map_id \| Determines the type of Error Map for which data is to be read out: \| Determines the type of Error Map for which data is to be read out: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| map_id \| ERRM \| The error map option reads the error map. \|⏎\| map_id \| OVEM \| The overview error map option reads the overview error map in FCM mode (defined by DCRM). If the Overview Error Map was in LMAP mode for the last executed test, an error message will be issued. \|⏎\| map_id \| LMAP \| The label map option reads the overview error map in LMAP mode (defined by DCRM). If the overview error map was in FCM mode for the last executed test, an error message will be issued if LMAP is selected.<br><br>Note If you use the LMAP error map mode, [FLBL](<02.06.02.02.123_FLBL__-__Failing_LaBeLs.md>) ignores the RETC clearing flag for failures. \|⏎\| map_id \| RECD \| The receive data option reads the error map (compatibility with the [ERMP](<02.06.02.02.114_ERMP__-__ERror_MaP.md>) query). Equivalent to ERRM. \|⏎\| map_id \| DERR \| The per pin detailed error map option reads the detailed error map. Equivalent to ERRM. \|⏎\| map_id \| EOLY \| The errors only map option reads the per pin errors only in the error map. Equivalent to ERRM. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Error map information will only be returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. All pins in pinlist must be O or IO pin types. \| Error map information will only be returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated with a comma. All pins in pinlist must be O or IO pin types. \|⏎\| map_state \| Returns the current state of the error map. The state can be: \| Returns the current state of the error map. The state can be: \|⏎\| map_state \| INVL \| Invalid is returned if no error map details are available (the setup has been changed, but no functional test has been performed). \|⏎\| map_state \| INCS \| This is returned if the contents of the error map are inconsistent with the current setup. \|⏎\| map_state \| CONS \| This is returned after a functional test has been performed and indicates that the contents of the error map are consistent with the current setup. \|⏎\| start \| This is an integer that indicates the first cycle that was recorded, or, for the LMAP option of map_id, the first label call that was recorded. start will be a value between 0 and 2^48. \| This is an integer that indicates the first cycle that was recorded, or, for the LMAP option of map_id, the first label call that was recorded. start will be a value between 0 and 2^48. \|⏎\| number \| This is an integer that indicates the number of cycles recorded, or for the LMAP option of map_id, the number of label calls recorded. If ERRM, RECD or DERR have been selected, the range for this parameter depends on whether the internal or the external error map is used. For the internal error map, it is 0 to 8191; for the external error map the upper limit can be higher. When EOLY has been selected, the upper limit is 2^48.<br><br>For the [Pin Scale 5000 card](<../02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.04.02_Pin_Scale_5000_card.md>), the upper limit is 4096.<br><br>In the reply, this parameter indicates the number of cycles (or label calls) which have been read. If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \| This is an integer that indicates the number of cycles recorded, or for the LMAP option of map_id, the number of label calls recorded. If ERRM, RECD or DERR have been selected, the range for this parameter depends on whether the internal or the external error map is used. For the internal error map, it is 0 to 8191; for the external error map the upper limit can be higher. When EOLY has been selected, the upper limit is 2^48.<br><br>For the [Pin Scale 5000 card](<../02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.04.02_Pin_Scale_5000_card.md>), the upper limit is 4096.<br><br>In the reply, this parameter indicates the number of cycles (or label calls) which have been read. If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98841_2.htm`。` |
| 223 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.02.37_VRCS__-__Valid_ReConStruction.md` | `98861_2.htm` | #0 | 18×3 | 46 | 8 | `\| reconstr_type \| The reconstruction type determines how test cycles are counted. \| The reconstruction type determines how test cycles are counted. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| reconstr_type \| STRT \| Start analysis. Specifies that cycles are counted from the start of the test. \|⏎\| reconstr_type \| STOP \| Stop analysis. Specifies that cycles are counted from the end of the test. \|⏎\| reconstr_cond \| The reconstruction condition defines how VRCS? reacts to ambiguous sequencer instructions, that is, instructions that may generate a varying number of cycles from one test execution to the next. Examples for such instructions are JMPE or multiple counted match loops. \| The reconstruction condition defines how VRCS? reacts to ambiguous sequencer instructions, that is, instructions that may generate a varying number of cycles from one test execution to the next. Examples for such instructions are JMPE or multiple counted match loops. \|⏎\|  \| STRD \| Reconstruction stops at the first ambiguous instruction. The result may be incomplete, but is reliable. \|⏎\|  \| FULL \| Reconstruction continues even if ambiguous instructions are present. The maximum possible number of cycles is assumed for the ambiguous instructions. The result may be complete, but possible not completely reliable. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. pinlist can include pins configured as I, O and IO types. \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. pinlist can include pins configured as I, O and IO types. \|⏎\| reconstr_state \| This parameter returns the state of the reconstruction. \| This parameter returns the state of the reconstruction. \|⏎\|  \| INVL \| If INVL is returned, no valid cycles exist. This occurs if you have hit BREAK before any vectors are generated. \|⏎\|  \| PRED \| If PRED is returned, all returned values are predicted (a change to the setup has been made, but a test has not yet been executed). \|⏎\|  \| REAL \| If REAL is returned, all values given are values from the last executed test. \|⏎\| reconstr_oper \| OKAY \| The reconstruction was successful, no problems encountered. \|⏎\|  \| MMRV \| Multiple Matchloop Restriction Violation:<br><br>The reconstruction stopped at the first match loop because there are multiple match loops in the pattern, and the match loop execution counter, which is needed for the reconstruction, can only hold the count from the last match loop execution. \|⏎\|  \| JMPE \| The reconstruction stopped at a jump on error instruction because execution path of the sequencer is unknown (for example, whether the jump was taken or not). \|⏎\|  \| RETC \| The reconstruction stopped at a return conditional instruction because execution path of the sequencer is unknown (for example, whether the jump was taken or not).<br><br>Note If you use the LMAP error map mode, [FLBL](<02.06.02.02.123_FLBL__-__Failing_LaBeLs.md>) ignores the RETC clearing flag for failures. \|⏎\|  \| ABRT \| The pattern was empty or there was an unspecified problem during the reconstruction. \|⏎\| start_cycle \| The start cycle is an integer which identifies the first cycle for which results exist. \| The start cycle is an integer which identifies the first cycle for which results exist. \|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This parameter is an integer which defines the total number of valid cycles. \| This parameter is an integer which defines the total number of valid cycles. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98861_2.htm`。` |
| 224 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.02.38_WFEX__-__WaveForm_EXpected_data.md` | `98873_2.htm` | #0 | 39×3 | 68 | 49 | `\| start_cycle \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle for which information is to be returned. The scanning method and the range of values this parameter can take depend on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode, enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis mode, enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for stop-on-error tests. \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle for which information is to be returned. The scanning method and the range of values this parameter can take depend on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode, enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis mode, enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for stop-on-error tests. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles for which information is to be returned. The range for this parameter is 0 to 2^48.<br><br>If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles for which information is to be returned. The range for this parameter is 0 to 2^48.<br><br>If a 0 is returned, no data is available for the given range. \|⏎\| timing_set \| This parameter is an integer that represents the timing set used in the given cycle range. \| This parameter is an integer that represents the timing set used in the given cycle range. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. You can use the @ character to read the error data for all pins. \| Information is only returned for the pins specified in pinlist. If more than one pin is to be included, the names must be separated by a comma. You can use the @ character to read the error data for all pins. \|⏎\| err_type \| This parameter is always of the type NOER since this query only reports expected data. \| This parameter is always of the type NOER since this query only reports expected data. \|⏎\| err_type \| NOER \| Error information is not available. The reported range lies outside the range covered by the Error Map. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| Edge action data in binary format. This parameter reports the length of data (in bytes) being transferred, followed by the reconstructed data. The data is returned for each pin separately. For each cycle of the reported cycle range 6 bytes are returned. Each byte of reconstructed data is to be decoded as follows: \| Edge action data in binary format. This parameter reports the length of data (in bytes) being transferred, followed by the reconstructed data. The data is returned for each pin separately. For each cycle of the reported cycle range 6 bytes are returned. Each byte of reconstructed data is to be decoded as follows: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 7: Error flag. \| bit 7: Error flag. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| No error on receive edge, or a drive / unused edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Cannot occur since only expected data are reported. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 6, 5: Data parameter \| bits 6, 5: Data parameter \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Fixed value \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Data parameter 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Data parameter 2 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 4: Edge direction \| bit 4: Edge direction \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| Drive / tri-state edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Receive edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Edge action \| bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Edge action \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| If bit 4 is a 0, then drive / tri-state data: \| If bit 4 is a 0, then drive / tri-state data: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 3, 2: Tri-state data \| bits 3, 2: Tri-state data \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Tri-state off \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Tri-state on \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 1, 0: Drive data \| bits 1, 0: Drive data \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Drive 0 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Drive 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| If bit 4 is a 1, then expected data: \| If bit 4 is a 1, then expected data: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 3: Compare type \| bit 3: Compare type \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| Edge compare \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Window compare \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 2, 1, 0: Compare method \| bits 2, 1, 0: Compare method \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 010 \| Compare to 0 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 011 \| Compare to 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 001 \| Compare to intermediate \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 100 \| Compare to mask \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 101 \| Compare to unstable \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 000 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 111 \| Window close \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98873_2.htm`。` |

Each of these bytes has to be decoded as follows: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 7: Error flag. \| bit 7: Error flag. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| No error on receive edge, or a drive or unused edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Error on receive edge. (The system returns this only if err_type is ERON). In case of a failing window compare, both the window open edge and the window close edge are marked. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 6, 5: Data parameter \| bits 6, 5: Data parameter \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Fixed value \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Data parameter 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Data parameter 2 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 4: Edge direction \| bit 4: Edge direction \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| Drive / tri-state edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Receive edge \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Edge action \| bits 3, 2, 1, 0: Edge action \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| If bit 4 is a 0, then drive / tri-state data: \| If bit 4 is a 0, then drive / tri-state data: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 3, 2: Tri-state data \| bits 3, 2: Tri-state data \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Tri-state off \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Tri-state on \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 1, 0: Drive data \| bits 1, 0: Drive data \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 10 \| Drive 0 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 11 \| Drive 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 00 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| If bit 4 is a 1, then receive data: \| If bit 4 is a 1, then receive data: \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bit 3: Compare type \| bit 3: Compare type \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 0 \| Edge compare \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 1 \| Window compare \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| bits 2, 1, 0: Compare method \| bits 2, 1, 0: Compare method \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 010 \| Compare to 0 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 011 \| Compare to 1 \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 001 \| Compare to intermediate \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 100 \| Compare to mask \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 101 \| Compare to unstable \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 000 \| Not used \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 110 \| Reconstruction not possible \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| 111 \| Window close \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98874_2.htm`。` |

| 228 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.14.02.02_SSCT__SSCT__-__Source_Synchronous_data__Combine_and_Transfer.md` | `98756_2.htm` | #0 | 11×3 | 21 | 12 | `\| exec_mode \| Possible execution modes are: \| Possible execution modes are: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| exec_mode \| IMM \| The command is executed immediately. \|⏎\| exec_mode \| ACC \| Command execution is delayed to the next FTST? call or the next SSCT IMM call (whichever comes first). \|⏎\| exec_mode \| DEL \| Deletes all SSCT instances that ar still pending for the specified destination pin and source pins. The other parameters are ignored. \|⏎\| read_mode \| Possible read modes are: \| Possible read modes are: \|⏎\| read_mode \| NORM \| Source data is processed as it is stored in the source channels. This is the default. \|⏎\| read_mode \| INV \| Source data is inverted internally before it is processed. The source data itself is not changed. \|⏎\| read_mode \| Note that the read modes of SSAC and SSCT are concatenated. If inversion has been set for a source channel with SSAC, and the read mode of SSCT is also set to INV then the data of the respective channel will be inverted twice, and therefore processed as it is stored. \| Note that the read modes of SSAC and SSCT are concatenated. If inversion has been set for a source channel with SSAC, and the read mode of SSCT is also set to INV then the data of the respective channel will be inverted twice, and therefore processed as it is stored. \|⏎\| no_of_results \| Specifies the number of results to be processed. The default is all results (as defined with SSAC). The maximum is the number of results that are configured by SSAC.<br><br>The number to be specified here must be calculated as `result_granularity * number_of_cycles`.<br><br>The result granularity is the number of results per cycle. It can be 1 (one global result), in which case you must specify the number of cycles, or 8 (one result for every edge), in which case you must specify `8 * number_of_cycles`.<br><br>The number of bytes required to store the specified number of results must be a multiple of 8. If this requirement is not satisfied, the number of results will be auto-corrected. \| Specifies the number of results to be processed. The default is all results (as defined with SSAC). The maximum is the number of results that are configured by SSAC.<br><br>The number to be specified here must be calculated as `result_granularity * number_of_cycles`.<br><br>The result granularity is the number of results per cycle. It can be 1 (one global result), in which case you must specify the number of cycles, or 8 (one result for every edge), in which case you must specify `8 * number_of_cycles`.<br><br>The number of bytes required to store the specified number of results must be a multiple of 8. If this requirement is not satisfied, the number of results will be auto-corrected. \|⏎\| dest_pin \| The target buffer pin into which the combined results are transferred. This should be an input pin, in order to avoid conflicts within the result area between the buffer function and pin specific result data. This pin must have been defined as a buffer pin with SSAC.<br><br>Note that the Error Map for this pin must be located in RDRAM (DCRT command). \| The target buffer pin into which the combined results are transferred. This should be an input pin, in order to avoid conflicts within the result area between the buffer function and pin specific result data. This pin must have been defined as a buffer pin with SSAC.<br><br>Note that the Error Map for this pin must be located in RDRAM (DCRT command). \|⏎\| source_pins \| The pins whose results are to be combined. Note that the Error Map for these pins must be located in RDRAM (DCRT command). \| The pins whose results are to be combined. | 231 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.17.05.03_PRCF__PRCF__-__Pseudo_Random_bit_sequence_ConFiguration.md` | `118218_2.htm` | #0 | 8×3 | 16 | 8 | `\| PRBS_set_name \| Specifies the name of a PRBS set that must have been defined with PRDF. \| Specifies the name of a PRBS set that must have been defined with PRDF. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| property \| Specifies a property of the PRBS set. There are two properties to be configured: \| Specifies a property of the PRBS set. There are two properties to be configured: \|⏎\| property \| POLYNOMIAL \| This property defines the connection polynomial of the LFSR. It is coded as a bit string where the position of the bit identifies the degree of the addend, and its value defines the coefficient of this addend. The constant term of the polynomial, which is always 1, must be omitted. The last bit is supposed to determine the degree n of the polynomial. Therefore, the length of the string must be n, and its last bit must be "1". \|⏎\| property \| SEED \| This property defines the seed for the LFSR in the order in which it is output. This property is only required for PRBS generation. The seed for the PRBS check is taken from the received data. \|⏎\| ALL \| This value can be specified in the query to retrieve the configuration data of all PRBS sets. \| This value can be specified in the query to retrieve the configuration data of all PRBS sets. \|⏎\| PRM \| This value can be specified to modify or retrieve the configuration of the current primary PRBS set. \| This value can be specified to modify or retrieve the configuration of the current primary PRBS set. \|⏎\| property_value \| Specify a bit string for this parameter. Its meaning depends on the selected property. For POLYNOMIAL it encodes the connection polynomial. For SEED, it defines the initial state of the LFSR. The maximum length is 32 in both cases.<br><br>- The default for the polynomial is<br><br>11111110111010110011110000100101,<br><br>which is the polynomial<br><br>1+x+x2+x3+x4+x5+x6+x7+x9+x10+x11+x13+x15+x16+x19+x20+x21+x22+x27+x30+x32.<br><br>- The default for the seed is<br><br>00110010011011110001001101100111. \| Specify a bit string for this parameter. Its meaning depends on the selected property. For POLYNOMIAL it encodes the connection polynomial. For SEED, it defines the initial state of the LFSR. The maximum length is 32 in both cases.<br><br>- The default for the polynomial is<br><br>11111110111010110011110000100101,<br><br>which is the polynomial<br><br>1+x+x2+x3+x4+x5+x6+x7+x9+x10+x11+x13+x15+x16+x19+x20+x21+x22+x27+x30+x32.<br><br>- The default for the seed is<br><br>00110010011011110001001101100111. \|⏎\| pinlist \| Specifies the pins to which pin this configuration is to be applied. \| Specifies the pins to which pin this configuration is to be applied. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/118218_2.htm`。` |

| 233 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.18.02.02_ASWM__ASWM__-__Auto_SWing_Mode.md` | `98487_2.htm` | #0 | 8×3 | 15 | 9 | `\| command \| This parameter is used to set the specified mode: \| This parameter is used to set the specified mode: \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| command \| SET \| Sets the mode specified regardless of the previous mode. \|⏎\| command \| MIN \| Sets the 'minimum' mode of either the mode specified or the previous mode. For example, SEARCH is the minimum mode of the two modes SEARCH and AUTO. \|⏎\| command \| MAX \| Sets the 'maximum' mode of either the mode specified or the previous mode. For example, SEARCH is the maximum mode of the two modes SEARCH and FREEZE. \|⏎\| mode \| The mode can take one of the following values: \| The mode can take one of the following values: \|⏎\| mode \| AUTO \| Automatic level swing adjustment is done for all waveform sets. Any change of waveforms or levels triggers an automatic swing adjustment. \|⏎\| mode \| FREEZE \| No swing adjustment is performed. The actual swing adjustment settings are used. | 245 | `02_System_reference/02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.13.01.13_SmartRDI_2.5.0.md` | `343299.htm` | #0 | 7×2 | 13 | 1 | `\| Category \| Description \|⏎\|---\|---\|⏎\| General \| General Features<br><br>- Pure Generation Mode<br><br>See the new keyword PURE_GENERATION_MODE in [RDI_Configure file](<02.07.13.04_RDI_Configure_file.md>).<br><br>- Offline Result Emulation<br><br>See [Using Offline Result Emulation in SmartRDI](<02.07.13.08.20_Using_Offline_Result_Emulation_in_SmartRDI.md>).<br><br>- Pass/Fail per label without break vectors (BFLM mode)<br><br>See [Pass/Fail per label without break vectors](<02.07.13.08.09.02_Pass_Fail_per_label_without_break_vectors.md>).<br><br>- Burst site upload<br><br>See [Controlling burst site upload](<02.07.13.08.04_Controlling_burst_site_upload.md>) \|⏎\| General \| General Improvements<br><br>- Test time is improved with:<br><br>- No PPMU/BADC setup after the 1st run (higher than SmarTest 7.4.2)<br>- DCScale DC & TMU runTimeValue speed-up<br>- `RDI_INIT()` Xput Improvement<br><br>- Changes to parameters default values in [RDI_Configure file](<02.07.13.04_RDI_Configure_file.md>) to enhance compatibility.<br><br>- Change the default value of PPMU_DEFAULT_VALUE_MODE from SA to BADC because: BADC has a better accuracy than SA mode; BADC is specified for current measurement, while SA mode is not; BADC provides better Xput for low pin counts.<br>- Change the default value of PER_LABEL_FUNC_RESULT_MODE from FLBL to ERMP to achieve better throughput, especially for high site counts.<br>- Change the default value of DEFAULT_SMV_READ_MODE from EMAP to DIGCAP for better upload performance.<br>- Change the default value of VEC_MODE_SAMPLING_WAIT from MSET to PATTERN for more accurate sampling wait of the pattern loop for PPMU DC sampling tests in VEC mode. \|⏎\| Digital Capture \| Immediate upload<br><br>See [Using Immediate Upload in SmartRDI](<02.07.13.13.03_Using_Immediate_Upload_in_SmartRDI.md>). \|⏎\| Relay \| runTimeValue for digital relays<br><br>See the new function [setOnRunTime()](<02.07.13.14.06_setOnRunTime.md>), [setOffRunTime()](<02.07.13.14.04_setOffRunTime.md>) and [runTimeVal()](<02.07.13.08.03.01_runTimeVal.md#_144754__relay_control>). \|⏎\| PMUX \| - A new class `route()` to support the routing GUI tool new in SmarTest 7.4.3<br><br>See [SmartRDI: DUT board connections control by Routing Tools](<02.07.13.15_SmartRDI__DUT_board_connections_control_by_Routing_Tools.md>) for details of this new route() class.<br><br>- PMUX status report for debug<br>- Support of the global pin "ALL" in the `mux()` and `module()` classes \|⏎\| Tool \| Get cycle/vector number of a comment in a pattern<br><br>See [getCommentCycleNum()](<02.07.13.22.07_getCommentCycleNum.md>) and [getCommentVectorNum()](<02.07.13.22.08_getCommentVectorNum.md>). \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/343299.htm`。` |

This value can be changed on the message level. \|⏎\| Device reset \| This parameter defines how many frames are to be inserted before the actual boot sequence.<br><br>This attribute is only used for dynamic booting (actually it is a parameter of the PA frame for dynamic booting), but it must also be present if user defined booting is applied. \|⏎\| Device boot-up \| This parameter defines the number of empty superframes that are to be executed before the actual test. These superframes are also stored in the Superframe Viewer file if creation of this file is activated.<br><br>This attribute is only used for dynamic booting, but it must also be present if user defined booting is applied. \|⏎\| Root frequency \| These two parameters define the bus frequency, see section 6.2 of the SLIMbus specification.<br><br>Note that these parameters have no effect on the SmarTest timing. It is the responsibility of the user to ensure that the bus frequency specified here and the SmarTest timing are compatible. \|⏎\| Clock gear \| These two parameters define the bus frequency, see section 6.2 of the SLIMbus specification.<br><br>Note that these parameters have no effect on the SmarTest timing. It is the responsibility of the user to ensure that the bus frequency specified here and the SmarTest timing are compatible. \|⏎\| Subframe mode \| This parameter defines the initial subframe mode. See section 6.3.1.4 of the SLIMbus specification. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/141253.htm`。` |

The selected file becomes the active hardware response file with the name `testsuite; MAPI; call#; site#`. If the file contains a large amount of data such as waveform data, only the first 2048 bytes are shown.<br><br>If SmarTest determines the selected file to be an ASCII file, the contents of the file are displayed as ASCII characters. If a non-printable ASCII character is found in the selected file, the part before it is displayed as ASCII characters, and the part after it is displayed as hexadecimal code (0xXX). \|⏎\| HW Response File \| This field displays the hardware response file name that is assigned to the execution shown in the Testsuite; MAPI; call#; site# field. | 263 | `02_System_reference/02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.34.02.02.04_CDCP__-__Cycle_Device_Cycle_Parameter.md` | `98499_4.htm` | #0 | 23×3 | 46 | 23 | `\| start_cycle \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle to be analyzed. The range of values for this parameter depends on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \| This is an integer that defines the first cycle to be analyzed. The range of values for this parameter depends on the analysis mode:<br><br>- In the Start Analysis mode enter a positive value between 0 and 2^48. The system responds by counting cycles forwards from the first cycle of the test and returns a positive number. The Start Analysis mode is the default setting.<br>- In the Stop Analysis enter a negative number between -2^48 and -1. The system responds by counting cycles backwards relative to the end of the test at -1 cycle and returns a negative number. This mode is used by the system for Stop-on-Error tests. \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| cycle \| In the query, the consecutive number of the displayed cycle. \| In the query, the consecutive number of the displayed cycle. \|⏎\| no_of_cycles \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles to be analyzed. The range for this parameter is 0 to 1000. \| This is an integer that defines the number of cycles to be analyzed. The range for this parameter is 0 to 1000. \|⏎\| strt_disp \| This parameter defines the offset of your range of interest from start_cycle. The actual display starts with the last device cycle that does not begin before start_cycle and does not end before start_cycle + strt_disp.<br><br>The purpose of this construction is to enable a correct device cycle reconstruction even if the first cycle you are interested in does not lie at a device cycle boundary (a device cycle can generate up to 16 cycles). \| This parameter defines the offset of your range of interest from start_cycle. The actual display starts with the last device cycle that does not begin before start_cycle and does not end before start_cycle + strt_disp.<br><br>The purpose of this construction is to enable a correct device cycle reconstruction even if the first cycle you are interested in does not lie at a device cycle boundary (a device cycle can generate up to 16 cycles). \|⏎\| disp_length \| Specifies the display length in cycles. This parameter determines the end of your range of interest. The actual display stops with the last device cycle that starts before start_cycle + strt_disp + disp_length.<br><br>If this parameter is larger than no_of_cycles, it is ignored. \| Specifies the display length in cycles. This parameter determines the end of your range of interest. The actual display stops with the last device cycle that starts before start_cycle + strt_disp + disp_length.<br><br>If this parameter is larger than no_of_cycles, it is ignored. \|⏎\| pinlist \| The pin name or names for which a device cycle data transfer is to be made. If more than one pin is required, the names must be separated with a comma. The @ character can be used to download data for all configured pins. \| The pin name or names for which a device cycle data transfer is to be made. If more than one pin is required, the names must be separated with a comma. The @ character can be used to download data for all configured pins. \|⏎\| dev_cyc_name \| A reply parameter, giving the name of the reconstructed device cycle. \| A reply parameter, giving the name of the reconstructed device cycle. \|⏎\| dev_cyc_len \| A reply parameter, giving the length of the reconstructed device cycle. For SmarTest 7.10 this is always 1. \| A reply parameter, giving the length of the reconstructed device cycle. For SmarTest 7.10 this is always 1. \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| Drive Data parameters. They contain values for both drive data and tri-state. The values the system returns are: \| Drive Data parameters. They contain values for both drive data and tri-state. The values the system returns are: \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F00 \| drive 0, tri-state off \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F10 \| drive 1, tri-state off \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F0Z \| drive 0, tri-state on \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| F1Z \| drive 1, tri-state on \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| N \| Not used. Returned if no drive data parameter is set to a drive and tri-state value. \|⏎\| DD1 and DD2 \| UC \| Unclear. The system may return this value to indicate that the interpretation of the device cycle for that parameter is unclear, that is, the physical waveforms are inconsistent for the device cycle. \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| Expected Data Parameters. The values the system returns are: \| Expected Data Parameters. The values the system returns are: \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| F0 \| compare to 0 \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| F1 \| compare to 1 \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FI \| compare to intermediate \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FX \| compare to mask \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| FU \| compare to unstable \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| N \| Not used. Returned if no expected data parameter is set to a compare value. \|⏎\| ED1 and ED2 \| UC \| Unclear. In a multi-port setup, the same variable name can occur among the spec variables of more than one port within the same specification. For basic information on the local and global scope of spec variables in a multi-port setup see [Global and local scope of base variables](<../02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.06.01.05.03.01_Global_and_local_scope_of_base_variables.md>). \| The name of a base (spec) variable. In a multi-port setup, the same variable name can occur among the spec variables of more than one port within the same specification. In this case, you can qualify the variable name by a port name in the following format<br><br><spec_name>@<port_name>,<br><br>if you want to modify the variable on that port only.<br><br>Note, however, that the scope of the variable will be set to local for the port in question after such a modification (even if it is set to global in the setup), until the setup is downloaded again.<br><br>Note also that in a multi-port setup, you cannot change the value of a variable globally (that is, without specifying a port) unless at least one of the variable instances has its scope set to global in the setup. For basic information on the local and global scope of spec variables in a multi-port setup see [Global and local scope of base variables](<../02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.06.01.05.03.01_Global_and_local_scope_of_base_variables.md>). \|⏎\| spec_or_equ_name \| The name of a base (spec) or derived (equation) variable. An equation variable cannot be specified in the command or in a query which uses the extra parameter. \| The name of a base (spec) or derived (equation) variable. An equation variable cannot be specified in the command or in a query which uses the extra parameter. \|⏎\| ALL \| Requires that the actual values of both base and derived variables are returned. \| Requires that the actual values of both base and derived variables are returned. \|⏎\| EQUA \| Requires that the actual values of only the derived (equation) variables are returned. \| Requires that the actual values of only the derived (equation) variables are returned. \|⏎\| MAX \| Requires that the maximum values of all the base (spec) variables are returned.<br><br>This keyword can also be (spec) variables are returned.<br><br>This keyword can also be specified as an extra parameter in combination with the spec_name parameter to retrieve only the maximum value of the specified spec variable. \|⏎\| MIN \| Requires that the maximum of all the base (spec) variables are returned.<br><br>This keyword can also be specified as an extra parameter in combination with the spec_name parameter to retrieve only the minimum value of the specified spec variable. \| Requires that the maximum of all the base (spec) variables are returned.<br><br>This keyword can also be specified as an extra parameter in combination with the spec_name parameter to retrieve only the minimum value of the specified spec variable. \|⏎\| SPEC \| Requires that the actual values of only the base (spec) variables are returned. \| Requires that the actual values of only the base (spec) variables are returned. \|⏎\| UNIT \| Requires that the units of all the base (spec) variables are returned.<br><br>This keyword can also be specified as an extra parameter in combination with the spec_name parameter to retrieve only the unit of the specified spec variable. \| Requires that the units of all the base (spec) variables are returned.<br><br>This keyword can also be specified as an extra parameter in combination with the spec_name parameter to retrieve only the unit of the specified spec variable. \|⏎\| value \| Float with 5 possible decimal places. \| Float with 5 possible decimal places. \|⏎\| pinlist \| By this extra parameter, you can modify the runtime definitions for an individual pin. Note that you cannot retrieve the pin specific values with SVLR?.<br><br>Note You can use the pinlist parameter to modify the value of a spec variable of an individual pin, temporarily. If you make a new modification on a different spec variable, only the last modification is kept while the previous spec variable is reset to the setup value defined in the spec file. To make a permanent modification on a spec variable, specify all pins (that is, @) as the pinlist. Otherwise, to change the value of a spec variable of an individual pin permanently, you have to use a specset for each pin and modify the spec variable in that specset. \| By this extra parameter, you can modify the runtime definitions for an individual pin. Note that you cannot retrieve the pin specific values with SVLR?.<br><br>Note You can use the pinlist parameter to modify the value of a spec variable of an individual pin, temporarily. If you make a new modification on a different spec variable, only the last modification is kept while the previous spec variable is reset to the setup value defined in the spec file. To make a permanent modification on a spec variable, specify all pins (that is, @) as the pinlist. Otherwise, to change the value of a spec variable of an individual pin permanently, you have to use a specset for each pin and modify the spec variable in that specset. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/98764_4.htm`。` |

\|  \| Driver asks handler for received bin and reprobe information. \|⏎\|  \| bin,bin,... \| Handler sends bin data back to driver. \|⏎\| releaseparts \|  \| Driver acknowledges correct binning data (if correct), or starts sending test results from the beginning (max. repeat count: verify_retry_count). \|⏎\| stop handler: \| stop handler: \| stop handler: \|⏎\| status? \|  \| Asks handler for current status. \|⏎\|  \| n \| Handler returns bit-coded status information. \|⏎\| stop \|  \| Stops handler, only if received status was started. \|⏎\| status? \|  \| Asks handler for status again. \|⏎\|  \| n \| Handler returns bit-coded status information (should now be stopped). \|⏎\| start handler: \| start handler: \| start handler: \|⏎\| status? \|  \| Asks handler for current status. \|⏎\|  \| n \| Handler returns bit-coded status information. \|⏎\| start \|  \| Starts handler, only if received status was stopped. \|⏎\| status? \|  \| Asks handler for status again. \|⏎\|  \| n \| Handler returns bit-coded status information (should now be started). \|⏎\| status request: \| status request: \| status request: \|⏎\| status? \|  \| Asks handler for current status. \|⏎\|  \| n \| Handler returns bit-coded status information. \|⏎\| query status:<br><br>`<query site_mapping>`<br><br>`<query temperature_airtemp>`<br><br>`<query temperature_cool>`<br><br>`<query temperature_desoaktime>`<br><br>`<query temperature_heat>`<br><br>`<query temperature_lowerguardband>`<br><br>`<query temperature_masstemp>`<br><br>`<query temperature_setpoint>`<br><br>`<query temperature_soaktime>`<br><br>`<query temperature_tempcontrol>`<br><br>`<query temperature_temperature>`<br><br>`<query temperature_tempstatus>`<br><br>`<query temperature_testtemp>`<br><br>`<query temperature_upperguardband>` \|  \| Query the handler status by PH call ph_ge`t_status`. | 280 | `02_System_reference/02.09_Test_Program_Management/02.09.01.03.06.01.08.02.01_GPIB_communication_flow_for_Delta_Eclipse_handlers.md` | `344758.htm` | #0 | 14×3 | 36 | 6 | `\| Message from driver \| Message from handler \| Description \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| Query for handler status: \| Query for handler status: \| Query for handler status: \|⏎\| `FR?` \|  \| The driver asks for the status. \|⏎\|  \| `FR`n \| The handler returns `FR0` or `FR1`.<br><br>FR0: No devices at the contactor.<br><br>FR1: Parts at the contactor are ready for testing.<br><br>The tester then queries `FULLSITES?` to the handler if parts are at the contactor for testing. \|⏎\| Site population: \| Site population: \| Site population: \|⏎\| `FULLSITES?` \|  \| The driver asks the handler whether any device is ready for testing at the contactor or not. \|⏎\|  \| `Fullsites xxxxxxxx` \| The handler returns a string that represents devices at the test site.<br><br>For example, a string Fullsites xxxxxxxx, where each x character represents four test sites. Each x character is a hexadecimal number that uses four bits to indicate if devices are present. Therefore, if four devices are present, the x value will be F which is 1111 in binary. The devices at test sites 1, 3, and 4 are 1011 in binary or B in hexadecimal.<br><br>The response must contain all eight x characters representing the 32 existing test sites.<br><br>If four devices are present in a four test site configuration, the handler responses Fullsites 00000000F. \|⏎\| Bin devices: \| Bin devices: \| Bin devices: \|⏎\| `BINON:xxxxxxxx,xxxxxxxx,xxxxxxxx,xxxxxxxx;` (bins 0 to 15) \|  \| The driver sends the test results to the handler. The command requires the handler to echo back the result string with the ECHO command for verification.<br><br>All 32 characters are required in the command string. Each x character represents the bin assignment for a single test site.<br><br>The valid bin characters are 0 to F hexadecimal numbers which are bins 0 to 15. 0 indicates no device on the socket. \|⏎\|  \| `ECHO:xxxxxxxx,xxxxxxxx,xxxxxxxx,xxxxxxxx;` (bins 0 to 15) \| The handler returns an ECHO result string to the tester for validation. The results will not bin out at this time until an ECHOOK is received from the tester. \|⏎\| `BINON:xyxyxyxyxyxyxyxy,xyxyxyxyxyxyxyxy, xyxyxyxyxyxyxyxy, xyxyxyxyxyxyxyxy;` (bins 0 to 255) \|  \| The driver sends the test results to the handler. The command requires the handler to echo back the results string with the ECHO command for verification.<br><br>32 test sites are represented by the two character xy in the command syntax string. All 64 characters are required in the command string.<br><br>The characters are grouped into eight sets that are separated by a comma. Each pair of xy characters represents the bin assignment for a single test site. The valid bin characters are 0 to FF hexadecimal numbers which represents bins 0 to 255. 0 indicates that the socket has no device.<br><br>For example, bin 01 = bin 1, bin FF = bin 255, bin AE = bin 174. \|⏎\|  \| `ECHO:xyxyxyxyxyxyxyxy,xyxyxyxyxyxyxyxy, xyxyxyxyxyxyxyxy, xyxyxyxyxyxyxyxy;` (bins 0 to 255) \| The handler returns an ECHO result string to the tester for validation. The results will not bin out at this time until an ECHOOK is received from the tester. \|⏎\| `ECHOOK` \|  \| The driver confirms that the bin information is correct. That means the BINON and ECHO strings are matched. \|⏎\| `ECHONG` \|  \| The echoed bin data is not correct.<br><br>The handler resends a `BINON` command and waits for an `ECHO`. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/344758.htm`。` |
| 281 | `02_System_reference/02.09_Test_Program_Management/02.09.01.03.06.01.08.02.02_Auto-retest_communication_flow_for_Eclipse_handlers.md` | `351739.htm` | #0 | 53×3 | 143 | 16 | `\| Message from driver \| Message from handler \| Description \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| Start of a normal lot (first pass): \| Start of a normal lot (first pass): \| Start of a normal lot (first pass): \|⏎\| `VERSION?` \|  \| The tester sends the query to the handler. \|⏎\|  \| `xxxx x.x.x` \| The handler replies, for example, "`Eclipse 0.2.4.10`." \|⏎\| `FR?` \|  \| The tester asks if the handler is running. \|⏎\|  \| `FR n` \| The handler replies, for example, "`FR 0`." \|⏎\| `DL 0` \|  \| The tester sends the `DL 0` command. \|⏎\| `SRQMASK FF` \|  \| The tester sends the `SRQMASK FF` command to mask the status byte. \|⏎\| `LOTCLEAR?` \|  \| The tester sends the query to clear the handler lot data. \|⏎\|  \| `LOTCLEARED` \| The handler replies the query to indicate that the lot data has be cleared. \|⏎\| `INPUTQTY xxxx` \|  \| The test sets a lot quantity to the handler. \|⏎\|  \| `SETTINGOK` \| The handler returns that the setting is OK. \|⏎\| The tester polling \| The tester polling \| The tester polling \|⏎\| `SRQKIND?` \|  \| The tester sends the query to obtain the request bit for the special operation for the status byte (serial polling). \|⏎\|  \| `SRQKIND 2` \| The handler replies the query with `SRQKIND 2` to indicate `bit 1` is set. The bit is the Normal Lot Start Request bit. \|⏎\| `LOTORDER 0` \|  \| The tester replies the `SRQKIND 2` with `LOTORDER 0` to tell the handler that operations are allowed to start. \|⏎\| `FULLSITES?` \|  \| The tester sends the site population query. \|⏎\|  \| `Fullsites 00000000` \| The handler replies the query with `Fullsites 00000000`. \|⏎\| The tester polling \| The tester polling \| The tester polling \|⏎\| `FULLSITES?` \|  \| The tester sends the site population query. \|⏎\|  \| `Fullsites 000007FF` \| The handler replies the query with `Fullsites 000007FF`. \|⏎\| End of the Normal Lot and Before the Start of Retest Lot (second pass). When Auto Retest Mode is Full: \| End of the Normal Lot and Before the Start of Retest Lot (second pass). When Auto Retest Mode is Full: \| End of the Normal Lot and Before the Start of Retest Lot (second pass). When Auto Retest Mode is Full: \|⏎\| `SRQKIND?` \|  \| The tester sends the query to obtain the request bit for the special operation for the status byte (serial polling). \|⏎\|  \| `SRQKIND 8` \| The handler replies the query with `SRQKIND 8` to indicate the Lot End Request. \|⏎\| `LOTRETESTCLEAR` \|  \| The tester sends the query to clear lot data on the handler. \|⏎\|  \| `LOTRETESTCLEARED` \| The handler replies the query to indicate that all data is cleared. \|⏎\| `LOTORDER 2` \|  \| The tester replies that the lot end has been detected. \|⏎\| The tester polling \| The tester polling \| The tester polling \|⏎\| `SRQKIND?` \|  \| The tester sends the query for running status. \|⏎\|  \| `SRQKIND 4` \| The handler replies the query with `SRQKIND 4` to request for the Retest Lot Start. \|⏎\| `LOTORDER 0` \|  \| The tester replies with `LOTORDER 0` to tell the handler operations are allowed to start. \|⏎\| `FULLSITES?` \|  \| The tester sends the query. \|⏎\|  \| `Fullsites 00000000` \| The handler replies the query with `Fullsites 00000000`. \|⏎\| The tester polling \| The tester polling \| The tester polling \|⏎\| `FULLSITES?` \|  \| The tester sends the query. \|⏎\|  \| `Fullsites 000003bf` \| The handler replies the query `Fullsites 000003bf`. \|⏎\| End of the Normal Lot and Before the Start of Retest Lot (second pass). When Auto-retest mode is half: \| End of the Normal Lot and Before the Start of Retest Lot (second pass). When Auto-retest mode is half: \| End of the Normal Lot and Before the Start of Retest Lot (second pass). When Auto-retest mode is half: \|⏎\| `SRQKIND?` \|  \| The tester sends the query to ask for the status. \|⏎\|  \| `SRQKIND 8` \| The handler replies the query with `SRQKIND 8` for the Lot End Request. \|⏎\| `LOTRETESTCLEAR` \|  \| The tester sends the query to clear the lot data. \|⏎\|  \| `LOTRETESTCLEARED` \| The handler replies the query to indicate the lot data has been cleared. \|⏎\| `LOTORDER 2` \|  \| The tester replies with `LOTORDER 2` that the lot end has been detected. The handler is in a stop state. \|⏎\| `SRQKIND?` \|  \| The tester sends the query to ask for the status. \|⏎\|  \| `SRQKIND 4` \| The handler replies the query to indicate the Retest Lot Start Request. \|⏎\| `LOTORDER 0` \|  \| The tester replies with `LOTORDER 0` to tell the handler that operations can start. \|⏎\| `FULLSITES?` \|  \| The tester sends the query to ask the site population. \|⏎\|  \| `Fullsites 00000000` \| The handler replies the query. \|⏎\| `FULLSITES?` \|  \| The tester sends the query. \|⏎\|  \| `Fullsites 000003bf` \| The handler replies the query. \|⏎\| End of Retest Lot: \| End of Retest Lot: \| End of Retest Lot: \|⏎\| `SRQKIND?` \|  \| The driver sends the query to ask for the status. \|⏎\|  \| `SRQKIND 10` \| The handler replies `SRQKIND 10` to indicate the Retest Lot End Request or Final Lot End Request. \|⏎\| `LOTORDER 2` \|  \| The tester replies `LOTORDER 2` to tell the handler that the Retest Lot End or Final Lot End Request has been received. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/351739.htm`。` |

| 285 | `02_System_reference/02.09_Test_Program_Management/02.09.01.03.06.01.19.03.05_GPIB_communication_flow_for_Tesec_handlers.md` | `69419.htm` | #0 | 6×3 | 17 | 1 | `\| Message from driver \| Message from handler \| Comment \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| wait for devices: [<SRQ>] \| wait for devices: [<SRQ>] \| The driver will wait for an SRQ.<br><br>Waiting for devices repeats until there is a device to test, performing status queries in between. \|⏎\| SRQ: \|  \|  \|⏎\|  \| SRQ \| Handler sends test start SRQ 0x4? where ? represents the present devices in a bit field.<br><br>0x40 is ignored by the driver. \|⏎\| Bin and reprobe devices: \|  \|  \|⏎\| S<n><bins>Q \|  \| driver sends bin information.<br><br><n> is one of 'A', 'B', .... indicating the number of bins (sites) <bins> is a list of '@', 'A', 'B', ... giving the bin results.<br><br>For example, SBACQ sends two devices to bin A (1) and B (2) respectively.<br><br>If configured at handler, a special bin can be used to trigger a reprobe operation (for example, 'E' (5)). \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/69419.htm`。` |
| 286 | `02_System_reference/02.09_Test_Program_Management/02.09.01.03.06.01.21.01.03_GPIB_communication_flow_for_Yokogawa_handlers.md` | `69446.htm` | #0 | 12×3 | 32 | 4 | `\| Message from driver \| Message from handler \| Comment \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| wait for devices:[SRQ] \| wait for devices:[SRQ] \| The driver will wait for an SRQ \|⏎\| SRQ: \|  \|  \|⏎\|  \| SRQ \| Handler returns test start SRQ. \|⏎\| site population \| site population \|  \|⏎\| FULLSITES? \|  \| Driver asks for the current population. \|⏎\|  \| FULLSITES n \| Handler returns bit coded site population. \|⏎\| bin devices: \| bin devices: \| bin devices: \|⏎\| ![bin devices](<../../../../raw/69342.png>) \|  \| Driver send bin information x is data of each site:<br><br>- A: no data<br><br>- 1 to 8: bin 1 to 8 \|⏎\|  \| ![bin devices echo](<../../../../raw/69343.png>) \| Handler returns bin information \|⏎\| ECHOOK \|  \| Driver confirms bin information is correct \|⏎\| ECHONG \|  \| ECHOed bin data was not correct.<br><br>In the case where a ECHONG was sent back to the handler the BINON command is repeated and an ECHO is waited for. If the ECHOed bin information is again incorrect a last ECHONG is sent and error message is displayed. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/69446.htm`。` |

| 338 | `02_System_reference/02.10_Data_Logging/02.10.01.06.05.61_TestNameAttrType.md` | `34701.htm` | #0 | 4×4 | 15 | 1 | `\| Field Name \| Data Type \| Description \| Comments \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| length \| int \| Attribute length \| These fields form the attribute header. \|⏎\| type \| short \| Attribute type \| These fields form the attribute header. \|⏎\| name \| string \| Test name \| The displayed test name has the format <testname>`@`<pinname>`[`<counter>`]`, where:<br><br>- <testname>: The name of the test<br>- <pinname>: The name of the pin on which this test was performed.<br><br>The pin name is included for multiport labels (which generate separate test events per pin).<br><br>It is also included if any of the JudgeAndLog_xxx member functions of the [TESTSET](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.244_TESTSET.md>) class are used (or any of the variants of [TEST()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.241_TEST.md>) that they replace) and a pin name is specified as a parameter of that function.<br><br>In other cases, the pin name is not included in the test name.<br><br>- <counter>: A counter to distinguish multiple executions of the same test (for multiport labels: the same test on the same pin). \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/34701.htm`。` |
